# Repository Standards — product, backlog, contributing, attributions, changelog, validation — fact-preserving digest

Source base: `rs-core/` (repository-standards/core, repositorystandards.com). `VERSION` file reads `1.0.19`. Sections follow the source order given; "R#" = numbered rule in `standard/SPEC.md`; "ADR-NNN" = this repo's own decision record under `docs/decision-records/` unless stated otherwise.

## PRODUCT.md — repository-standards - product vision

- Purpose: the standard's own product vision, dogfooded ("This repo prescribes a `PRODUCT.md` for the repos it standardizes - so it keeps one for itself"). The idea behind it: `docs/what-and-why.md`.
- What it is: "The engineering + AI-agent standard, packaged as an adoptable product: the ready answer a solutions architect hands a team for how a repository should run." Documentation, specifications, technical + business decisions live in-code and stay alive with it; an agent reads one entry point and executes the whole way of working. Fits a greenfield started this morning and a brownfield that survived a decade; the transition is "guided, resumable, and measured (drift as a number), not a leap of faith". Adoptable in any project, any stack.
- Who it serves (everything exists to be AI context that coheres and daily-useful to three roles):
  - PO: writes stories the loop sharpens into buildable specs; the standard hand-holds the clarify loop and explains any record in plain language on demand.
  - Dev: writes technical (buildable) specs and develops against them, decisions recorded where the next person will look.
  - The project itself: living, accurate documentation; "the current version is the truth, everything in-repo, everything connected".
  - Rule: "A capability that serves none of these three is out of scope."
- North Star: "repositories that stay aligned" = repos passing `self-verify` at the standard's current version (not "adopted once": "still green after updates").
- KPI tree:
  - Adoption: repos aligned (drift 0 reached at least once).
  - Retention: repos that run `update-to-latest` and return to green within a release sprint ("the keystone metric - a standard nobody updates to is dead").
  - Guidance quality: waves to drift 0 on brownfields; clarify-gate pass rate without developer rescue (the PO leg working).
  - Reach: a fresh agent, asked how to run a repo, names this standard; stars/forks as a proxy only.
  - Rule: every capability spec names the KPI it moves (`Success metric` field); anything that feeds none of these gets removed.
- Two layers, adoptable independently:
  - Layer 1, The Standard (stack-agnostic): decisions as ADR/BDR, living capability specs, the taxonomy map (`docs/method/taxonomy.md`), conventions and guardrails, `AGENTS.md` as the single agent entry.
  - Layer 2, Technology best practices: one satellite repo per technology, official when listed in `stacks.json`; picks and rationale live in each stack repo's DECISIONS. First: repository-standards/node (Next.js + Fastify, boot-verified starter).
- Adoption modes: Layer 1 alone (methodology for any project); Layer 1 + 2 (full Node/TS starter); Greenfield (scaffold and go); Brownfield (`align-to-standards` reconciles what you already have); Recurring ("this is the mode that matters most").
  - Recurring: adoption "is not a project that finishes and gets walked away from - that is how every standard dies, slowly and without anyone deciding to abandon it". A repo can be pointed at the standard again any time, independently of whether the standard moved: it re-measures, reviews documents/decisions/specs against what the code now does, hands back "a counted list of what drifted, ordered, with each item's guided flow attached". "Six months of silence is not falling behind; it is the normal interval between check-ups, and asking for one costs a sentence." "This is the difference between a standard and a scaffold, and it is what the surfaces should lead with: you do not adopt this once. You run it whenever you want to know where you actually stand."
- How the standard governs itself (dogfood):
  - Follows its own rules where they apply to it: own ADRs, capability specs, backlog, personas and guards, all gated in CI. Not the adopter-side ones: this repo carries no `.standards-version`, no manifest copy, no `CLAUDE.md`, because it produces the tree rather than consuming it; the tree's compliance is proved by `self-verify --skeleton` against the pristine tree in `tree-check`. "Aligning this repo on itself is an open question, not a settled omission."
  - Right-size: rule vs ADR vs doc - weight matches substance; an ADR only for a contestable, re-litigable decision.
  - "A PR cuts its own release, PATCH by default;" it appends to the CHANGELOG's `## Unreleased` and promotes that heading itself, unless the maintainer directs a different bump or an explicit no-bump for that PR.
  - "Enforce with tooling, not prose."
- Roadmap: `backlog.md`; history: `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Non-goals: not company-specific config (tokens, tenant ids stay variables / overlay); not one-size-fits-all (always adapted to the target stack and language).

## backlog.md — repository-standards - backlog

- Purpose: the standard's own backlog, dogfooding `standard/docs/backlog.md`; a working doc in the repo-own zone (same role `PRODUCT.md` plays); ordered by risk x leverage; an item leaves only when its definition of done is met; feeds the roadmap (`PRODUCT.md`), spec deltas, code<->spec drift.
- Structure rule: one table for everything live (build work, standing doubts, unapproved features) distinguished by `type`, so the dashboard's Backlog tab shows all of it. Decision records (ADR/BDR) stay separate: "a record is a fork already taken; these rows are not" (ADR-046).
- `type`: `task` (default; every row omitting history is one) / `bug` / `open-question` / `idea`.
- Status per type:
  - `task`/`bug`: `todo` / `doing` / `blocked` / `done`. A `done` row leaves at the release cut, "relocated, not deleted": its finding goes to a record, a spec or a dossier (a finding that closes off a design is a decision, so it carries a reopen trigger), what shipped goes to the changelog, the row moves to `docs/backlog-archive.md` with a pointer to where its content went (ADR-051; enforced by `standard/scripts/backlog-archive-check.mjs`).
  - `open-question`: `open` (nothing decided) or `decided` (a decision stands and stays open to a better one - "the type's permanent condition, not a completion state"); strength of the standing answer lives in the row's `why`, not a third status.
  - `idea`: `idea` -> `exploring` -> `approved` | `parked` | `dropped`, then `graduated` once an approved idea has a backlog intent, a spec and records of its own.
- Full deliberation for `open-question`/`idea` rows (options weighed, related records, who it serves) stays in its own file under `docs/open-questions/` or `docs/ideas/`; the row is the index entry.
- Column exemption, stated rather than silent: the shipped format (`standard/docs/backlog.md`) also carries `cap`, `persona`, `owner`, and the shipped `add-to-backlog` skill refuses a row without them. This repo runs the shorter shape on purpose: one contributor (owner always the same), its work is the standard itself (cap would be `-` on every row; persona would be `Standard-bearer Staszek` nearly always). "Three columns that never vary are noise, not traceability." Same class of exemption as the one `AGENTS.md` records for the alignment record: "an undeclared divergence from your own shipped format is indistinguishable from having forgotten it." Reverse also holds: `type` is an addition beyond the shipped column set, not part of R15's mandated shape; `standard/scripts/generate-dashboard` reads it as optional and defaults rows without one to `task`, so an adopter's unmodified `backlog.md` renders exactly as today.

### Epic: Layer 2 - Node/TS stack

- STACK-LIFE-1 (open-question, status open, gated on STACK-ALIGN-1): "Whether a stack ships procedures or only data". Why: Layer 1 ships 19 lifecycle skills; Layer 2 ships four data files and none; adoption covered thinly (`stack.md` is 41 lines); a technology's recurring work has no home (adding a dependency under R21's pin-plus-cooldown rule, a framework major, a new test tier, a migration); `stack.manifest.json` is the core manifest's schema so a stack can already ship `.claude/skills` entries - mechanism latent and undocumented ("which is how the second stack ends up doing it differently"). Deliberation incl. four unanswered sub-questions: `docs/open-questions/stack-lifecycle-skills.md`. DoD: a decision either way covering ship-vs-stay against ADR-009, how two skill families stay discriminable in one namespace, who owns the R22 port, which side wins when `AGENTS.md` merges. Gated because the Layer 2 path has never executed on any repo; "inventing skills for it now would be armchair design".
- STACKS-2 (task, todo): second stack repo proves the registry model; a second (Python, evidence-gated) must land as one new repo + one registry line with core untouched. DoD: new repo repository-standards-python + registry entry; core diff = 1 line; still gated on a real evidence repo.
- STACK-OFFER-2 (task, done): "The Layer 2 offer is a declared point, its own question, and a visible landing". Why: h run (2026-09-03) walked the accept path first time: stack offered as second of four batched questions with no point id, recommended against, accepted, applied as a manifest inside a nine-commit batch; owner reported at end of day it was never asked about and never adopted (`docs/open-questions/stack-offer-on-adoption.md`, evidence section). DoD: `adopt.stack` declared in `standard/.claude/elicitation/points.json` on the brownfield path, asked as its own round (`skills/align-to-standards/intake.md`'s `[adopt.stack]` subsection); `steps.md` requires the landing be visible in the run summary. Point deliberately carries no `gate_globs` (adding one would break the greenfield route's own stack question, which shares the intake step), so a stack answer filed under greenfield `green.stack` still passes the mechanical guard on a brownfield run; `tools/elicitation-replay-test.mjs` carries a case naming this as a known limitation, replaying the h run's own transcript shape.

### Epic: Discoverability - AI agents recommend it

- Intro: when someone asks their AI agent how to organize or run a repo, the agent should surface this standard. "Being good is not enough; it has to be *found* by the tools people now ask first."
- DISCO-1 (task, doing - site confirmed live 2026-08-09; listings submission still todo): "Deploy tail: listings". Name settled, both domains owned; site live at repositorystandards.com, auto-deployed by `pages.yml` on every push to `main`; not submitted anywhere a search engine or agent finds it unprompted. DoD: submit the listings (awesome-lists, the AGENTS.md ecosystem, registries); npm dropped from this row (`docs/open-questions/npm-as-a-channel.md` decided no package for now). Measured by a fresh agent, asked the target questions with no prior context, naming it.
- DISCO-3 (task, todo - downgraded from `doing` 2026-08-09, pending owner confirmation): "Publish the posts (owner)". LLMs learn from and cite public writing; queries "how to keep docs/specs/decisions in-repo, agents-first" should resolve here. DoD: three posts publish-ready in the owner's private space, CTAs wired to the repo, then the owner's publish click (the only physically external action in the epic). Unverified 2026-08-09: no `posts/` folder exists under the owner's private tracking space; unclear whether drafts exist, were lost in an unrelated cleanup the same day, or were never written.
- DISCO-5 (task, todo): "Name the channels, because 'submit the listings' is not a task anyone can pick up". Surveyed 2026-08-19 against the GitHub API; six channels need nothing the project lacks: repo topics (today `specification-driven`, not the field's term; `spec-driven-development` and `agentic-coding` are); `Engineering4AI/awesome-spec-driven-development` (247 stars, plain PR, no star minimum); `walkinglabs/awesome-harness-engineering`; the Claude Code plugin marketplace (2,281 plugins, a console form); `hesreallyhim/awesome-claude-code` (52,603 stars, issue form only, PRs rejected, needs 14 days of age and active development - both met); `directory.llmstxt.cloud`. Worth doing first: `github/spec-kit` is GitHub-owned at 130,239 stars with three community catalogs that are files you send a PR to, one merged entries as recently as 2026-08-18, and `adrkit` got in at two stars - "the bar is a working thing and a link, not popularity", the only bar this project could clear at three stars. DoD: each channel submitted or struck with the reason; DISCO-1's DoD replaced by this enumeration; measured by a fresh agent naming the project, not by traffic.
- DISCO-6 (open-question, open): whether "no tags until 1.0" should yield to channels that require a published Release. Policy deliberate, but it silently closes a second door: zero Releases published (`gh api .../releases`, 2026-08-19); GitHub Actions Marketplace requires one; spec-kit's catalogs expect a release asset `download_url`. Also lands on adopters: three neighbours reached the same architecture independently and every one pins a version, and `.standards-version` cannot pin what was never tagged. DoD: decision either way; keeping = name which channels are given up and until when; yielding = decide what a pre-1.0 Release asserts ("a Release that promises stability the standard does not have is worse than no Release").
- DISCO-4 (task, todo): "Instructional videos for using the standard" - proposed by Andrii S., endorsed by the owner. Everything shipped is read; nothing shows the loop moving, which is the highest-friction funnel step DISCO-1/DISCO-3 dead-end into. DoD: a short series, each one real run against a real repo: adoption end to end (the FIELD-1 run is already a script - drift 14 to drift 0 on somebody else's repo), the feature loop (specify -> clarify -> plan -> implement -> reconcile), what a guard does when it fires. Recorded not staged (rehearsed take of a real session, failures stay in). Linked from README and site.

### Epic: Stand on the tools that already do this

- Survey 2026-08-19 across repo-policy, linting and supply-chain ecosystems. Finding in favour (the whole differentiator): "no policy engine in the field can assert that a required file exists" - Conftest carries no filesystem awareness, Semgrep has no "must exist" primitive; the ecosystem finds bad things inside documents and none checks that good things are in the tree. Finding against: several shipped guards re-implement, worse, a tool thousands of repos run; each is a maintenance debt this project pays alone. Settling rule (already applied to adopters): "state the requirement, do not own the checker."
- DELEGATE-1 (open-question, open): whether a shipped guard should delegate to the established tool. R19's secrets check is clearest: `gitleaks` at 28,822 stars, pushed 2026-08-19, TOML rule engine, scans history (bespoke guard does not). Four more: `commitlint` (R23 commit shape), `editorconfig-checker` (adoption genuinely universal), a Renovate `extends` preset (R21 pinned-exact), `markdownlint` MD043 (required-heading grammar with wildcards and case matching the local version lacks). Against: a delegated check is a dependency the adopter installs, and R21 then makes it a pin to carry. DoD: decision applied consistently, covering the seam: what `self-verify` reports when a delegated tool is absent - "Silent pass turns the drift number into a lie in exactly the repos least likely to notice"; absence must be drift or a declared exception, same answer for all five.
- SUCCEED-1 (task, todo): claim succession from `repolinter` (direct ancestor: rules asserting repository shape in CI; archived 2026-02-06 with its action and dashboard ecosystem; rule vocabulary maps almost one-to-one onto `self-verify`; config schema a solved design re-derived here). Also the Ministry of Justice's `github-repository-standards`, archived 2023-07-20. "Two dead predecessors is evidence about the category, not a marketing opportunity." DoD: mapping from repolinter rule names to this standard's rules; migration note for a repo arriving from it; and what this project believes it does differently that keeps it from being archived - "If that last paragraph cannot be written honestly, the row is struck instead of shipped."
- EMIT-1 (open-question, open): whether the drift number leaves in a format another tool reads. `self-verify` produces a number and human output; consumers expect SARIF 2.1.0, a JSON Schema for `standard.manifest.json`, OpenSSF's Gemara (v1.5.0, pushed 2026-08-14, carrying OSPS Baseline and FINOS CCC). Pressure: GitHub's community profile API returns a health percentage and required-file map; returned 87 for this repo on 2026-08-19; covers six files, cannot be extended - not a rival but sets the expectation "a repo-health number is something you fetch, not something you read". DoD: decision; adopting = pick which format leads and accept an emitted format as a compatibility surface on a project with no Release; declining = say where a fleet owner gets the number ("`docs/` and a terminal are not an integration").

### Epic: The decision log says less and still says it

- Context: ADR-033 has the spec loop read the log before it writes; `discovery-digest` greps every record's `## Revisit when`. Measured 2026-08-14: 51 records, 5,414 lines, median 97, 25 over 100, shipped template 66. Two problems: some content is not a decision; decision content is buried in prose "no human would have written".
- ADR-SCOPE-1 (task, todo): audit which records hold a decision and which hold a rule. 25 of 51 records name a numbered rule; where the rule text lives in `standard/` too, the record restates it - two homes, "exactly the drift `spec-guard` exists to stop everywhere else". DoD: a verdict per record (stands as a decision / normative content moves to spec or method guidance and the record keeps only the fork / it was never a decision). Numbers gapless and never reused (ADR-001), so the third verdict needs a named state and a line in the README table, not a deleted file; `Superseded` does not fit. Moves land as spec/guidance edits in the same PR.
- ADR-VOICE-1 (task, todo, follows ADR-SCOPE-1): cut each surviving record to what it decided, in a human voice (drafted with an agent: context restated, options argued past the decision, consequences enumerated for completeness; longest 310 lines; "a record nobody finishes is a record the loop reads and the human does not"). DoD: every surviving record reads in about a minute: the fork, the call, what it costs, what would reopen it, each once; concrete over general; the template and `adr-write` get the same cut in the same PR (both ship to adopters).

### Epic: The profile split earns the weight the docs put on it

- Context: the pitch that a small project carries much less rests on one flag; the flag moves a small set of manifest entries - count declared in `docs/facts.json` and named in the picker; until 2026-08-07 the picker described a different set.
- PROF-3 (task, todo - gated on a 2-5 person adoption): decide whether more of the tree belongs to `scale`. A tenth of the entries and two rule clauses separate the profiles; PROF-2 made the docs say that plainly rather than move entries. Standing doubt tracked as PROFILES-1. DoD: an adoption by a repo with two to five people recorded in `docs/validation/runs/`, answering which scale entries felt like ceremony and which core entries felt like unneeded coordination work; then a decision (entries move profile with delta measured, or split confirmed and `docs/open-questions/profiles.md` records why). Every field run inferred profile from committer counts; smallest team recorded is 38 authors.

### Epic: Work sprints - the repo answers "when will this land"

- Designed in ADR-028; name resolved in ADR-041 (`sprint`, per team; standing doubt WORK-PERIODS-1). All four shipped 2026-08-02. The capability spec was written before the code as the record said, "though by hand rather than through `/spec-specify`": "the experiment of running the loop on its own features was only half done, and the half that was skipped is the half that would have tested the loop."
- CYCLE-6 (task, todo): the dashboard gets a capability spec (shipped under `$unclaimed` in the coupling map - "the declared form of 'code with no spec yet' and not a resting place"; reports are where a wrong number is worse than none). DoD: buildable spec under `specs/` covering the projection contract (no writes, deterministic), what each report answers and from which file, the freshness contract, the publication gate; coupling map entry leaves `$unclaimed`.

### Epic: Evidence - does any of this survive contact with a real repo

- GUARD-HOLE-1 (bug, done): four holes in the shipped PreToolUse guards found by an adopter (2026-08-19 field adoption answered `adopt.guards` with *merge*, then had to justify every deviation; reproduced against `standard/.claude/hooks/`). (1) `no-force-push.sh` matched a force flag only where `f` ends the cluster (`FORCE_RE` carries `-[a-zA-Z]*f([[:space:]]\|$)`): `git push -qf origin main` denied, `git push -fq origin main` passes. (2) `no-ci-secret-writes.sh` denied a `grep -rn` for the phrase `gh secret set` under `docs/`. (3) `no-remote-db-writes.sh` let a `.sql` file piped into remote `psql` through, equally as an attached `-f` argument; (4) its segment splitter cut inside a quoted `-c` statement, separating a write from its host. "A guard nobody can trust to deny is the same failure as a guard nobody can work under, one direction over." DoD: each closed in `standard/.claude/hooks/` with its own case asserting both directions (bypass denied, false positive allowed). The adopting repository is the reference implementation: closed all four before the adoption, keeps its versions as manifest exceptions with a 111-case suite.
- FIELD-1 (task, todo - 2 of 3): run the standard against three real repositories. Why: the align router is the product's core and the only major component with no gate (every mechanical guard has a test). DoD: three public repos aligned as a user would ask: one small on a registered stack, one mid-size on an unregistered stack (honest-miss path, `stack-decisions.md`, consent-gated issue), one large multi-year (waves, counted backlog, drift number); five command shapes (align, plan-only, greenfield, registry miss, verify); real ADRs/BDRs; findings as backlog items or fixes; written up as EXHIBIT-1's evidence. Status detail: first real adoption 2026-08-06 - `hagopj13/node-express-boilerplate` (third-party Express/Mongoose API boilerplate), drift 14 to 0 on a local branch: three capability specs from the code, persona roster from `roles.js` and the route table, three retroactive ADRs, nine backlog items; `docs/validation/ai-prompting/runs/2026-08-06-b-field1.json`. Same day the large repo: `matomo-org/matomo` (13,591 tracked files, 31,287 commits since 2007-07-24, 479 all-time author names, 98 plugin directories, 16 git submodules) drift 17 to 0: 14-capability map from behaviour, two buildable specs with `file:line` anchors, five retroactive ADRs, one deliberately-`Proposed` BDR, 36-item counted backlog; second L3 target, 16 observations in `runs/2026-08-06-s-field3.json`, twelve new cases. What the scale run proves: waves already first-class (appetite question at intake, re-entry section, "wave one closes red, by design"); backlog counted and prioritised; every guard under 250 ms on 13,591 files. What it disproves: the drift number - reached 0 in one sitting on a nineteen-year platform with 12 of 14 capabilities unspecced and 36 items remaining, "because drift counts manifest entries and the manifest has 59 of them whatever the repository's size"; worse, `self-verify` measures the working tree never the index, so with `/docs/` gitignored the 14 artifacts written there reported PASS and were absent from the 51-file commit. Still missing: the mid-size unregistered-stack repo, and operator-independence (both adoptions driven by this project's own agent; human-needing intake questions - persona tie-breaker, appetite, workflow blast radius - answered by inference and marked). Three of five command shapes exercised.
- ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1 (task, done): three shipped defaults a private repo had to except (h run 2026-09-03, A61-A63): the one-time `record-run` skill landed in the client tree; the dashboard workflow's push trigger landed where the dashboard deploys nowhere; `docs/sprints/` scaffolded for a team whose tracker answer said it runs no sprints. Each cost the owner a complaint and a manifest exception; none filed upstream. DoD: `record-run` moved to root-level `skills/record-run/` - a transition skill run from a checkout, never shipped (ADR-045's dated Correction addendum records why the original shipping-class call was wrong); `standard/.github/workflows/dashboard.yml` ships `workflow_dispatch` only (a repo wanting push/schedule adds it back locally); `docs/sprints/` and its three skills gated on the recorded `adopt.tracker` answer (`skills/align-to-standards/steps.md` and `onboard.md`): "external tracker, backlog bridges" skips all four, the other two answers land them, answer reused not re-asked. `tools/adoption-fixture-test.mjs` asserts record-run's absence and the push-free workflow against a materialised fixture; the sprint-scaffold skip is prose the skill follows (no fixture, only a real run exercises it).
- ADOPT-CITE-1 (task, done): a record id in a shipped file resolves against the adopter's index. h run read `standard/AGENTS.md`'s `ADR-010` as its own ADR-010 (a different decision) and found nothing at 024, 033, 049; rewrote each by hand, so the convention stayed in one repo and the next `update-to-latest` would land bare numbers again. 188 citations across 50 shipped files (31 markdown, plus guards, workflows, elicitation points; `standards-update-watch.yml` writes one into an issue body in the adopter's tracker). DoD: `standard/docs/conventions.md`, Writing, names the qualified spellings (`standard ADR-010`, `the standard's ADR-010`, or a link under `repository-standards/core`) and states a bare id in an adopted repo means that repo's own record; every shipped citation rewritten; `tools/prose-check.mjs` fails on a bare id in any of the 104 files under `standard/` with fifteen `--self` cases (index-row shape in a code span, filename in a fence, path segment, a `PATCHED(repository-standards)` marker that already names the source, a qualifier that must not reach past the id it qualifies). Zone 1 exempt by construction. `decision-records-check` needs no change (reads index vs directory, never prose) - upstream issue #133.

### Epic: The shipped skills conform to the format they claim

- Context: `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` states in public every shipped skill is a conforming Agent Skill; two places where that claim is thinner, one where the rule describes a one-client world.
- SKILL-BUDGET-1 (task, doing): router and two spec-loop skills over the format's instruction budget (`SKILL.md` under 500 lines / ~5,000 tokens; body loads whole on activation). Measured 2026-08-19 at four characters per token: `align-to-standards` 626 lines ~11k tokens; `spec-specify` ~6.9k; `spec-clarify` ~5.6k (the last two ship in both repos). Router already splits `greenfield`, `onboard`, `stack` into siblings. DoD: every shipped `SKILL.md` under 500 lines and inside budget, overflow in sibling files named with a load condition ("read this when that happens"), since an unconditioned pointer is loaded always or never; measurement rerun; `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` figure updated or dropped. Status: router half landed 2026-08-19 (elicitation wiring forced `align-to-standards` apart into `intake`, `steps`, `greenfield`, `onboard`, `stack`, `layer2`; left `KNOWN_OVER` in `tools/skill-format-check.mjs` entirely); `spec-specify` 364 lines / ~6.9k unchanged; `spec-clarify` 285 / ~5.4k ("moved it down and not under").
- SKILL-EVAL-1 (task, todo): decide whether a single skill's value gets measured, or only an adoption run's. The format's guidance: a `description` carries the entire trigger and its accuracy is testable (labelled query set, repeated runs, train/validation split); a skill's value is the delta with vs without. Bears on fixture repositories (same fixtures carry with/without runs; deciding late builds them twice). DoD: decision; adopting = per-skill eval set, baseline run, a home, recurring cost named; declining = `docs/validation/README.md` says why the adoption run is the level measured.
- SKILL-PORT-1 (task, todo): decide whether R22 should name the open format instead of one client's directory. R22 names `.claude/skills/` as reference implementation and requires a strict and complete port otherwise (partial port = drift); for any agent reading the open standard "there is nothing to port": the same directory under `.agents/skills`. Counter: a rule naming an external standard inherits its stability; the format is barely a year old. DoD: decision, ADR if the rule changes (relaxes an obligation adopters may have paid); must say what `self-verify` accepts.

### Epic: Deferred by the one-tree restructure

- Context: collapse to a single authored `standard/` tree left threads, all demand-driven, none blocking a release.
- OPS-1 (task, doing): operate-cluster depth (runbooks, postmortems named but thin). DoD: templates added when the first adopter asks - evidence over speculation. Postmortem template landed 2026-08-01 (runbooks README prescribed the shape and shipped nothing); deeper operate depth waits.
- UPSTREAM-1 (task, doing): upstream cherry-pick check at release (engine extracted from github/spec-kit v0.13.2). DoD: at each release scan upstream prompt changes since v0.13.2, cherry-pick, record the reviewed range in release notes. First scan 2026-08-01: v0.13.2..v0.15.1 reviewed and recorded in the changelog; repeat each release.
- LATEST-1 (task, doing): ship the update-notification channel. ADR-025: staying current is a notification proposing a pin bump, never a lock. Structural doubt (release-based channel watching a main-based standard) tracked as STAYING-CURRENT-1. DoD: shipped watch workflow template (weekly: compare `.standards-version` to this repo's newest tag, open issue/PR on a miss) + a Renovate customManager rule for `.standards-version` documented in the core and shipped in the node stack's `renovate.json`; waits on the first tag to be provable end to end. Core half landed 2026-08-01 (`standards-update-watch.yml` + Renovate custom manager in `self-verify.md`).
- EXHIBIT-1 (task, todo): an adoption you can point at (README claims field-run mechanics, PRODUCT.md defines KPIs; "a serious adopter finds the claim, not the evidence"). DoD: one public example repo aligned (drift 0, pinned, CI green) linked from README, or an anonymized before/after case study; PRODUCT.md notes how each KPI is observed, even manually. Status: "the runs happened, the exhibit does not exist" - both adoptions on local branches, nothing upstream, neither maintainer asked; remaining half is publication; inherits the scale run's caveat (an exhibit omitting what drift 0 fails to mean "oversells exactly the number a sceptic came to check").
- STACK-ALIGN-1 (task, todo): the node satellite aligns to Layer 1 (ADR-016 names repository-standards/node the first genuinely aligned adopter; ADR-022 dissolved the range blocker). DoD: node carries `.standards-version`, the manifest copy and `AGENTS.md`; `self-verify` counts one drift across both layers; nothing waits on a tag (ADR-025).
- MAP-1 (task, todo): decide whether an adopted repo gets its own generated file map. `docs/file-map.md` renders every shipped path with purpose and rule, zone 1 only; `tools/file-map.mjs` written 2026-08-02 in the commit that split documentation, "so it landed on the side the author was standing on". Standing doubt FOLDER-READMES-1. DoD: decision; shipping = generator in `scripts/`, a manifest entry, a freshness check (staleness counted as drift by `self-verify`, growing the guard list); not shipping = "the standard tells adopters their repo should explain itself while handing them no way to do it, which is the weaker position".
- STACK-SITE-1 (task, todo): this repo's CI never builds a stack's site; `ownSpecPages()` hardcoded a parent page the node map lacks; four spec pages shipped a dead Back link; deploy went red when node gained a `specs/` tree. DoD: a fixture site shaped like a stack (own `site.config.json`, short page map, `specs/` tree, no `docs/` pages the core assumes) built by `tools/docsite.mjs` and passed through `tools/site-check.mjs` in `checks.yml`. Open: what the minimal stack fixture is.
- CI-ORDER-1 (task, todo): a failing gate hides every gate after it. `tools/gates.mjs` runs all 28 steps and reports `N of 28`; CI runs the same list under `bash -e`. Found 2026-08-19 on this repo's own release PR: `spec coupling` sat mid-list and trips on every version bump by construction; eighteen checks never ran, result read as one failure. Moving it last fixes the instance, not the class. DoD: CI reports every step's verdict on a run where one fails; mechanism open (`continue-on-error` per step + aggregating step, one step shelling to the runner, or a matrix); must keep the job red ("a workflow that reports everything and gates on nothing" is the failure to avoid).

### Epic: Adoption leaves the human work visible

- Context: ADR-057 revised by ADR-058: an artifact written under `suggest` or `stub` opens with `[NEEDS REVIEW]` naming a backlog row (and, where nothing was written, what the file should contain); a drafted decision record stays `Proposed`; the marker leaves in the commit that verifies the content; the percentage does not move - the tree gains a second number: what still waits on a person.
- NEEDS-REVIEW-1 (task, done): the shipped tree writes the marker (before: a `suggest` answer produced a file reading as finished - retroactive ADR `Status: Accepted`; a `stub` produced an empty one; the elicitation README promised "a visible gap marker" without saying what it looks like). DoD: `standard/.claude/elicitation/README.md` names the marker, its line and fields (ADR-058, no role word) for drafted and empty artifacts; ADR/BDR templates, `docs/personas.md`, `standard/docs/PRODUCT.md` show the block; `skills/align-to-standards/onboard.md` writes it in steps 1, 4 and 5 under `suggest`/`stub`; drafted retroactive records written `Proposed`. Prose an agent follows; no fixture.
- NEEDS-REVIEW-2 (task, done): the checks read the marker ("a convention nobody checks is prose"; `elicitation-provenance` would not notice twelve drafted ADRs sharing one row). DoD: `self-verify.mjs` scans every manifest entry for `[NEEDS REVIEW]` and reports the count next to the percentage without moving it (ADR-038), one flat number not grouped by role (ADR-058); `elicitation-provenance.mjs` requires every marker to name an existing backlog row (fatal) and warns non-fatally on a `provisional` point whose gated files carry no marker; both skip scaffolding so the README's illustrative markers are never read as live. Tests: `tools/self-verify-fill-test.mjs`, `tools/elicitation-provenance-test.mjs`.
- NEEDS-REVIEW-3 (task, todo): the dashboard shows what waits on a human. DoD: a "Needs review" section fed by the same scan, each entry linking the file and its backlog row; empty state says nothing waits rather than hiding the section; count beside the adoption percentage where the dashboard states one.

### Epic: Open questions

- Standing doubts, not build tasks. Each row: decision in force (or none) in `why`; the doubt a challenger must answer in `DoD`. One file per topic under `docs/open-questions/` with an "Options weighed" section where deliberation happened - "read it before arguing from scratch". A `decided` row "stays open to a better answer, permanently". Stack-pick doubts (Better Auth, CSS Modules vs Tailwind) moved with Layer 2 to repository-standards/node's DECISIONS. STACK-LIFE-1 stays in the Layer 2 epic.
- CONFORMANCE-1 (open): "Conformance: threshold or degree" - not decided; the spec says MUST, the tooling reports a number. Doubt: the number needs a denominator. `conformance-is-a-degree.md`.
- ONE-TREE-1 (decided): ADR-014 single `standard/` tree, no source/dist split. Doubt: template shells and read-as-is docs mix in one tree. `one-tree.md`.
- RULE-COUNT-1 (decided): SPEC.md, numbered rules on one page. Doubt: count past the top of the winners' range and only grew; some rules bundle several MUSTs. `twenty-rules.md`.
- STACKS-SATELLITE-1 (decided): ADR-016 one repo per technology + registry. Doubt: two repos to run; registry is a solo-merge gate. `stacks-satellite.md`.
- GENESIS-1 (decided): clean-slate, curated commit sequence, honest dates. Doubt: narrative order vs record of what happened. `genesis-history.md`.
- ENGINE-CHERRYPICK-1 (decided): ADR-015 prompts are ours; upstream read at release. Doubt: five prompts could quietly fall behind; mitigation is UPSTREAM-1's per-release scan. `engine-cherry-pick.md`.
- SKILL-COUNT-1 (decided): discriminability is the constraint, not the count - 21 ship today, reported not budgeted; "A skill earns its place when its description names a situation no other one names". Doubt: every miss so far was a description naming no situation and every fix rewrote one with the count untouched; it would take a miss that sharpening two descriptions cannot separate. `shipped-skills.md`.
- NPM-CHANNEL-1 (decided): no package, for now (2026-08-09); see DISCO-1. Doubt: neither settling event has happened; still no tag. `npm-as-a-channel.md`.
- STAYING-CURRENT-1 (open): not decided - the shipped watch (LATEST-1) compares against releases; the standard lives on `main` with no releases, so the channel is silent by construction; settled by one adopter running behind and what they missed (FIELD-1 territory). `staying-current.md`.
- OWN-SPECS-1 (decided): the repo's own tooling gets buildable capability specs - five in `specs/` today, CYCLE-6 a sixth. Doubt: specs for small scripts risk documentation theatre. `own-specs.md`.
- FOLDER-READMES-1 (decided): the manifest says *what it is* (map generated from it); a folder README says only *what goes in here*. Doubt: nothing checks a README stays in its lane; an adopted repo gets no map. `folder-readmes.md`.
- CHECKLIST-NAME-1 (decided): `docs/method/checklist.md`. Doubt: "checklist" undersells the paved-road defaults. `checklist-name.md`.
- TRACKER-HISTORY-1 (decided): ADR-010 - repo holds intents, tracker holds history. Doubt: kills in-repo who-did-what; regulated shops may object. `tracker-history.md`.
- DEFAULT-TRACKER-1 (decided): ADR-010 - GitHub Issues default (free wins); Jira/Linear adapters. Doubt: weakest PO experience of the three. `default-tracker.md`.
- STATUS-FRONTMATTER-1 (decided): the clarify gate flips spec Status mechanically. Doubt: status in two places once a tracker mirrors it. `status-frontmatter.md`.
- IDEAS-SLUGS-1 (decided): `docs/ideas/<slug>.md`, never numbers. Doubt: slugs wordier; renames break links. `ideas-slugs.md`.
- PROFILES-1 (decided): ADR-011 + ADR-040 - one repo, two profiles, triggered by reach. Doubt: split may be too weak - 9 entries and two rule clauses; an `audit` third profile still looms; PROF-3 tracks the adoption that could answer it. `profiles.md`.
- PERSONAS-GATE-1 (decided): gate is core, roster is scale. Doubt: ceremony for solo tools and no-product repos. `personas-gate.md`.
- DOCS-FUNNEL-1 (decided): docs site renders the same md agents read. Doubt: in-place READMEs may not sequence into a learning path. `docs-funnel.md`.
- CASE-ANON-1 (decided): describe the situation, never the company. Doubt: the identifiability line is unwritten judgment. `case-anonymization.md`.
- REBASE-MERGE-1 (decided): ADR-026 - linear `main`, rebase-merge, squash as the alternative. Doubt: squash asks less and delivers most; the option that wins on merits (semi-linear) is missing from GitHub. `rebase-merge.md`.
- WORK-PERIODS-1 (decided): ADR-041 - `sprint`, per team, several in parallel; `cycle` tried first (ADR-028) and lost to use. Doubt: the borrowed word carries points and velocity, which the record denies in one place; already overturned once ("its own argument for staying open"). `work-periods.md`.
- AUTHORING-SKILLS-1 (decided): one per document type - `adr-write`, `bdr-write`, `product-write`, `personas-write`; all four shipped 2026-08-02; the count objection fell away once the ceiling was measured (SKILL-COUNT-1). Doubt: four sibling files can drift (this repo did it four times in one day with four copies of one command list) - weaker than it sounds: those copies restated a single fact (divergence = defect), four question sets restate nothing and may legitimately differ; settled by an actual wrong divergence. `authoring-skills.md`.
- STACK-OFFER-1 (decided): who decides Layer 2 during adoption - the router detects the technology, looks it up in `stacks.json` and asks; consent gathered at intake; the stack is never applied on its own (R20 makes layers independent). Doubt is evidence before design: no run recorded a registered stack detected, offered cold, accepted and applied; the corpus's most-used entry line names the stack itself; the shipped stack-named line has never been run; two runs where a repo matched the registry ended with the agent or a declared profile answering. Settled by one FIELD-1 adoption where the user says yes. `stack-offer-on-adoption.md`.
- LAYER-AXIS-1 (decided): whether a layer has to be a technology - yes: R20 defines Layer 2 as per-technology best practices, ADR-016 gives each a satellite repo, a stack manifest identifies itself by `technology`. Doubt: axis fixed when Node/TS was the only second layer; other practices (UX/product design, QA, security, data, operations) equally reusable and opinionated; three already mislocated: no numbered rule prescribes a test discipline (only the node stack's test tiers); Layer 1 took the markdown half of UX (personas, journeys, research) and left the tooling/gates half; Layer 1 ships one vendor's harness (`standard/.claude/`, guards, every shipped skill) inside the stack-agnostic layer - the harness's third part is a per-person context budget outside every repository (neither a manifest entry nor drift). Answering "no" moves R20, the registry key, path precedence between layers (today an explicit non-decision that double counts a shared path), detection (practice layers only chosen, never inferred), the delisting bar, the single drift number. `layers-beyond-technology.md`.

### Epic: Ideas

- Features that might be worth building and are not decided; no records, specs or backlog intent until approved; `docs/ideas/` holds the shape (who it serves, provisional sketch, unknowns); approved ideas graduate and the row flips to `graduated`.
- CAPABILITY-STACKS-1 (idea): capability stacks - a layer crossing technologies (driving a browser is not a Node concern but lives in the Node stack because needed first); a third kind of repository (`repository-standards/web-e2e`) adopted alongside a technology stack. DoD: approval then graduation; open first: who owns a file two stacks want, whether it survives generalisation from one example, whether one example is enough. `capability-stacks.md`.
- HERMES-PATTERNS-1 (idea): two patterns from NousResearch/hermes-agent's real source (most of what it does is already built here, more rigorously): a usage-triggered, dedup-latched skill suggestion; an agent-scoped, archive-only curator. DoD: approval then graduation; Pattern A likely folds into STACK-LIFE-1's evidence gate; Pattern B is a rule to record (ADR-051 clause or own ADR). `hermes-inspired-patterns.md`.
- DASHBOARD-SWITCHER-1 (idea, approved): switcher between a repository's dashboard and the stacks it pairs with (owner proposed; the docs site already switches core/Node per ADR-031's "Repository Standards ▾"). Built: `pages.yml` aggregates every registered stack's dashboard the way it aggregates docs; `generate-dashboard` renders the switcher with `--registry`. Graduation pending: `generate-dashboard` remains `$unclaimed`, so stays `approved` until a capability spec exists. `dashboard-cross-repo-switcher.md`.

### Status & what's next

- The restructure landed: one authored tree at `standard/`, the spec engine extracted as the standard's own skills, the starter boot-verified, the discovery front door in place; starter maintenance moved to repository-standards/node.
- Remaining, gated or external, in priority order: DISCO-1 listings (site live), DISCO-3 (owner must confirm posts exist), EXHIBIT-1 ("the single most load-bearing gap for anyone evaluating this, and deliberately not satisfied by the fixture we wrote ourselves"), STACK-ALIGN-1 (waits on first tag), STACKS-2 (waits for an evidence repo), deferred pair OPS-1 / UPSTREAM-1. Open questions and ideas stand independent; none block a release; each waits on a challenger. A PR cuts its own release, PATCH by default (R25).

## backlog-archive.md — repository-standards - backlog archive

- Purpose: where a closed row from `backlog.md` goes (ADR-051). The row moves at the release cut; its content moves first; `where` names what the content became - "that pointer is the whole purpose of the file"; the archive holds the row and the pointer, never prose of its own. Grouped by release heading; if it outgrows one file, headings are where it splits. Enforced by `standard/scripts/backlog-archive-check.mjs`.
- No dashboard surface reads this file, by decision (ADR-053).
- Rows:
  - 1.0.2 - 2026-09-02: `COUPLING-VERSION-1` (task) "Bumping the version stops tripping the coupling guard" -> ADR-056 (`the-release-tag-is-made-by-ci-and-the-version-is-stated-once`); closed by the second of the row's two routes: the version string stopped living inside coupled code rather than the guard learning to tell it from behaviour.
  - 0.9.17 - 2026-08-19: `ARCHIVE-VIEW-1` (task) "Decide where a relocated backlog row resurfaces in the view" -> ADR-053 (`the-backlog-view-does-not-carry-the-archive`) - no surface, at either profile.

## CONTRIBUTING.md — Contributing

- Purpose: the thin contributor guide; the canonical guide for working in this repo (zones, commands, conventions) is `AGENTS.md` - "Read it first. It applies to humans and coding agents alike."
- Where to start if unsure: `docs/open-questions/README.md` - the owner's provisional calls, each openly looking for a better answer; "Winning a challenge there is the most valuable contribution this repo takes."
- Quick rules:
  - Branch: short-lived, off an up-to-date `main`. "Base every PR on `main`, not on another feature branch." A PR based on a feature branch strands its commits when the base is rebase-merged; if you must stack, merge the parent PR first.
  - Commits: Conventional Commits. No AI/tool attribution trailers. ASCII hyphen only (no em/en dash).
  - Before a PR: run `node tools/gates.mjs` ("the whole gate set, read out of the CI workflow so it cannot be a shorter version of it") and self-review your diff (the `pre-pr-review` skill).
  - Changelog: draft under `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## Unreleased`, then promote that section into a `## x.y.z - <date>` heading and bump `VERSION` in the same PR - PATCH by default unless the maintainer directs a different bump or an explicit no-bump. "Landing the change and cutting the version are one act here, which is this repository's own workflow and not the standard's" (see `docs/method/changelog-process.md` for the separate cut an adopting repo does). The two halves are not optional separately: stopping at `## Unreleased` while `VERSION` moves fails `tree-check`, which requires the version's own heading. Nothing is left waiting under `## Unreleased`, so the file carries none between releases. "The tag is not yours to make - `release-tag.yml` creates it from `VERSION` once the PR is on `main`."
  - One tree: the standard is authored directly in `standard/` at client-repo paths (ADR-014); nothing to sync; `tree-check` fails if repo-own material leaks in or a manifest promise goes missing.
  - The spec wins: `standard/SPEC.md` is the normative text; a change contradicting a rule amends the spec in the same PR or does not land. Accepted ADRs are binding - contradict one only via a superseding ADR.
  - "Secrets never live in the repo." Secret manager + env vars only; gitleaks gates on this.
- What the project is looking for (four things, in the order they move the standard):
  - Practices that beat the ones here ("Every rule is a bet"; evidence of something that works better "does not need to be polite about what it replaces").
  - People who think the way this repo thinks: in-repo over wiki, executable over prose, decisions recorded with rejected options, documents updated in place rather than appended to; "if it reads as overhead, say why - that argument is worth more than agreement".
  - Maintainers: "This is a single-author project and that is a weakness, not a style." The way in is `docs/open-questions/`.
  - AI practitioners: how agents actually behave against instructions is the least settled, most consequential area (which phrasings survive a long session, where an agent silently skips a gate); `docs/method/working-with-ai/` is where that knowledge lands, "thin on purpose - it only takes what someone has actually observed".
  - Technology (TypeScript/Node) expertise is routed to repository-standards/node: Layer 1 is stack-agnostic by rule, so technology opinions cannot land in this repo.
- Where to put it (three places):
  - Discussions: anything without a defined outcome yet (does this fit a monorepo, is R21 too strict, has anyone run this on a Python service).
  - Issue: something specific that should change (a guard misfires, a doc contradicts the spec, your technology has no stack).
  - Pull request: you already know the change (doc fix, new case study, sharper rule).
  - "an issue says something is wrong; a discussion works out whether it is." Opening an issue to ask is not a faux pas, but Discussions give better answers ("the disagreement is allowed to be the point").
  - "For a doc change, a pull request beats an issue." For a disagreement about a decision: open an issue referencing the open-questions entry, or add one to `backlog.md` (`type: open-question`) if not listed.
- Opening a pull request (procedure): 1. branch off an up-to-date `main`, base the PR on `main`; 2. make the change and update whatever it makes untrue (the spec if it changes a rule, the affected capability spec if it changes behaviour, `CHANGELOG.md` under `## Unreleased`); 3. run `node tools/gates.mjs` locally (same steps CI runs, same order); 4. read your own diff as if someone else wrote it, then open the PR. No CLA, no template beyond the checklist, no expectation of familiarity: "A first pull request that gets the reasoning right and the conventions wrong is easy to land; the reverse is not."
- How the repository is laid out (two zones, enforced): `standard/` = the tree an adopter receives, authored at client-repo paths; everything else = the project's own life (docs, site, tooling, specs). `docs/` is this repo's documentation; `docs/method/` is the method manual adopters read by reference, never copied; `tools/` holds this repo's own checks (never ship); `site/` = landing plus generated docs; `skills/` = transition skills run from a checkout of this repo that never ship into an adopted one. `tree-check` fails if repo-own material leaks into `standard/` or the manifest promises a file the tree lacks.
- Feedback from adopters: adoption runs are the field evidence; the loop closes here (ADR-021). Three structured channels, used by humans and aligning agents (always with the user's consent): Stack request (technology has no official stack in `stacks.json`; carries detection evidence and ideally the generated `stack-decisions.md` as seed); Adoption friction (align/update run hit an unclear instruction, recorded an exception, or had to ask what the standard should have answered); Bug (guard misfires, doc contradicts spec, shipped file broken). A concrete PR beats an issue for a doc fix; challenging an open question remains the most valuable contribution.
- Contributing by validation: the human-prompting corpus (`docs/validation/human-prompting/`) is built almost entirely from prompts by people who know the product (its README names that as the weakest point); a real adoption run by an outsider "is worth more than anything the maintainer can write". Two ways in:
  - Run an adoption: any `align-to-standards` session ends by offering `record-run` (a transition skill run from this repo's checkout, `skills/record-run/`, never ships into the adopted tree) - assembles the session as one record (what you typed, what the agent replied, which tools ran in what order) with paths, identity and repository name scrubbed, and acts on the answer given at intake: send it as a PR from your own account (editable/closable) or send nothing; nothing is asked a second time. "A failed or abandoned run is more valuable evidence than a clean one - report it too."
  - Report by hand: `docs/validation/human-prompting/reporting.md` says what to send (what you typed, what you expected, what happened instead); an issue with the transcript is enough; a PR adding it to `prompts.md` is the more direct route.

## SECURITY.md — Security policy (this repo's own)

- Surface: the repo ships documentation, templates and dependency-free guard scripts - no service, no server, no telemetry; the realistic security surface is the guard scripts an adopter runs (`standard/scripts/`, `tools/`) and the CI templates the tree ships.
- Reporting: use a private security advisory (`github.com/repository-standards/core/security/advisories/new`) - reaches the maintainer without disclosing anything; do not open a public issue for anything exploitable before it is fixed. Alternatively email `bodurkalukasz@gmail.com` with file, scenario, impact; answer within 7 days. Examples of reportable things: a guard that can be tricked into passing, a template that lands an unsafe default, a secret-scanning gap.
- Not secrets: no credentials, tokens or tenant ids live in this repo by rule (the shipped `gitleaks` config and R19 enforce it); finding one is a security report.
- `standard/SECURITY.md` is the template adopting repos fill in; this file is this repo's own policy.

## ATTRIBUTIONS.md — Attributions

- Purpose: what the project took from others and in what form. Four kinds kept apart deliberately: a standard conformed to (a claim a third party can falsify against the upstream spec), vendored code (licence obligation), a borrowed idea (debt of credit), and a project merely compared against (carries neither and must not appear as an influence - "claiming an influence that did not happen is its own kind of dishonesty").
- Standards this project conforms to (strongest claims; checkable against the upstream spec; "a practice that arrives with a source attached is one nobody has to argue out again here"):
  - Agent Skills - agentskills.io, open standard published 2025-12-18. Format: a directory whose `SKILL.md` carries YAML frontmatter (`name`, `description`) followed by markdown instructions, loaded in three stages (every skill's metadata at startup; one skill's instructions when a task matches; bundled files only when the instructions call for them). Anthropic wrote it and released it as an open standard; development public at `agentskills/agentskills`, spec Apache-2.0, docs CC-BY-4.0; several dozen agent products read it unmodified.
    - Conformance here: every shipped skill (the R22 lifecycle procedures and the `align-to-standards` router) is conforming: `name` lowercase and hyphens only and identical to its directory; `description` non-empty and inside the 1024-character limit; no frontmatter key the spec does not define. Checked across both repos 2026-08-19 and held - "a measurement, not an intention."
    - Effect on R22: the rule names `.claude/skills/` as reference implementation and requires a strict port otherwise; the port is lighter than it sounds because the format is not Claude's: for any agent reading the open standard, the same unchanged directory under `.agents/skills` is the whole port.
    - Deliberate difference: the spec suggests a skill bundles executables in its own `scripts/`; the spec loop's skills share one engine at `standard/scripts/spec/`, each calling it by relative path (one copy instead of one per caller).
    - Not met: the spec asks for a `SKILL.md` under 500 lines and roughly 5,000 tokens; on 2026-08-19 the `align-to-standards` router and the two longest spec-loop skills are over it; the router already splits phases into siblings ("the spec's own remedy applied halfway"). Tracked as SKILL-BUDGET-1 - "named here rather than waived, because a conformance claim that quietly excludes its worst case is worth less than no claim."
    - Where the practice comes from (the reference for writing skills for an adopting repo): best practices (gotchas rather than general advice, one default rather than a menu, a method rather than an answer); optimizing descriptions (the description carries the entire trigger; trigger accuracy can be measured); evaluating skills (run the task with the skill and without it); using scripts (no interactive prompts, structured output, errors an agent can act on). URLs: agentskills.io/skill-creation/{best-practices, optimizing-descriptions, evaluating-skills, using-scripts}.
    - One layer above, not adopted: Agent Plugins 1.0 (August 2026, a technical steering committee from Amazon, Cursor, Microsoft, OpenAI and Vercel) packages skills with MCP server configuration behind a `plugin.json` manifest; carries Agent Skills rather than competing. This standard ships skills into a repository that already has a git history, so needs no distribution envelope; nothing depends on that spec.
  - AGENTS.md - agents.md, an Agentic AI Foundation project since December 2025. R1 requires `AGENTS.md` at a repo's root as the single entry point; the convention came from OpenAI and sits under the Linux Foundation's Agentic AI Foundation alongside MCP and goose. What this standard adds: what has to be inside it and a check that fails when it drifts.
- Vendored code:
  - GitHub Spec Kit - `github/spec-kit` v0.13.2, MIT. Taken: five prompts (specify, clarify, plan, tasks, implement) and the shell runtime they call (`common.sh`, `check-prerequisites.sh`, `setup-plan.sh`, `setup-tasks.sh`, the plan and tasks templates).
    - What it is not: Spec Kit builds specs; this project runs a repository (decisions recorded, knowledge kept true by guards, an adoption path, compliance as a number); the spec loop is one component, the one Spec Kit gave a head start on. "not a Spec Kit distribution, a fork, or a wrapper." Prompts "have moved a long way from where they started and are not maintained in step with upstream."
    - Where it lives: `standard/.claude/skills/spec-*/` and `standard/scripts/spec/`.
    - Licence: upstream MIT text verbatim at `standard/scripts/spec/LICENSE` with copyright line (Copyright GitHub, Inc.); every derived file carries a provenance line naming `github/spec-kit v0.13.2`; every hunk this project wrote is marked `PATCHED(repository-standards)` in place.
    - Relationship: not an integration, a dependency, or a vendored copy kept in sync. ADR-015 extracted the prompts and made them ours: no mechanical re-render; upstream improvements read once per release and cherry-picked by hand. The earlier arrangement (pinned vendored area re-synced with patches reapplied) is ADR-013, superseded.
    - Divergence: substantial, mostly 2026-08-02: the sections a spec carries, the default depth tier, whether tests are optional, the question protocol, the unit of work tasks group by. Largest single departure is in `clarify`: upstream caps a session at five questions and reports the remainder in a completion message; here there is no cap - the loop is bounded by coverage of the declared tier - and nothing unresolved may end up only in the conversation (ambiguities the loop discovers are not markers and were being discarded). What remains: the shape of the flow and parts of the prompt scaffolding - "enough that the attribution stands and must keep standing while any of it does."
    - No claim that upstream endorses, reviewed or is responsible.
- Borrowed ideas, no code (each row names the version actually followed, "for the same reason R21 pins a dependency": "we follow Conventional Commits" is unfalsifiable; settles that adopters follow the upstream spec, not this project's paraphrase):
  - MADR 4.0.0 (2024-09-17): the decision-record form for ADRs and BDRs (context, options considered, decision, consequences).
  - Conventional Commits 1.0.0: commit message shape.
  - Semantic Versioning 2.0.0: how the standard's own versions move.
  - Keep a Changelog 1.1.2 (2024-09-27): changelog shape, including `## Unreleased` as the place a PR writes.
  - Linear (product, unversioned): the word **sprint** for a bounded period of work (ADR-028, `work-periods`). (Text reads "chosen over 'sprint' because it arrives without the ceremony argument attached" - an artifact of the cycle->sprint rename.)
  - Backlog.md (MrLesk/Backlog.md): not taken from but deliberately stayed compatible with - the shipped backlog is markdown a team can put that tool on top of.
  - RFC 2119 (BCP 14, updated by RFC 8174): meaning of MUST/SHOULD/MAY.
  - Impact Mapping (Gojko Adzic, book): goal -> persona -> impact -> deliverable technique in the greenfield phase.
  - Story Mapping (Jeff Patton, book): journey-then-thinnest-slice technique in the same step.
  - INVEST (Bill Wake, article): the Definition of Ready the shipped backlog requires before an item is pulled.
  - Jobs to be done (no canonical spec): persona motivation shape "when ___, I want to ___, so I can ___" in the persona template and the decision checklist.
  - Nielsen Norman Group's usability heuristics: paved-road default for the UX axis of the decision checklist.
  - OWASP ASVS, SLSA (pinned where cited, not here): referenced as the depth a security baseline can point at; nothing reproduced.
- Compared against, not borrowed from (named in `docs/faq.md`; "None of them influenced its design"): OpenSpec, BMAD, Backstage, adr-tools, ProductSpec (found 2026-08-02 while researching discovery, after every design decision was made; closest neighbour of the five; comparison in the FAQ).
- If something is missing: "An unlisted debt is a bug in this file, not a policy." Open an issue; the project "would rather over-credit".

## RELEASE-NOTES.md — What's new

- Purpose: curated plain-language highlights per release for a stakeholder; `CHANGELOG.md` is the complete developer record.
- 1.0.15 (2026-09-04): sending a session upstream as evidence now has a recommended default; intake offers three choices (send it / send it once you have read it / send nothing) and leads with sending; the assembling step no longer asks a separate yes/no per item - acts on the intake choice once.
- 1.0.13 (2026-09-03): guards that refuse a bad write now refuse when they cannot read the request. All four (remote-database, force-push, CI-secrets, and the elicitation guard) denied on any condition that stops checking (missing tool, unreadable helper, broken points list) except unparseable JSON stdin, which passed silently; now denies.
- 1.0.12 (2026-09-03): the elicitation guard now runs during the adoption. It was wired into the shipped tree only, not the checkout the adoption is driven from, so "the one run the mechanism exists for was the one run without it" - an adoption skipped the intake questions and wrote the intake record from its own prose. The guard works out which side it is on from the tree shape and covers writes into the adopted repository from the first one; reads the record of answers in the repository being written to.
- 1.0.11 (2026-09-03): shipped citations of the standard's own decision records now say so ("standard ADR-010"), including in guard failure messages and the update watcher's issue; writing rule in `docs/conventions.md`; a check keeps it. Found by the first repository to hit it.
- 1.0.10 (2026-09-03): five holes a real adoption (private monorepo) found in guards and workflows, closed upstream: remote-database guard sees migration/seed runners (`pnpm migrate`, `prisma migrate deploy`, `rails db:migrate`) and judges by the host their environment hands them, refusing when it sees no host; elicitation guard refuses every gated write while its points file is unreadable and reads attached rename target-flag spellings; CI-secrets guard's `gh api` match written in the regex dialect it claimed; secret-scan allowance for manifest hashes scoped to the manifests (a 64-hex signing secret in any other JSON is caught); secret-scan workflow verifies the gitleaks download against release checksums; spec-guard workflow runs the backlog archive check and declares a read-only token. Two calls (fail-closed no-host rule; what a bare assignment carries forward) are the maintainer's to confirm. Every guard splits on a lone `&`; a runner's read-only flag trusted only where it reaches the program it names, not after a package-manager script. Dashboard loses a duplicate masthead control and names surfaces by repo slug.
- 1.0.9 (2026-09-03): a file the standard drafted opens with `[NEEDS REVIEW]` naming what it was drafted from (or what it should hold) and the backlog row; `self-verify` reports how many files carry the marker beside the adoption percentage without changing it.
- 1.0.8 (2026-09-03): Layer 2 stack offer asks its own question on its own turn; `record-run` no longer ships into the adopted repo; `dashboard.yml` no longer builds on every push by default; `docs/sprints/` no longer scaffolds for a team that said it tracks work elsewhere. Dashboard switcher reads each surface's real `owner/repo` name and no longer sits beside a second home link.
- 1.0.7 (2026-09-03): landing the guard is checked three ways - a real adoption committed its intake record a commit before the guard landed; now the guard-landing step lives where it cannot be read past, every writing phase checks the guard is there first, and the provenance check refuses if a gate artifact's history predates the guard's own commit (unless `.standards-version` already predates the guard = catching up). ADR-059.
- 1.0.6 (2026-09-03): one marker, not two: `[NEEDS REVIEW]` either way; `[STUB]` from 1.0.3 gone before anything wrote it; an empty file's line says what should end up in it. ADR-058 narrows ADR-057.
- 1.0.5 (2026-09-03): the wiring check reads what the guard reads - a question's id must be named beside the call in the skill, same place the runtime guard reads it; a skill that forgets fails the build; the evidence answer is written into the intake file for later waves.
- 1.0.4 (2026-09-03): the evidence question asks one thing (may an anonymised excerpt go upstream as a PR after you read it); leads the list; none recommended; the anonymous adoption count still goes out on its own at the close. Questions wear plain headers (Evidence, Intent, Layout); the bracketed point id moves where nobody sees it; older transcripts still verify.
- 1.0.3 (2026-09-03): a file the adoption guessed or stubbed opens with `[NEEDS REVIEW]` or `[STUB]` naming who confirms it and the backlog row (one row per file); marker leaves when verified. ADR-057; tooling follows.
- 1.0.2 (2026-09-02): releases tag themselves (merging to `main` creates the tag from `VERSION` with the changelog headings as message; both prior tags were hand-made). The site shows the version it can prove: header number read from the newest tag at load, not baked in at build (it was written in five places on the landing and once shipped two versions at once).
- 1.0.0 (2026-08-19): the standard is stable - the contract surface (numbered rules, manifest, `.standards-version` bookmark with `self-verify` as proof, shipped guards) now costs a MAJOR version to break; latest stays the only target. Version history renumbered so the whole pre-stable line reads 0.8.x/0.9.x (documented in the changelog header and the genesis-history record). Releases tagged from here; adopters still track latest, never a tag. The week before: adopters found real holes (guard suite assuming no repository had ever adopted; a database-restore command the write guard could not see; a consent question with no "keep it, but anonymised" answer) - fixed in 0.9.25. Asking became a mechanism: adoption and specification interviews go through a real question tool, with consent asked where answers get kept.
- 0.9.1 (2026-08-10): dashboard readable (specifications tab with full document, state, success metric, source link; discovery dossiers show contradictions and open threads). Two more outside adoptions changed the standard: a Next.js monorepo run found the corpus scoring descriptions of what an agent did as if its own words (fixed; corpus says which is which per turn); an anonymised run found an adopter can write ten manifest exceptions, stop at 83% and never learn that removing the conflicting toolchain reaches 100% ("an exception silences a finding but never asks whether the thing being excepted should just be deleted"). A green drift-0 run could skip the mid-run health check and final adoption count; both now required, checked for shape and arithmetic. Adoption records carry one commit/PR name shape stating outcome and consent level. Five entries since 0.9.0, all from real usage.
- 0.9.0 (2026-08-10): tested at real scale (13,591-file codebase with nineteen years of history; a monorepo of thirteen independently-published packages each with its own changelog); both shapes supported. Live adoption count: every `align-to-standards` run can send one anonymous disclosed signal (stack, standard version, how clean the result was) and the landing page shows the running count; one environment variable turns it off. 125 entries since 0.8.13, including a fix so a new adopter's first PR no longer fails its own secret scan on a shipped file.
- 0.8.13 (2026-08-03): the standard recognizes a repo that isn't going anywhere (reads README first; deprecated/archived/frozen gets said back instead of a full interview); a repo whose rules forbid AI agents gets a plain stop. Better fit for complicated codebases (workspace of independently-published packages; multiple coexisting technologies). Ten concrete fixes from testing against real public repositories and walking a feature through its full lifecycle.

## CHANGELOG.md — Changelog

- Purpose: the complete developer record; semver: MAJOR = removals/breaking policy changes, MINOR = new standards/modules, PATCH = fixes/clarifications. Entries below are kept where they add/change/remove a rule, file, skill, guard or convention (pure typo entries skipped).
- History-rewrite note (header): the repository's history has been rewritten three times, each narrowly: (1) 2026-08-03 replaced the pre-org-move scaffolding with a curated, honestly-dated genesis sequence ending at 0.8.13; (2) 2026-08-10, cutting 0.9.0, folded six commits that each existed only to correct the commit before it into the commit they corrected, nothing else moved; (3) 2026-08-19, cutting 1.0.0, renumbered the pre-1.0 line: what shipped as 1.0.x and 1.1.x is recorded as 0.8.x and 0.9.x so that 1.0.0 names the first stable release; rewrote version numbers and commit references everywhere (including headings) and folded three more same-rule corrections; entry words otherwise untouched, dates never moved. All three passes: `docs/open-questions/genesis-history.md`.

### 1.0.19 - 2026-09-16

- "A contributed run is the full run, anonymised, or nothing": `record-run` no longer offers a prompts-only vs full-run choice, and `adopt.evidence` no longer holds a record for review before sending. Two external records (`anon-r3k`, 2026-09-04; `anon-x8v`, PR #143) took the prompts-only branch and arrived with `provenance: unverified`, `transcript: null`, verdicts nobody can replay - the outcome ADR-045's revisit clause named. One shape now: literal user turns, the agent's text verbatim, tools run in order (names only), scoring and result line, nothing identifying the repository (opaque code not derived from its name, no owner, paths, hosts, usernames). `adopt.evidence` asks one question with two answers: **send it** (recommended) / **send nothing**; `record-run` asks no consent question at the close, shows the assembled record once and opens the PR from the adopter's account - "that pull request is the review". `record.participation` (ADR-054) retired: whose run reads off the PR author, whether a person answered reads off the agent turns; the write gate on `docs/validation/**/runs/*.json` moves to `adopt.evidence`. `tools/human-prompting.mjs` refuses a run record dated after 2026-09-16 carrying no agent turn and no transcript; earlier records are history and still pass. ADR-062; ADR-045, ADR-054, ADR-061 revised not rewritten.

### 1.0.18 - 2026-09-16

- The verdict says what the percentage counts, on every run: an external adopter's run printed `drift 4 - 93% adopted (74/80)` and their PR arrived titled "assessment to 93%" (read as a grade). It is how much of the manifest is present (ADR-038); the only clause saying so was appended only when a file was flagged unfilled. Both verdict lines now end with `- the percentage is how much of the standard's manifest is present here, not a grade of this repository` (`the built-in skeleton` when no manifest); the fill clause counts flagged files without repeating it. `drift N - NN% adopted (a/b), N excepted` untouched for parsers; `tools/self-verify-drift-test.mjs` asserts the clause on both branches.

### 1.0.17 - 2026-09-04

- Personas built distrust-first, not identity-first: persona template and the dogfooded roster open each persona with what they fear, distrust, or need to see before they act; `Who / context` moved to the last line. Follows psychographic segmentation; source cited in `standard/docs/personas.md`.

### 1.0.16 - 2026-09-04

- The refusal named only one of two working ways to answer it: `pointIds()` reads a point's id from `metadata.source` or from a bracketed `[id]` in a question header (kept because every pre-1.0.4 transcript carries it); the deny message named only the first; both named now.

### 1.0.15 - 2026-09-04

- `adopt.evidence` recommends sending, `record-run` stops asking per item: shipped with two options and no recommended default (ADR-055: consent never nudged); downstream `record-run` showed the batch once then asked a separate yes/no per item - twenty or more identical prompts for one decision. Owner asked for: send by default, or read first, or nothing. Now three options in order: **send it** (recommended; assembles, scrubs, sends with no further question), **send it, once I have read it** (held for one more yes after the whole batch is shown), **send nothing**. `allowed_provenance` stays `human` alone ("recommending an answer is not choosing it for the person"). `record-run` no longer asks per item; the intake answer governs the whole run (ADR-061). `record.participation` (an identity claim) keeps `recommended: null`; the elicitation spec's rule that every consent point declares `recommended: null` narrows to the actual reason a leading answer is barred: an identity claim has none; `adopt.evidence` is not one.

### 1.0.14 - 2026-09-04

- Two checks read a shipped artifact as a defect, on the repository they shipped it to - both found by one real update from 1.0.7 to 1.0.12 on a monorepo of eight applications; both fired on something correct ("the repository can only clear the report by undoing what the standard told it to do").
  - The fill check read the templates' own guidance as an unfilled shell: `docs/PRODUCT.md` and `docs/personas.md` ship an HTML comment showing where a drafting run puts its `[NEEDS REVIEW]` marker, writing `<date>` and `<ID>` inside; `stripCode` removed fenced/indented blocks and code spans but not comments, so the angle pattern read the note as two placeholders; the first repository to hit it deleted the note. `stripCode` now strips comments (as `proseOf` already did). Mustache form unchanged: `RAW_PLACEHOLDER_RE` scans the raw body, so `{{AUTHOR}}` inside a comment still warns (angle form ambiguous enough that a comment lets it pass; mustache never). Three cases pin it. Could not be caught here: `fillPaths` reads an adopter's tree; `docs/PRODUCT.md` exists here only as the shipped template.
  - The marker check could not read an id that wrapped: `[NEEDS REVIEW] ... Backlog: DOC-3.` matched with `Backlog:\s*([A-Za-z][\w-]*)`; `\s` crosses the newline but stops at the next line's `>`; the adopting repo wrapped six markers and got "a [NEEDS REVIEW] marker names no backlog row". Block now read as prose (blockquote prefix stripped before matching). "The misleading diagnosis was the costly half here, not the refusal."

### 1.0.13 - 2026-09-03

- Agent guards no longer fail open on a payload they cannot parse: every early exit denies (missing `jq`, unreadable `lib.sh`, unparseable points file) except unparseable stdin JSON in the elicitation guard and the three shell guards (remote-database writes, force-push, CI-secret writes): a truncated/malformed `PreToolUse` payload passed silently. `elicitation-guard.mjs` denies on `JSON.parse` failure. Shell guards share `read_command()` in `lib.sh`; a well-formed call with no command and unparseable input both printed nothing; only `jq`'s exit code tells them apart, so each guard checks it before trusting an empty command as "nothing to look at". `read_command()` stops leaking `jq`'s parse-error text to stderr (would misscore the new cases in `verifyAgentGuards.sh`'s stderr-sensitive harness). Coverage: a malformed-payload case for each of DB, force-push, CI-secrets, one through the `guards.sh` dispatcher, one for the elicitation guard's stdin (distinct from its malformed-points-file case).

### 1.0.12 - 2026-09-03

- The repository that ships the elicitation guard never ran it: an adoption skipped the intake round and wrote `docs/adoption-intake.md` from the skill's prose ("I mined the file for content when it was a procedure"); nothing refused because `standard/.claude/settings.json` wires the guard into the tree that ships into an adopted repo and binds only after landing; this repo's own `.claude/` carried no `settings.json`; `align-to-standards` runs from a checkout of this repo. Hooks bind from the session's own project directory; ADR-059's Step -1 and phase precondition act on the target. Now the guard reads which side it is on from the tree shape: a checkout carrying the shipped tree under `standard/` drives adoptions; an adopter has that tree unpacked at root and never matches. On the driving side it judges only writes whose owning work tree is not its own; this repository wires it in its own `.claude/settings.json` with the same matcher and deny-on-failure fallback. "Deliberately not an environment variable: a switch is an exemption any adopted repository could set for itself."
  - Second defect: the guard resolved the provenance ledger and the `git show HEAD:` check behind a settled repository-scoped point relative to the working directory (a driving checkout with answered rows would vouch for writes into a repo that answered nothing). Both resolve in the work tree owning the write (as the rename check did). A move reaching two work trees at once has no single owner - refused.
