A gated path whose directory does not exist yet resolves to its nearest existing ancestor.
  - The guard's test asserts: this checkout wires the guard it ships; a driving tree's own writes go through and the identical write is refused in a tree without that shape; an answer committed here settles nothing over there. Named escape: an unasked artifact written *here* is not refused. ADR-060.

### 1.0.11 - 2026-09-03

- A record id in a shipped file said nothing about whose index answers it: the tree cited own records by bare number (`ADR-010`, `ADR-024`, `ADR-033`, `ADR-049` in `AGENTS.md` alone); the private monorepo of run h read `ADR-010` as its own (issue #133). 188 citations across 50 shipped files now carry the qualifier (`standard ADR-010`, or the possessive opening a sentence); 31 markdown; the rest guards, workflows, elicitation points (`standards-update-watch.yml` writes a citation into the adopter's issue). Two spellings already qualified did not move: a link into `repository-standards/core`, and a `PATCHED(repository-standards)` marker introducing its id directly (three markers whose id sat further along the line did move - "the marker qualifies what it introduces and not the whole line"). Ids that are not citations stayed bare or moved into a code span (`decision-records-check.mjs` teaches numbering with `ADR-004` and `BDR-004` as distinct ids - a statement about the adopter's index). `standard/docs/conventions.md`, Writing, states the rule; `tools/prose-check.mjs` fails on an unqualified id in any of the 104 files under `standard/`. `decision-records-check` needs no change (never reads prose).

### 1.0.10 - 2026-09-03

- Five entries are guard/workflow defects found by the private monorepo adoption (run h, `docs/validation/human-prompting/scenarios/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.md`), fixed first in that repo's copy, upstreamed.
- Remote-database guard sees migration and seed runners: `no-remote-db-writes.sh` looked for a SQL client, connection URL or dump restore; `pnpm migrate`, `npx prisma migrate deploy`, `rails db:migrate`, `python manage.py migrate` ran unchecked. Now recognises a package runner or interpreter followed by the migrate/seed verb or an entry file under `migrate/`/`seed/` (alembic, flask db, flyway, liquibase, dotnet ef named explicitly), resolving the host the way the runner does: assignment on the runner's segment, carried from an earlier `export`, process environment, then env files (`.env .env.local database/.env`, overridable via `REMOTE_DB_ENV_FILES` in the hook command). Read-only modes (`migrate:status`, `--dry-run`, `prisma migrate diff`) pass regardless of host unless `--reset` rides along; `DB_SSL_TUNNEL` on refuses whatever host is named. Two safe-side calls open to revision: no host visible anywhere is a denial (reason names where it looked); a bare (unexported) assignment carries forward only when it names a remote host (`DB_HOST=localhost` after an exported remote one does not clear it). "Assignments that persist across segments" logic lives in `lib.sh` as `carried_value`; the client branch uses it too (old command-wide scan let `grep -rn "PGHOST=prod" docs/; psql -c "DROP TABLE t"` deny a local client, and an early `export PGHOST=prod` override an inline `PGHOST=localhost`). A read-only flag (`--status`, `--dry-run`, `--plan`) trusted only where it reaches the program it names: after a package-manager script the host is judged (`pnpm seed-with-reset --status` runs the reset); `pnpm migrate --dry-run` against a remote host is the one existing case whose verdict changed (`migrate:status` is the read-only spelling there).
- A lone `&` separates guard segments: `split_segments` and `split_segments_quoted` in `lib.sh` cut on `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, newlines but not a lone `&`; now they do; the `&` of a redirection (`2>&1`, `&>log`) does not split. Found by the adopting repository's automated review.
- Elicitation guard fails closed on a points file it cannot parse (an unparseable `.claude/elicitation/points.json` was treated as absent - every write allowed; now refuses every gated write until the JSON is fixed) and reads attached rename flags (`movedFromUnder` recognised `-t DIR` only; `mv -tDIR` and `mv --target-directory=DIR` now read). Suite asserts the three forms and the corrupt points file.
- `gh api` writes to CI secrets matched with an extended regex: the branch used `grep -qi 'gh[[:space:]]\+api'` among `grep -qiE` siblings; `\+` undefined in POSIX basic regex; now `-E` with plain `+`; suite exercises the REST branch both directions (PUT and PATCH against secrets/variables routes refused; a plain read and a search for the phrase not).
- The gitleaks tarball verified against release checksums before install: the shipped secret-scan workflow pinned the version and ran whatever the download returned; install step now fetches the release's checksums file and refuses to unpack an archive whose sha256 is not listed (archive keeps its release name because `sha256sum -c` looks it up by name).
- Manifest-hash allowlist scoped to the manifests: the shipped `.gitleaks.toml` allowed a JSON line whose value is 64 lowercase hex characters, scoped by shape alone (belief that `paths` is evaluated first and would switch the rule off for the whole file). `condition = "AND"` requires path and line together; without it the allowlist reached every `.json` in an adopting repo, and 64 hex is also an HMAC signing secret shape (`generic-api-key` the only rule catching unnamed secrets). Entry now carries `condition` and a `paths` regex for the two manifests; path matched repo-relative in git mode and absolute under `gitleaks dir`, so the regex allows a directory prefix rather than anchoring at `^`. Key is `condition`, not `matchCondition` (gitleaks rejects the latter). Verified with gitleaks 8.30.1.
- `spec-guard.yml` runs the backlog archive guard and declares a read-only token: `scripts/backlog-archive-check.mjs` is a required core entry and a blocking diff-kind gate, and no shipped workflow ran it (self-verify notes diff-kind guards instead of running them). A step runs it between the coupling guard and the full-tree audit, against the PR base or the push's `before` commit, skipping with a message when neither; job declares `permissions: contents: read`.

### 1.0.9 - 2026-09-03

- The shipped tree writes the `[NEEDS REVIEW]` marker (closes NEEDS-REVIEW-1): `standard/.claude/elicitation/README.md` shows the marker line for drafted and empty artifacts (ADR-058 fields, no role word); ADR/BDR templates, `docs/personas.md`, `standard/docs/PRODUCT.md` carry the block; `skills/align-to-standards/onboard.md` steps 1, 4, 5 write it under `suggest`/`stub`; a drafted retroactive decision record lands `Proposed`. Manifest hashes regenerated.
- `self-verify` counts the marker; `elicitation-provenance` checks what it names (closes NEEDS-REVIEW-2): count beside the adoption percentage without moving it (ADR-038), one flat number; provenance check: every marker names an existing backlog row (fatal); `provisional` ledger row whose gated files carry no marker = non-fatal warning; both skip scaffolding. Tests in `tools/self-verify-fill-test.mjs`, `tools/elicitation-provenance-test.mjs`.

### 1.0.8 - 2026-09-03

- Dashboard masthead has one navigational control: the 1.0.7 switcher sat beside a second home link (`h1` plain text, a `D.meta.home` pill); `h1` is now the link, pill gone, switcher takes its place; a build without `--registry` (only this repo's `pages.yml` passes it) keeps the single click home from the title.
- Switcher names a surface by its repo slug (`owner/repo`, as `stacks.json` and `CORE_REPO` do), not "Repository Standards"/"Node".
- Layer 2 offer is its own question, asked once and landed visibly (closes STACK-OFFER-2): `adopt.stack` declared in `standard/.claude/elicitation/points.json` on the brownfield path as its own `AskUserQuestion` round; run summary must say what landed; no `gate_globs` (sharing the greenfield point's globs broke that route's gate in testing); `tools/elicitation-replay-test.mjs` names the known limitation.
- Three shipped defaults a private repo had to except (closes ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1): `record-run` now at `skills/record-run/` (transition skill, never shipped - ADR-045's correction); `dashboard.yml` ships `workflow_dispatch` only; `docs/sprints/` and its three skills gated on the recorded `adopt.tracker` answer (external tracker, backlog bridges skips all four). `tools/adoption-fixture-test.mjs` asserts record-run's absence and the push-free workflow; sprint-scaffold skip is prose.

### 1.0.7 - 2026-09-03

- ADR-059: the elicitation guard is landed through its own file, reached only through Step -1: `steps.md`'s guard-landing step sat inline as item "0." ahead of ten reconcile-wave steps ("a single instruction, told once, near the top" - the shape ADR-054 found insufficient). Moved to `skills/align-to-standards/land-guard.md`, reached through a new `SKILL.md` Step -1 read before Step 0 Intake and before any route; `steps.md` opens with a pointer. Named Step -1 so existing "Step 0" references in `intake.md`/`onboard.md` (the intake step) need no change.
- Every phase that writes first confirms the guard landed: a resumed/compacted session can reach a first write on a stale memory. `steps.md` (step 1), `greenfield.md`, `onboard.md` open their first write with the same three-line check (`.claude/elicitation/points.json` exists; `.claude/settings.json` wires `elicitation-guard.mjs`; `docs/adoption-provenance.md` exists) and stop for a restart on nonzero exit. `stack.md`/`layer2.md` carry no check (both require a pre-existing `.standards-version`, implying the guard landed).
- The guard's commit order checked against the Gate artifacts: LomondGroup/propertycloud PR #1199 (PC-2847) committed `docs/adoption-intake.md` a commit before the guard landed on a fresh adoption; self-verify stayed green (compared ledger content, never commit order). `scripts/elicitation-provenance.mjs` (R28) now checks by commit ancestry that the guard's landing commit precedes the oldest commit of `docs/adoption-intake.md`, `docs/adoption-assessment.md`, `.standards-version` - required, blocking; exempt when `.standards-version` already existed the commit before the guard's commit (the update-to-latest shape).

### 1.0.6 - 2026-09-03

- ADR-058 revising ADR-057: one marker. 1.0.3 gave a drafted artifact `[NEEDS REVIEW]` and an empty one `[STUB]`; applied to a real adoption the same day, both answered "has a person looked at this file", and `[STUB]` told less than an empty file deserves (who fills it, not what it is for). Decided: `[NEEDS REVIEW]` only; drafted line as ADR-057 had it minus one field (what it was drafted from, the backlog row); empty artifact's line says nothing is written yet, what the file should contain in one sentence, the backlog row; neither names who acts (the artifact's kind says). One row per marked file; drafted record stays `Proposed`; marker leaves in the verifying commit; percentage does not move (ADR-038); companion count is one flat number. ADR-057 status `Accepted, revised by 058`. Nothing in the shipped tree changed in this release.

### 1.0.5 - 2026-09-03

- Every call site names its point beside the call; the check reads what the guard reads: `elicitation-points-check` accepted a heading plus the tool's name anywhere in the file (four sites on the old header contract passed). Now reads the block each point owns and requires the id beside the call - `metadata.source` `<id>` or the pre-1.0.4 header form - so a site forgetting the field fails the build. The recommended-first rule reads the whole option list and compares the first bold option whole ("do not migrate yet" no longer passes for "migrate"). Shipped elicitation README states the `metadata.source` contract; spec-specify, spec-update, spec-reconcile blocks name their point; transcript checker gains a fixture.
- The evidence answer is written where the close reads it: `adopt.evidence` decides whether step 8 assembles a run record, and step 8 fires at every wave close (sessions that never saw intake). The intake file gains an **Evidence** line; step 8 reads it; a missing line or `pending` row means the question is put now; `record-run` no longer reads as licensed to ask again after **send nothing**.

### 1.0.4 - 2026-09-03

- The evidence question asks one thing and leads with sending: previously asked whether the session may be kept, led with keep-local, closed with record-nothing; adopters took the last. Now: send an anonymised excerpt upstream as a PR after reading, led with; no option recommended (ADR-055); human answer only; `record-run` still takes a per-item yes (ADR-045). The adoption ping is not on the list (no session content; goes out at every wave close, disclosed not asked - ADR-047).
- The question's id travels out of sight: `[adopt.evidence]` in the `AskUserQuestion` header read as code and did not fit the header's twelve characters. The id goes in `metadata.source` (not displayed; lists every id when one call puts several questions); the header says what the question is about (Evidence, Intent, Layout). Guard and transcript checker read the new field first and the bracketed header second (old transcripts/adopted repos still verify).

### 1.0.3 - 2026-09-03

- ADR-057: an artifact the adoption drafted or stubbed says so at the top. Before: one row per question in `docs/adoption-provenance.md`; a run drafting twelve retroactive ADRs under one answer left one row, each written `Status: Accepted`. A separate hand-written hand-off file rejected ("a fourth curated record of the same fact"). Decided: artifact opens with `[NEEDS REVIEW]` (what it was drafted from, backlog row) or `[STUB]` (nothing written, backlog row); neither names who acts; one backlog row per marked file; drafted record stays `Proposed`; marker leaves in the verifying commit ("`git log` is the record of who verified"); percentage does not move (ADR-038); `self-verify` gains a companion count. Three backlog rows under new epic "Adoption leaves the human work visible"; nothing in the shipped tree changes in this release.

### 1.0.2 - 2026-09-02

- The release tag is created by CI: tags `1.0.0` and `1.0.1` were typed by hand. `.github/workflows/release-tag.yml` runs on every push to `main`, reads `VERSION`, tags that commit if the version has no tag (annotated, body = the release's changelog headings), no-op otherwise. Its own file (needs `contents: write` where deploy runs `contents: read`; `pages.yml` cancels in-progress runs, unrecoverable for a tag). Tags authored by `github-actions[bot]`.
- The version is stated only where something reads it: a bump rewrote ten places; `docs/facts.json` declared nine; the landing stated a version five times (pill, disclosure prose, footer, twice in hero script) with only the pill structurally hookable; generated docs carried it in their shell; two places inside coupled capabilities fired the coupling guard on every release (merged past with `--admin`). Landing pill now reads the newest tag from the GitHub API at runtime (like the "Live adoptions" badge since ADR-047), hidden until fetch succeeds; prose names the release line; usage examples read `--version <x.y.z>`; `standard/SPEC.md` says where the number lives. One restatement remains in `standard/standard.manifest.json` (self-verify compares it against `.standards-version`). Gates invert: `site-check` fails any version-shaped string on the landing; `tree-check` fails a shipped tree that restates the version. ADR-056; closes COUPLING-VERSION-1.
- Writing a changelog entry no longer walks a contributor into a red gate: `CONTRIBUTING.md` said add under `## Unreleased`; `tree-check` requires a `VERSION` move to arrive with its own heading; the step now describes the whole act (write under Unreleased, promote to `## x.y.z - <date>`, bump `VERSION` in the same PR, leave a fresh empty `## Unreleased`); the tag is not the contributor's to make.
- Every answer about this repository starts from its records: `standard/AGENTS.md` requires an answer about the repo (state, history, why) to come from the repository's records and cite them, not the model's session memory; `adr-write`'s description narrowed so "what did we decide about X" routes to reading the log.

### 1.0.1 - 2026-08-19

- The elicitation guard no longer waves NotebookEdit through: the hook read the write target only from `file_path`; NotebookEdit passes `notebook_path`; now reads both, and `new_source` alongside `content`/`new_string` for provenance declarations. Found by code review on the first repository to vendor 1.0.0.
- PATCH-per-PR returns to being this repository's own workflow: the 2026-08-18 change leaked into the shipped standard (R18 told adopting repos their PRs bump versions); reversed at every shipped surface - R18 again says a PR never adds a version heading and never bumps, the maintainer cuts every release; changelog template, manifest purpose, file map, decision catalog, changelog method pages agree; R25 keeps the PR-bumps rule for this repository (CONTRIBUTING.md); the method page names the divergence.
- Three arrow glyphs leave `spec-clarify/completion.md` (plain-hyphen convention).

### 1.0.0 - 2026-08-19

- First stable, first tagged release; no behaviour changes (0.9.25 is the tree stabilised).
- The version line renumbered: releases recorded as 0.8.0 through 0.9.25 shipped as 1.0.0-1.0.13 and 1.1.0-1.1.25; cutting a real 1.0.0 on top would make the number ambiguous; renumbered into 0.x. From here MAJOR = removals/breaking policy changes, MINOR = new standards/modules, PATCH = fixes/clarifications. Stability binds the contract surface: numbered rules in `SPEC.md`, the manifest schema, `.standards-version` + `self-verify` as the update loop, the shipped guard set. Standard stays living (ADR-025); a breaking change to that surface owes a MAJOR bump.
- The release is tagged (annotated tag); adopters still track latest, never a tag.

### 0.9.25 - 2026-08-19

- Three defects, one source: the first repository to run `update-to-latest` against 0.9.24 found all from inside its own adoption ("the second time an adopter's checkout caught what every gate in this repository missed"); closes the class too.
- The guard suite no longer assumes the repository was never adopted: `verifyAgentGuards.sh`'s elicitation cases expected refusals that hold only before anything was answered; in an adopted repo (committed rows answering `adopt.personas`, `adopt.layout`) the guard rightly allows and the suite scored two failures. Cases now build a scratch git repository (shipped `points.json`, one tracked file, no ledger) and run inside it. `adoption-fixture-test` commits a ledger answering every repository-scoped point and asserts the suite still passes.
- Restoring a dump into a remote database is a write: `pg_restore -d <remote-uri> dump.tar` carries no verb, `-f` or `.sql`; guard now treats `pg_restore` and `mysqlimport` as write-only clients, denied against any non-loopback host; local restore and `pg_restore --list` allowed.
- Consent can be conditional on anonymisation: `adopt.evidence` offered keep-and-send, keep-local, nothing; the first real adopter answered none: keep it on condition machine paths and identity are scrubbed and the repository appears only as `/git/<repo>`. Fourth answer added (not a different scrub - `record-run` scrubs at every level - but the scrub becomes a condition of consent); two options now reach a PR upstream.

- The ecosystem survey lands as work: DISCO-5 (six channels + spec-kit's catalogues, "an inclusion bar that let a two-star project in"), DISCO-6 (no-tags policy closes every channel requiring a Release), new epic with DELEGATE-1 (gitleaks, commitlint, editorconfig-checker, Renovate, markdownlint), SUCCEED-1 (repolinter archived 2026-02-06), EMIT-1.

### 0.9.23 - 2026-08-19

- Asking is now a mechanism, not an instruction: `AGENTS.md` said "ask once, up front" from the beginning; across twenty-two shipped skills zero calls to `AskUserQuestion`. A complete adoption against a real repository asked one question in 1140 transcript lines, invented five personas, appended a section to thirty-three decision records the owner wrote, renamed `docs/ADR`/`docs/BDR` across seventy-eight files, and quoted the owner authorising a full migration in a sentence never typed. Three layers:
  - `.claude/elicitation/points.json` declares the twenty-two places the standard must ask instead of decide: the question, the answer that leads, the three kinds of answer always offered, the provenance each answer records, the paths it gates. `elicitation-points-check` fails when a declared point has no call site, against a baseline that may only shrink.
  - `.claude/hooks/elicitation-guard.mjs` is a `PreToolUse` hook: a `Write`/`Edit` to a gated path refused unless that point's question fired this session; also a `Bash` command moving a git-tracked path. Reads the transcript structurally (an assistant turn that called the tool) because compaction replays the model's summary as a user turn - a textual scan would let a run vouch for itself.
  - `docs/adoption-provenance.md`: one table, one row per point; `scripts/elicitation-provenance.mjs` checks it: a `provisional` answer names an existing backlog row; a `human` answer names who and when; `pending` stops being acceptable once this adoption writes at a gated path.
  - The guard permits writing a **stub**: a write declaring its point `absent` passes, the gap stays visible, the run reads unfinished. "Guessing is what has no legal path."
  - Measured: replayed past the guard, the run that caused this is stopped at 5 of 5 artifact writes; a run only claiming to have asked is stopped; a run that asked is never refused. Those and two more are `tools/elicitation-replay-test.mjs`, alongside 31 guard cases and 20 ledger cases. The ledger check run over that repository's real artifacts named the same five (intake, decision records, personas, backlog, product) without the transcript. Cost: an earlier draft treated everything under a shipped directory as scaffolding (thirty-seven real records read as untouched template); now separated three ways: verbatim files compared to hash; a directory's scaffolding recognised by the standard's naming; a fill-from-repo slot counts as untouched only while it holds the shipped placeholders.
  - Renaming what a repository has is allowed - unasked renaming is not: the hook reads `Bash` and refuses a move whose source git tracks; untracked paths pass. The question offers reshaping as its second answer; `[adopt.records]` widened to cover editing existing records (previously only records the repo lacked).
  - The `pending` rule was red on arrival for brownfields (fired on records, `PRODUCT.md`, specs written years before): reaching now scoped to what the adoption itself wrote - the commit introducing the point list, everything after, everything uncommitted (point list rather than `.standards-version`, same tree on a fresh adoption and years apart on an update). Where no git work tree, the check says so and stands down. Verified on a real pre-adoption clone.
  - The hook cannot bootstrap itself: alignment lands the guard, points, matcher and ledger first (none gated) and stops for a restart. The guard asks git about renames in the repository the move targets. The `PreToolUse` matcher now names `Bash` (said `Write|Edit|NotebookEdit`); a test asserts every tool the guard judges is one the wiring reaches.
  - Not proven: that an answer, once given, was honoured - ADR-054 records the known gap.
- The recommended answer always points at the standard: run the same day, it asked (three `AskUserQuestion` calls, nine questions, every declared one carrying its point id) and four of five recommendations named the least convergent answer (e.g. keep your own layout and map the standard onto it). Every point declares `recommended`; the first option a skill offers must be it; `elicitation-points-check` fails when they disagree (`tools/elicitation-points-check-test.mjs`). The recommendation is the answer that converges on the standard, or the answer a person gives now rather than defers; `null` legal only for consent. Keeping the repository's own way stays on every list but never first.
- The point list is a floor; questions a run invents grow it: the ledger carries a second table for invented questions; `elicitation-provenance.mjs` fails when missing. A point names every file that asks it; `elicitation-points-check` reads each option order. Two new points from the live run: `adopt.tracker` (where tracked work lives when already tracked elsewhere), `adopt.profile` (greenfield asked it bundled with the stack; brownfield never asked - the copied manifest carries a default). Changed: `adopt.records` leads with rewriting records into the standard's shape; `adopt.backlog` leads with a seeded backlog whose rows name the role, not a person.
- Questions asked in the user's language (from their first message; deliberately not a point); artifacts' language is a decision: new point `adopt.language`; `AGENTS.md`'s `Working language` slot never asked; recommended: code and commits in English, docs and specs in the team's language; the one point where `inferred` is legitimate.
- An already-adopted repo gets this layer through the update, wired: `update-to-latest` lands guard, points, matcher, ledger first, then stops for a restart; the matcher is the half that goes missing (merging into `.claude/settings.json` is not a copy); ledger arrives all `pending` and must not be back-filled. The skill says self-verify reads the whole tree against the target, not the delta.
- The shipped entry line stopped asking the user to ask: was `follow repositorystandards.com - take this repo onto the standard, interview me for what you need` (`prompts.md` scored three runs `asked` then annotated "asked because the line told it to"). Now `adopt this repo to repositorystandards.com` in README, quick start and method; the hook refuses to write an unasked artifact.
- The flow that writes specifications now asks: `spec-clarify` had questions as chat markdown (bolded question, `Recommended: Option A`, lettered table, "reply with the option letter"); `spec-specify` asked nothing. Every clarify question is now a tool call; `spec-specify` asks the boundary before Purpose/Scope, what done means before acceptance criteria, the unknowns in place of the informed guess; `spec-update` asks before a change moves a boundary; `spec-reconcile` asks before rewriting a criterion to match what was built. `skill` in the declaration takes a list like `file`; `elicitation-points-check` requires a call site in every declared skill; a named-but-missing file fails loudly. Both patched skills at their recorded budget ceiling (ratchet may only shrink): question detail moved to siblings; marker cap and priority order stated once.
- Asked once, or asked every time: every point declares a `scope`. **repository**-scoped: once its ledger row says somebody answered and that row is committed, later sessions write without asking (committed, not merely written - closes laundering). **work**-scoped: belongs to the piece of work in front of the run; no ledger row settles it. Both directions asserted.
- The validation corpus says what it can and cannot evidence: thirty-eight run records were read as evidence the standard elicits well; 31 of 377 observations in one suite and 7 of 109 in the other bear on human interaction, none checked against a transcript. Each record carries an `$elicitation` block; `validation-claims-check` fails when counts disagree; a record claiming a person was present must name an existing transcript.
- The close of an adoption happens before the pull request (ADR-055): the three steps producing what the project learns (upstream friction review, offer to keep the session, anonymous ping) sat after "open one focused PR"; the largest field adoption yet (25 commits, 253 files, 20 questions) ran none; the counter still read 12. Reading order is execution order: friction, record, ping, PR last; consent asked in intake as `adopt.evidence` (gates `docs/adoption-intake.md`; recommends nothing; human only). Permission to assemble is not permission to send (`record-run` per-item yes). The ping reads the counter back and reports both numbers.

Measured on a real adopting repo: three files, three Documents cards, zero ideas in the pool. The merge now runs both ways on the same key (lowercased title); a file-based idea enters the pool via `asItem()`, identified by filename; status goes through the same reader (`parked` stays, `graduated` closes). Unchanged: todo/doing/blocked/done tiles count `task`/`bug` only. `tools/dashboard-ideas-test.mjs`.

### 0.9.3 - 2026-08-13

- The pre-PR check list promised the CI set and four were missing (`node tools/adoption-gates-test.mjs`, `bash standard/scripts/verifyAgentGuards.sh`, `node tools/validation-test.mjs`, `tree-check --self`); all four added in CI's order.

### 0.9.2 - 2026-08-13

- The pair check could not see a schema kept in one file: `schema-pair` walked a directory; a repo with a single `database/schema.sql` had no way to run it (`--dir` demanded a `pair:` comment from every `.sql`; installing it printed a false skip). `--file <path>` names a whole schema in one file; a named target not found is an error; `--dir`/`--file` each say so when handed the other kind. Walk skips `node_modules` and `.git` (as `facts-check`).

### 0.9.1 - 2026-08-10

- The dashboard summarised everything and let you read nothing: the dashboard (surface for people who never open the repository) showed a state projection with every document clipped to a one-line summary and a single hyperlink; Specifications had no tab; discovery dossiers were not read. Now: a build-time markdown-to-HTML pass (headings, lists, tables, code, blockquotes); a Specifications tab (list left, full document right, state / who it serves / success metric at top, section navigation, link to the source on the forge); every unresolved open marker (`NEEDS CLARIFICATION`, `DECISION`, `INPUT`, `ASSET`) listed as a gap on the document. Discovery dossiers appear on Documents with contradictions, revisit signals and entry table; an idea names the discovery topic it came from. Dossier bodies are the exception (raw material naming people; the page goes on the open web): shipped only under explicit `--with-discovery`, resolving to off when `--anonymise` is also passed; the page says when bodies are withheld. Tab strip: `Now` (a sprint view) is skipped for repositories with no sprints, tiles go with it; an attention list (specs blocked on a missing input; discovery topics newer than the spec that consumed them) moves onto `Backlog`, which becomes the first tab. `Backlog` tab counts what the tab holds (was `todo + doing + blocked` only: 14 on the tab above a list of 41); filter chips by `type` (task, bug, idea, open question) instead of epic headings. Cost stated: this repo's page 247 KB -> 777 KB (five spec bodies inlined); no server, no build step at the reader's end; discovery bodies stay off by default.
- The corpus gets its first outside run: `2026-08-10-f-adopter-nextjs-mt` - full brownfield adoption of 0.9.0 on a private multitenant Next.js monorepo, one session, contributed through `record-run` by an owner identified only by codename. Twenty-five prompt rows `A34`-`A58` (first typed by someone adopting for their own reasons; `A21`-`A25` reported rows came from the maintainer's probes). 29 observations: 12 pass, 12 partial, 5 fail. Failures include: agent read `.standards-version` as a pin until corrected; proposed a local fix for a shipped-guard defect until the owner said take it upstream; was asked `jak idzie?` three times (`A40` is the one row whose pass condition is its own absence). A first version covered 7 of 43 user turns with every agent turn a description - and passed every check; the owner rejected it; the renderer had no field to say a turn was quoted; scored as `A52` and `A56`/`A57`; `tools/human-prompting.mjs` gained `said_verbatim` per agent turn and `tools_in_order` per observation, validated and rendered distinctly; rebuilt run covers 43 of 43 user turns and 269 agent turns, 268 verbatim. `A58` added from a separate conversation about the adoption itself: the standard names a fallback for a user who is unavailable but not one who grants broad autonomy mid-run; this repository's `docs/personas.md` and `specs/capability-map.json` shipped with none of the "unconfirmed" marking; the `onboard.md` fix left as a linked follow-up.
- An anonymised run found drift that no exception could close: private company repository, Node stack, profile core, anonymised (shape only, no paths/ticket ids, every user turn verbatim). It carried a third-party spec-driven-development toolchain on the same ground as the standard's flow, including a specs layout the structure guard forbids. Unattended, the agent wrote ten manifest exceptions, stopped at drift 1 / 83% adopted, with a written defence of the local layout; told to remove the toolchain, it finished at drift 0 / 94%. "an exception still lets a run stop, and nothing in the flow asks whether the thing being excepted is something the adopter would rather delete"; the layout guard reads the repository's own file list, so its finding was un-exceptable by construction. The entry says `A34` records the entry line this run adds (the stack-naming prompt in Polish, with a typo and an unrelated git instruction bundled ahead - in `prompts.md` that row is `A59`); per-turn `asked` across the corpus falls 76% -> 72%.
- An adoption reached drift 0 without producing the two artifacts a human decides on: a real Node alignment finished green having produced neither the Gate 2 health report nor the Gate 5 count; the owner got numbers only after asking twice ("the adoption had happened to them rather than guided them"). ADR-042 had made intake a required manifest entry (Gate 0) and stopped there. ADR-048, R27: `docs/adoption-assessment.md` becomes a required manifest entry with a template (sibling of the intake entry); a new `adoption-gates` guard reads both artifacts for shape and arithmetic: all eight assessment passes rated `absent` / `partial` / `solid`, top risks named, findings grouped by the owner role that must act, a scope block whose categories sum to the total it claims with an owner role on every alignment item. Checks shape, never judgment; skips itself when no assessment exists (self-verify already reports that drift); a guard cannot carry a manifest exception. Two fixes alongside: the scope block's format lived only in a Zone 1 method page (`standard/docs/backlog.md` carried the `owner` column and never mentioned the block); "plan-only or execute" asked at Step 0 stays as an intent, and the consent licensing a wave is re-asked after Gate 2 with the drift number and count in front of the user.
- An adoption record arrived under a different commit subject every time (`docs(validation)`, `feat(human-prompting)`, `feat(validation)`): one shape for commit and PR: `feat(real-adoption): <repo slug or code>, <stack> - what the run showed`; outcome half not optional (an abandoned run says so); identity half bound to the consent level: Level 2 names the slug, Level 1 gets an opaque code not derived from the repository's name; `reporting.md` names the same shape; `real-adoption` scope is a claim that the session was somebody else's (project-driven runs keep their existing scope).

### 0.9.0 - 2026-08-10

- Summary: 125 entries across the week since 0.8.13: real-repo sampling (Python repositories, three human-prompting waves, a 13,591-file long-lived repository) and walking every loop (discovery, spec, work-cycles, clarify, update) end to end. New machinery: `record-run` (captures real adoption sessions with per-item consent) and the adoption-stats service (ADR-047, first honest count of live adoptions on the landing page). Backlog and dashboard unify around one typed index (ADR-046); a guard-suite audit closed a live bypass; the CI secret scan stopped failing on the shipped manifest. The earlier heading "No entries were written for 0.9.0, 0.9.1 and 0.8.2 - 0.8.12" comes from a stray `VERSION` bump on 2026-08-03 - never a release.
- The secret scan failed on the standard's own manifest (2026-08-10): a manifest entry's sha256 for `.claude/hooks/no-ci-secret-writes.sh` (the word `secret` + 64 high-entropy hex) tripped gitleaks' entropy rule on every adopter's first scan. Allowlist scoped by shape, not file: `paths` is the wrong tool (gitleaks matches a path entry before evaluating `condition`; `condition = "AND"` alongside `paths` behaved as a blanket exemption - verified: a planted non-hex secret went unreported). The shipped entry matches a JSON key whose entire value is 64 lowercase hex, only for `generic-api-key`. Workflow gains `-v` (prints location and rule; `--redact` keeps the value out). (Reworked in 1.0.10, which scoped by path with `condition`.)
- Twenty minutes of silence is indistinguishable from a hung session (2026-08-10): `AGENTS.md` gains a required, manifest-tracked section: while work runs, one or two sentences every 60-120 seconds saying what is happening and what it waits on; stated as obeyable ("do not chain a long run of silent tool calls"); waiting counts ("still running, about 8 minutes"). Outranking clause: "never invent progress, and never report a result that has not come back". `align-to-standards` states the rule binds the align run itself (brownfield assessment bites hardest). Same day: where work has a real count, heartbeat reports `Progress: N/M (Z%)`; otherwise `~Z% (estimate)` (the measured-vs-estimated split `timeline-update` uses).
- The landing claimed field-run adoption and nothing could be checked (2026-08-10): a consent-gated "send this? y/n" rejected ("a voluntary gate costs nothing to skip, so it gets skipped"). Disclosed, not asked: `align-to-standards` sends one ping per completed run (stack, standard version, final drift, day-granularity date; no repo name, no IP, no cross-ping identifier) to a Cloudflare Worker backed by D1, stating what it sends; skippable with `REPOSTDS_NO_TELEMETRY=1` (ADR-047). Landing reads the count client-side on every load (badge hidden until fetch succeeds). `site-check.mjs`'s external-host allowlist (own host + `github.com`) gained the Worker's host as a third explicit entry; a blinking dot dropped.
- A wrapped line turned the remote-database guard off (2026-08-10): an adopter probed the guards; `psql -h prod-db.example.com \` + `-c "DROP TABLE users"` on the next line was allowed (host and verb required in the same segment; segments split on `|` and newlines). Now where a command reaches is per segment; what it does is read across the whole string. Also closed: absolute path (`/usr/local/bin/psql`), `bash -c` wrapper, `-hHOST`, `PGHOST` in the environment, keyword-value conninfo; a remote client whose SQL comes from command substitution is refused. Deleting a remote branch joins force-push as history destruction; `COMMENT ON`, `REASSIGN OWNED`, `pg_terminate_backend` join the write verbs. Quoted local host (`-h "localhost"`) and a unix socket directory (`-h /var/run/postgresql`) are local.
- A hook that exits 127 is a command that ran unchecked (2026-08-10): `settings.json` invoked guards through `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`; unset -> exit 127, empty stdout -> non-blocking. Guards run behind `guards.sh`, which resolves them from its own path and denies when any is missing, unreadable, or exits without a verdict; `settings.json` denies when `guards.sh` cannot run.
- The guard suite went green while a live bypass sat in it (2026-08-10): `verifyAgentGuards.sh` had no piped or wrapped case, scored any non-empty stdout as denial, discarded stderr and exit code. Now a denial must be well-formed deny JSON; nonzero exit or unexpected stderr is its own failure; harness checked against deliberately broken guards; the suite runs in this repo's CI (never had).
- An item cannot leave the delta by being classified out of it (2026-08-10): a run put `record-run` on a "consciously skipped" list (ADR-045 settled it the other way). `align-to-standards` separates: ordering a wave is judgment; membership is not - the open list comes from measurement (`self-verify` against the manifest once pinned, the assessment before), the ship boundary is ADR-009's, a deferral is open drift re-read from measurement. A considered change dropped after a field test: bounding the exception hatch to members the repo changed would have turned a recorded decision into drift - the `usebruno/bruno` trial drops `pre-pr-review` because that project ships its own review skill; deliberate absence is legitimate; it costs a reviewed manifest line and lowers the percentage (property now has a test).
- The human-prompting corpus can hear from a real adoption (2026-08-09): 21st shipped skill `record-run` closes an `align-to-standards` session (success, partial, abandoned) by offering to assemble it into the corpus (a `prompts.md` row and a scored `runs/*.json`) at one of two consent levels; nothing sent without a per-item yes. `CONTRIBUTING.md` gains "Contributing by validation".
- The dashboard's Backlog tab showed build work and nothing else (2026-08-09): three index files (`backlog.md`, `docs/open-questions/README.md`, `docs/ideas/README.md`); `backlog.md` is now the one index (ADR-046): every row carries `type`; each type keeps its own status vocabulary (`open-question` two-state `open`/`decided`); deliberation in own files; folder READMEs lose their tables. `generate-dashboard`: `type` defaults to `task`; task-health counters scoped to `task`/`bug`; an unrecognised status word (previously fell to `todo`) is now a recognised word for every shipped type; plain pill per non-task type; a hardcoded "23 open questions and 1 idea" count dropped from `backlog.md` (no `facts.json` entry).
- R18 covers a monorepo of independently-published units (2026-08-09): `rails/rails`'s thirteen gems, each independently versioned with its own changelog; R18 gains a second clause: one changelog per unit, same one-mechanism rule applied per unit.
- An existing decision process gets an exception, not a second home (2026-08-09): Step 0 detects a mailing-list/RFC pointer, an `rfcs/` or `doc/design/` directory, a governance doc, and asks: adopt the ADR/BDR mechanism or keep the repo's own process recorded as a named exception against R5's manifest requirement.
- Intake leaves a record (2026-08-09): new rule R26 and required manifest entry `docs/adoption-intake.md`: filled before any work proceeds, never deferred, read by `self-verify`.
- No npm package, for now (2026-08-09): DISCO-1 had decided by assuming; `npm-as-a-channel` records the decision; no sign discovery runs through npm, no non-author adopter feeling `degit` as friction, no tag.
- The bounded period of work is a sprint (2026-08-07): `cycle` was picked (Linear uses it) and lost to use - people stop at the word; the dashboard tab label needed a sentence. Renamed everywhere: `docs/sprints/<team>/`, `sprint-open`, `sprint-close`, `scripts/sprint-guard.mjs`, the `work-sprints` capability spec, manifest entries, dashboard labels. ADR-041 defines the borrowed word against what it imports: no story points, no planning poker, no velocity as a commitment, an agreed date that may move rather than a timebox, no prescribed ceremony. ADR-028 renamed, not superseded; the open question records the overturn; showcase fixture moves in the same wave; no migration (no release carried the old paths).
- Two of the four reports were invented here (2026-08-07): changes-per-week from the changelog and finished-items-by-capability shown on a repo with no cycles; reports now come from closed cycles only; no Reports tab without one (same rule as Timeline and Cycles); weekly series removed from the data. Documents tab's four unclickable counters removed; search first.
- Following the procedure exactly could destroy a repository's release history (2026-08-07): `nlohmann/json`'s changelog is `ChangeLog.md`; manifest requires `CHANGELOG.md`; `self-verify` reads directory listings (reports missing); the adopter creates the file; on APFS or NTFS the write lands on the existing file (reproduced: 344 KB changelog replaced by a template). Stated accurately: inside git the overwrite lands as ` M ChangeLog.md` and `git checkout --` restores it; the danger is that it is quiet under `git add -A`. `existsSync` true (kernel case-insensitive), listing false. A missing required file is now checked for a case twin first; warning names file, hazard and two safe routes; fires on every entry class including `fill-from-repo`. Nothing had mentioned filesystem case sensitivity.
- The conversations are readable, and every typed line says who typed it (2026-08-07): `scenarios/` carries one generated page per run. Two rendering rules: a user turn is quoted, an agent turn is not (scorer's account, labelled as description); every typed line names its source, derived (opening line checked against the corpus row, labelled verbatim when it matches; every later line labelled as written by the scorer improvising a user). Nine of eighty-eight turns said so by hand; fifteen carry the label; "most observations are one prompt and one reply".

(b) A setext heading is a heading: section check was ATX-only; ripgrep's changelog is setext by its release checklist; three cases incl. two false positives (table row above delimiter; section in a code fence). (c) `facts-check` answered 0 for `*/CHANGELOG.md` (walk started at the empty fixed part; Rails told it had none of thirteen). (d) Nothing checked which branch shipped workflows name (`branches: [main]`; `requiredKeys` only asserts `on.push` exists): `self-verify` warns when the named branch is not the default, silent when it cannot read one. (e) The adoption skill still said `spec-guard.yml` reads `.nvmrc`; it pins an exact version; corrected, branch trap documented.

- A listed repository was being counted as an assessed one (2026-08-07): "103 of 118 targets were assessed at depth L1" - 76 of 118 rows have no observation in `runs/`; the page leads with the computed split; 42 is the number a coverage claim can rest on; a counting bug (only the `repo:` prefix stripped, so both fixtures read unobserved) fixed - every `<kind>:` prefix stripped.

Two forms now read from the raw file: `{{UPPER_SNAKE}}` and the `> **Template …` banner; mustache restricted to that shape (`${{ github.token }}` is content); angle form still reads the stripped body. `spec-structure` recognised only the angle form as "not a persona" in two places; both readers share one test.
- The guard told authors to delete a file the standard told them to write (2026-08-06): `spec-structure` classed `specs/<cap>/checklists/` as scaffolding; `spec-specify` creates `checklists/requirements.md`; R13 and ADR-010 make only `plan.md`/`tasks.md` ephemeral; `spec-reconcile` says the checklist survives the close.
- The change path never closed the discovery loop (2026-08-06): `Last reconciled:` was moved only by minting/clarifying skills; `spec-impact` now reads the dossier and names unreconciled entries; `spec-update` marks what it folded in and moves the stamp (nothing folded = stamp stays). A single handover contradicting itself is diffed against itself first (contradiction row names the two people); the dossier README comes from the shipped `docs/discovery/_template.md`.
- The update skill named no way to get the thing it reads (2026-08-06): the `git clone` (never `degit` - history needed) is in the skill; step 3 gains the changed-upstream-and-diverged-locally branch (three-way merge against the entry's reference copy; `merge`/`fill-from-repo` entries carry no `sha256`, so the skip was silent).
- A decision record could not say what made it binding (2026-08-06): templates gain **Decided by** (default "the author"); writing skills ask for body, date, tally, quorum.
- Stack detection counted files instead of weighing them (2026-08-06): Deno carries 715 `package.json`, 62 `tsconfig.json`, vendored `node_modules/` against one `Cargo.toml`; the step weighs what a manifest is doing, discounts fixtures/vendored trees/compat surfaces, names the deciding file. A workspace manifest composing repositories (Zephyr's `west.yml`, 79) is detected: this repository is the adoption unit, siblings are not adopted transitively, what it pins is a supply-chain ADR. Greenfield intake accepts "not decided the technology yet" (Layer 1 completes, stack step deferred, no fallback generated). `self-verify` prints the stack layer's own version.
- The tools index listed ten of eighteen tools (2026-08-06): eight newest missing (`prose-check`, `provenance-check`, `site-behaviour`, `clarify-gate-test`, `decision-records-check-test`, `self-verify-drift-test`, `manifest-hashes`, `validation`); rows read off each tool's header paragraph; "a habit, written down as one".
- The pull-request template asked about one decision stream (2026-08-06): `ADR impact` -> `Decision-record impact`, reading both streams, with the new state "contradicts an accepted record" (must stop a PR); restatements moved (manifest purpose, file map, tree's index, `pre-pr-review` step 6).
- Six files saying "TODO." bought sixteen percentage points (2026-08-06): on a sparse repo (manifest, pin, verifier) six `# Title` + `TODO.` files moved `drift 19 - 21% adopted (5/24)` to `drift 19 - 37% adopted (11/30)`; the earlier logged figures (25% vs 12%) did not reproduce (on the full tree the percentage does not move). The placeholder warning recognised only surviving template placeholders and walked a hardcoded eight-file list (`CONTRIBUTING.md` never on it). ADR-038: adopted percentage is structural, substance is judgment; rejected: required sections per entry; a length threshold. Shipped: file list derived from the manifest; a warning (never drift) on a body with no content beyond headings or a nothing-yet marker; the verdict line states the percentage counts entries present. Five cases in `tools/self-verify-fill-test.mjs`.
- By-reference links the tree ships are checked (2026-08-06): `link-check` resolves every `.../repository-standards/core/blob|tree/main/<path>` link against the local tree across every tracked and untracked text file (hand-swept four times before; instances lived in a shell script and a JSON example); 33 links today; anchors and trailing punctuation stripped; `<placeholder>` paths skipped.
- A manifest entry claimed it shipped in a release nobody has cut (2026-08-06): `since: "0.8.14"` against `VERSION` 0.8.13 -> `unreleased`; `tree-check` fails any `since` that is neither `unreleased` nor an `x.y.z` at or below `VERSION` (numeric part comparison); `--self` eleven cases (four pass, seven fail).
- The decision catalog was missing from `conventions.md` (2026-08-06): both `taxonomy.md` and `checklist.md` linked from the same sentence.
- "CI gates compliance from the first push" was true of a workflow that never ran on a push (2026-08-06): `spec-guard.yml` carried `on: pull_request:` only; now also `push: branches: [main]`; on push the structure guard reads the whole tree and the coupling guard diffs against the `before` commit (checking it exists and still exists); `on.push` joins the entry's declared keys (drift test case); a repo that cannot run it on pushes records an exception.
- Four public surfaces said 0.8.0 (2026-08-06): `docs/faq.md`, `README.md`, `llms.txt`, `site/llms.txt` (not among the five version surfaces `facts-check` covered); all declared in `docs/facts.json` (`site/llms.txt` separately); cutting a release now moves nine restatements.
- A repo running two technology stacks could register one (2026-08-06, ADR-037): `stack.manifest.json` stays for one stack; `stack.<technology>.manifest.json` per coexisting stack, read in filename order, each named with its technology, one drift number; an unparseable stack manifest is drift naming the file; two stacks declaring the same path is reported, not resolved; `mystack.manifest.json` is not read as a stack.
- The capability map had no way to say "that code is not in this repository" (2026-08-06, ADR-039): an entry may be `{ "external": "<repo>", "reason": "<why no glob here reaches it>" }`, both fields required (an external binding with no reason refuses the map); nothing enforced for it, never counted as a glob, still must have a spec, named on every audit and counted in the verdict; a capability with both globs and external couples on the local part.
- An alternate path satisfied a shipped file on its name alone (2026-08-06): a directory at an `altPath` is checked by name (a port); a single-file `copy` entry at an `altPath` was never compared (reproduced: `SPEC.md` moved and replaced by one line -> drift 0). A file at an alternate path is now hashed; a deliberate rewrite records `{ "kind": "content" }`. `self-verify-drift-test` stops hand-writing its case count (said 20 while 22 ran).
- The coupling audit could not see a capability until somebody staged it (2026-08-06): `--audit` used `git ls-files` with a filesystem fallback only when git listed nothing; now reads tracked and untracked files with ignore rules applied; three held cases (untracked code inside a glob is claimed; ignored build output is not unclaimed; a glob whose only matches are untracked is not a dead glob).
- A spec could declare `buildable` and carry none of what makes it buildable (2026-08-06): the structure guard checks `## Data contracts`, `## Interface contracts`, `## Acceptance criteria` on any spec declaring `buildable`; three shapes pass: `behavioral`; a heading saying in one line it does not apply ("None known."); a named sub-spec held to the tier only when it claims one; an empty heading or template instructions do not count. No tier at all is warned, not blocked (R9 makes buildable the default; failing every undeclared spec mid-adoption switches the guard off). This repo's `tree-guard`, `verify-engine`, `web-surface` lacked `## Data contracts`; all three now carry one.
- A repo could be certified compliant on files that were in no commit (2026-08-06): a required entry that exists but is git-ignored and untracked is drift, quoting the rule (`.gitignore:1:/docs/`); the test is ignored **and** untracked (not merely untracked - files are authored before staged); a non-git tree is checked as before. Reproduced on a three-file fixture (`OK - drift 0 - 100% adopted (2/2)` with `/docs/` ignored). Four cases (ignored entry; after `git add -f`; unstaged working tree; non-repository).
- The decision machinery: eight open failures (2026-08-06): (a) the idea path was unreachable: `adr-write`/`bdr-write` pointed at the `docs/ideas/` folder, never at `idea-write`; graduating sent the spec half to `spec-specify` unconditionally (minting `specs/<idea-slug>/` next to the existing spec); routing now goes through `spec-impact`; `spec-specify` no longer treats a name miss as a new capability. (b) A retired spec had no legal way to stop being false (ADR-036): `retired` freezes behaviour, not truth; corrected whenever a change makes a statement false, in that change's PR, bounded to what the capability did and what superseded it; claims no guard. (c) Ripple search stopped at specs, records, code; `spec-impact` produces a verdict per artifact (runbooks, personas, product scope, backlog DoD). (d) An unfilled record author (`{{AUTHOR}}`) was invisible; the scan reaches records by filename pattern (both layouts), reading a mustache token and an all-ellipsis row; skills fill the row with a person. (e) Two brownfield shapes: a repo with its own formal decision process takes a pointer record (decision stated in-repo, upstream document linked as authority); a founding decision with no code fingerprint is written from stated rationale, sources named, marked reconstructed, held `Proposed`. (f) The decision catalog's delivery axis assumed the repo owns its release gate; new fork: name the gate and what it can do, decide who watches it, decide the response before it fires.
- A request describing two capabilities was split by the one skill barred from asking (2026-08-06): `/spec-specify` detected collisions by exact slug match (minted `manager-shift-reassignment` beside `shift-swap-request`); now checks collision by behaviour (Purpose, Scope, Out of scope of near capabilities); for a two-capability sentence: write the spec for the primary capability and record the boundary as a typed `NEEDS DECISION` marker naming the other (blocks the clarify gate); near-misses go in the completion report.
- The clarify record had no fixed place in the spec (2026-08-06): template marks the position (immediately after `## Purpose`, before `## Scope`); the heading itself is not shipped (the gate greps for it); `tools/clarify-gate-test.mjs` asserts anchor, position and absence.
- A business decision could say how it would fail and not how it is held (2026-08-06): the BDR template gains `Confirmation`; `bdr-write` asks "what would catch us breaking this?" (naming where compliance is checked is required; designing it is the ADR's job; "nothing yet" is a real answer).
- The manifest asserted the required decisions R7 says it does not have (2026-08-06): `required: true` removed from all eight catalogued decision entries; the summary line carries no number; a `decisions` entry declaring `required` is a FAIL naming R7; four cases.
- A capability built where its globs do not look changed with `spec-guard: OK` (2026-08-06): the plan template's default layer folders (`models/`, `services/`, `cli/`, `lib/`) matched no capability glob; default now sliced by capability; the Structure Decision field says to bind chosen paths in the map and check with `--audit`; where the map declares `$unclaimed`, the diff run reports changed files neither claimed nor declared (deletions excluded).
- Two capabilities in one folder had no way to say which file was whose (2026-08-06): a map entry may start with `!` (exclusion); escapes closed: an exclusion handing a file to nobody is unclaimed; a dead exclusion reported; a capability of only exclusions refused; an object-form exclusion refused; exclusions under `$unclaimed` refused. Ten cases and a `never` assertion.
- The persona gate failed CI on four of the six files a plan writes (2026-08-06): `research.md`, `data-model.md`, `quickstart.md`, `contracts/` excluded like `plan.md`/`tasks.md`/`checklists/`; the full-tree warning about committed scaffolding grows the same four; directory forms anchored at `specs/<capability>/`; three cases.
- A spec could carry two `## Clarifications` sections (2026-08-06): `spec-structure` refuses a level-2 heading appearing twice, naming the lines; level 2 only; headings inside fenced blocks and HTML comments ignored; six cases.
- The standard was run against a 13,591-file, nineteen-year repository (2026-08-06): `matomo-org/matomo` (facts as in FIELD-1) from `drift 17 - 37% adopted (10/27)` to `self-verify: OK - drift 0 - 98% adopted (57/58), 1 excepted`. Guard timings best of three: self-verify 218 ms, spec-structure 64, facts-check 40, schema-pair 40, cycle-guard 42, decision-records-check 43, `spec-guard --audit` 76, diff mode 113; byte-identical output across runs. Nine more findings with reproductions: `schema-pair` skips when `database/schema/` is absent and no manifest entry requires it (30 `CREATE TABLE` statements at drift 0 with R24 unmet); `spec-guard --audit` reports unclaimed as 20 alphabetical paths with map defects conflated (150 of 296 files in `plugins/CoreHome`); the `.gitattributes` entry passes on `eol=crlf` for `*.sh`/`*.mjs`; a `content` exception on a merge-class entry can never apply; the percentage rises 37% -> 82% from copying shipped files; the three CI-landing options cover self-verify only while the audit step blocks; copy-class `.nvmrc` contradicts `engines`; no intake step for a substantive `AGENTS.md` whose rules live elsewhere; the shipped ADR index shows no example row format. Cases `ADOPT-11`-`ADOPT-15`, `GATE-33`-`GATE-36`, `SHAPE-16`, `DOC-19`, plus `GATE-37` (guard cost); 16 observations in `runs/2026-08-06-s-field3.json`; none patched here (design decisions or guard-output changes).
- The work-cycle guard checked the id and trusted everything else (2026-08-06): a `split:<id>` row was not read as finished (still reported `1 live block(s)`; the reference is checked too); the one-place invariant was keyed on the id alone (title now compared, markup stripped, across files; the `split:<id>` row and its remainder exempt); a reference was uppercased before comparison (`blocked:ADR-auth` reported `ADR-AUTH exists nowhere`); pool in-flight pointers unchecked (the `Cycle` cell must name an existing open cycle file; a non-empty `Items` cell must match the cycle's row count; an open cycle nothing points at is caught; a `done` row lingering is deliberately left - the template licenses it). `tools/cycle-guard-test.mjs` 37 -> 65 cases.
- A backlog row carries where it came from, under one id convention (2026-08-06): a `source` column with five categories (`onboarding`, `spec-delta`, `drift`, `decision`, `asked`), required by the Definition of Ready; one id convention (the prefix names what the item belongs to - the capability where there is one, the artifact type otherwise); `Maintainer (internal)` declared for internal work; a blocked row stays in `doing` carrying the id it waits on; the cycle commit count is scoped to each cycle's `assignee` set (nine-commit two-team history: 7 and 6 -> 5 and 4), reporting an assignee that maps to no git author and a zero-commit window.
- A fixed finding could leave the ledger from both sides at once (2026-08-06): a finding counted as fixed only with a `fix` URL; folding two branches' run files: 167 became 164. `fix` is now provenance; a pass whose case+target ever recorded a fail is a fix; the PR count reports only fixes citing one; the fixed table prints the re-verifying run file. `tools/validation-test.mjs` (new `--root`) runs beside `--check`; reverting the rule turns 6 of 9 cases red.
- Nine rules assumed a repo shape (2026-08-06): the integration rule binds to the reviewed change (mailing list, Gerrit); a merge queue batching changes is neither rebase nor squash - reserved for ADR-026's own record; the schema rule says the directory and typed twin do not apply to a migrations library, whose emitted DDL is an interface contract specified per backend; the runtime cost names Node, `jq` and a POSIX shell, with what a repo does when it cannot supply them (prerequisites page gains the platform half); the security baseline gains an adversarial-testing axis ("fuzz" returned nothing); three catalogues gain shapes (one structure described twice - Bazel beside CMake, a schema hand-forked into a second dialect, generate-or-declare paved road; the buildable tier for a content product; intake reads governance config `.jcheck/conf`, `.gitreview`, `CODEOWNERS`); `spec-reconcile` records a compatibility shim as what it preserves and when it can go; `facts-check` refuses a binary file by name (font TTF name table gave a coin-flip).
- The drift number answered two different questions with the same integer (2026-08-06): (a) a missing tool scored as drift (`pnpm check:all` with no `pnpm` printed `drift 1 - 99% adopted (78/79)`, string-identical to three real lint errors); a guard whose prerequisites are absent is not run - `SKIP`, counted as neither, named in the verdict; `OK` dropped whenever a check did not run; prerequisites from the guard entry's `requires` (`{ "kind": "command"|"path", "match": ..., "hint": ... }`) or inferred from any bare word in `run` that is no builtin and resolves nowhere on `PATH`; the `path` kind avoids side effects (running pulls the dependency tree off the network); inference errs toward running (quoted text blanked; `a || b` read as fallback; the executing `node` never probed). (b) `drift 0` read as "the method has been used here": a raw greenfield tree plus `.standards-version`, a `profile` key, an empty `specs/capability-map.json` reached `OK - drift 0 - 100% adopted (69/69)`; the drift-0 line now carries the caveat; the absence is reported, never scored (greenfield scaffolds in step 1 and specifies in step 6); clears when a real `specs/<capability>/spec.md` exists (not `plan.md`/`tasks.md`). Ten cases; the summary line's "20 cases" had been stale at 22; count computed.
- The one number a cycle publishes could not be reproduced (2026-08-06): `git log --oneline --since=<opened> --until=<closed> | wc -l` with bare dates resolves as approxidates; the showcase's three closed cycles gave 3/2/1 at 19:24, 3/2/2 at midnight, 2/1/1 at midday; the inclusive window returns 4/3/3. History: first claimed 63/51/88 on a 34-commit repo; a correction to 9/6/3 was also wrong; the prescription writes the times out and the spec records why the window is the two boundary days whole. The showcase's 19 oldest commits carry whole-minute authored timestamps.
- Thirteen version bumps had no changelog entry (2026-08-06): `tools/tree-check.mjs` fails when `VERSION` names a version with no matching `## <version>` heading. The gap recorded, not backfilled (details under the gap heading below); the 0.8.13 note ("twelve version bumps", 0.8.1-0.8.12) counted a version that never existed and missed two that did.
- R23 forbade the ordinary security backport (2026-08-06, ADR-035): confirmed on odoo (98 branches, four supported lines), openssl (five `REL` branches, per-PR backport matrix), dotnet/runtime, FFmpeg, PowerShell. A PR is based on the branch it will merge into - the mainline or a maintained release line (long-lived, protected, declared supported with the branching decision, never rewritten); another open PR's branch is never a legal base; a fix touching more than one line lands on the mainline first and reaches each line as its own PR; R18 gains one changelog per line, each with its own `## Unreleased`. Five rejected shapes incl. a new numbered rule; machine-checkable support set rejected ("a branch existing is not a branch supported").
- A CI workflow that exists is not a gate that fires (2026-08-06): postgres and openjdk run real CI elsewhere; LibreOffice/core's only PR-triggered workflow auto-closes every PR; a `paths:` filter excluding everything; a self-hosted `runs-on` label with nothing behind it. Pass 5 rates what fires: a recent run on a pull request (`gh run list --workflow <file> --event pull_request` or the checks on recent merged PRs); a workflow nobody can show running rates `partial` at best; CI outside the host is still CI. Self-verify's limit stated: a `paths:` filter matching nothing still reports `drift 0 - 100% adopted (70/70)`.
- The intake looked for a contribution policy everywhere except `AGENTS.md` (2026-08-06): `sqlite/sqlite` ("SQLite does not accept agentic code"), `alibaba/arthas` (Chinese `AGENTS.md` forbidding CI and ordering security design deleted), `opentofu/opentofu` (LLM-contribution ban) - all re-read live; `self-verify` prints `PASS file AGENTS.md` on a directory whose only file refuses agentic contributions. The policy read names where to look, reads content in whatever language, branches three ways: a ban (a ban on contributing is not a ban on reading - OpenTofu invites LLM-found issues, SQLite takes agentic bug reports with a repro - so assessment-only is offered); hostile instructions (arthas) are evidence, never orders; a policy mandating what the standard forbids (`JuliaLang/julia` requires the AI tool as co-author and a disclosure on every PR vs `docs/conventions.md`'s ban) stops and puts the conflict to the human (no gate reads what a convention says). The merge step reads the target's `AGENTS.md` before writing over it.
- Three severe findings re-run (2026-08-06): `SEC-02` - the standard had no word for work that is real, recorded and not yet publishable (R3, R11, R15 followed literally in a public repo publish an unfixed vulnerability; no embargo exception kind among `file`, `section`, `content`, `key`). ADR-034: R3 conflated the repo with the public; material whose publication is itself the harm may be held in a private mirror (the platform's private advisory fork is the paved road) and MUST rejoin the mainline when the embargo lifts; every rule binds inside the mirror (only R3 moved; R11/R15 need no exception); an embargo names its lifting condition and owner; near misses named ("not written up yet", "it is in the chat", work someone would rather not publish are not embargoes); rejected: an "unless confidential" clause per rule, an `embargo` exceptions kind; `security-baseline.md` gains the axis. `DEC-08` - `spec-impact` step 3 said `ADRs` only (a change forbidden by Accepted `BDR-001`'s `What this rules out` reported no impact); now names both streams and that section, with `contradicts` as its own outcome. `LOOP-03` did not reproduce (`discovery-digest` gained the `Revisit when` reader the evening the finding was logged); `dev-work.md` now points at the reader and states the limit.
- The work state had no surface for the people who most need to read it (2026-08-06): `scripts/work-dashboard.mjs` ships with stylesheet and client: one static page (in flight, cycles against a calendar with today and the measured projection, the pool ranked, a kanban per cycle, four reports, the changelog as history, every record/spec/idea/open question behind one search); a repo with no cycles gets no cycle views and is told why. Three properties: it is a projection (writes nothing; same commit -> same bytes; fingerprint over content); it notices staleness (polls a `state.json` beside it, reloads restoring the reader's place, waits with a prompt when a record is open or a search half-typed; nothing fetches/pulls/rebases; `--watch` and `--serve`); publication is gated on who may read the repository (Pages on a private repo is served publicly unless Enterprise Cloud; a JavaScript prompt is theatre): `--lock` encrypts the page at build time (AES-256-GCM, key stretched with 600,000 rounds of PBKDF2), the workflow's publish step opens for a private repository exactly when the `WORK_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` secret is set; the locked page names nothing (title "Work"); one shared secret, revoked by rebuilding, attackable offline - all stated beside the hosted identity gates. `--anonymise` drops assignees and the cycle owner (structured fields only); `--serve` binds loopback on port 9675. The generated page is not committed (published at `/work/`). Ships under `$unclaimed` with a backlog row for its spec.
- The spec loop reads the decision log before it writes (2026-08-06, ADR-033): `SPEC-18` - neither `/spec-specify` nor `/spec-clarify` read `docs/decision-records/` (they read `docs/discovery/`, non-normative per ADR-024); `AGENTS.md`'s red-flag list named ADRs not BDRs. `SPEC-17` - retirement awareness reached only `/spec-impact`, `/spec-update`, `discovery-digest`; `/spec-specify` contained "retired" zero times. Now both skills read the decision-record index before drafting or asking, opening in full only overlapping records; an Accepted record outranks the dossier, the draft and the user's answer; a contradiction stops the run with two routes (change the request, supersede the record); `/spec-clarify` can refuse an answer. `/spec-specify` matches capabilities on subject (`shift-reminders` vs `shift-notifications`), reads the matched spec's `Status`, stops on `retired`. R6 states the binding half; the template says what retirement means; `AGENTS.md`'s red flag and the constitution template's hard stop name both streams. Dry-run against `test-greenfield-core` broke the draft twice (the fixture's BDR index omits the governing record, so the bound rests on `decision-records-check`; a retirement clause forbade what the retiring record called for).
- The landing showed two versions at once (2026-08-06): `site/index.html` stated the version five times (pill `v0.8.13`; disclosure, footer, two hero-script lines `0.8.12`); `site-check` asserted the current version appears somewhere; `facts.json` declared only the pill's pattern. `site-check` now compares every version-shaped string on the landing against `VERSION` (line and string reported); `<svg>` bodies masked (path data reads as versions: thirteen from the GitHub mark, seventeen across the page) rather than deleted; comments and stylesheets stay in scope; `site/previous.html` (v0.7.2) outside the gate. No new `facts.json` patterns (declaring them would block legitimate rewording). `tools/site-check-test.mjs` (six fixture cases; reverting the scan turns five red); a self-closing `<svg/>` mask defect found by this change's own review. Validation record `runs/2026-08-06-i-doc12.json` against `repo:repository-standards/core` (the `self` target); the fixture row retired as `not-applicable`.
- The persona gate never opened the roster (2026-08-06): a three-way OR whose first arm (`**Serves:**` holding any non-placeholder string) decided every case; "for whom" in prose passed; emptying the roster changed no verdict. The roster is now the constraint: a filled `**Serves:**` must name somebody on it (whole value read, continuation lines included); the failure quotes claim and roster; prose naming stays a hatch; "for whom" alone no longer counts; an unreadable roster is reported (names the expected row shape); hatches kept (no personas.md and no specs; templates/READMEs; unfilled `<persona ...>` placeholder = serving nobody). 16 cases; `docs/tree/docs-personas-md.md` shows the parseable form.
- The skills had no catalogue (2026-08-06): `docs/skill-map.md`, generated by `tools/skill-map.mjs` from each skill's frontmatter verbatim, grouped by the moment a skill fires: deciding what to build; specifying a capability; planning and doing the work; closing the work; running the cadence; staying on the standard; the transition procedure listed apart. Refuses to render when a shipped skill belongs to no group (verified with a throwaway skill); `--check` in CI. (`tools/README.md` had drifted twice: four listed while seven existed, then ten while eighteen.)
- The update delta was read off the manifest, which cannot see most of a release (2026-08-06): between 0.8.12 and 0.8.13 the manifest diff reports no file added/removed and 50 entries changed only on `since`/`purpose`, while the release moved 4 payload files, 47 insertions, 22 deletions; only `copy` entries carry `sha256` (37 of 82 shipped files have no content recorded). Step 2 names the file diff between the two versions' shipped trees as the delta; the manifest diff indexes entries; the changelog carries prose; a partial delta is reported as partial. A sham update is caught today (built on the released 0.8.13 payload: `drift 10 - 87% adopted (68/78)`); the twentieth drift-test case builds the target manifest with `tools/manifest-hashes.mjs` from a real newer tree; an exception plus drift 0 works (`drift 0 - 99% adopted (76/77), 1 excepted`) only in the tree.

- A fixed defect now reads as fixed - 76 observations re-verified against the live tree, not relabelled (2026-08-05): the suite's convention ("a fixed defect keeps its fail verdict and gains a `fix` field") made `fail` stop meaning "broken today". Every `fail` with a non-empty `fix` in `runs/2026-08-03.json` and `runs/2026-08-04.json` (76) re-run against the merged tree by live script execution (`spec-guard.mjs`, `self-verify.mjs`, `cycle-guard.mjs`, `check-spec-clarified.sh`, `spec-structure.mjs`, `decision-records-check.mjs`, `setup-plan.sh`/`setup-tasks.sh`, `pnpm import` against synthetic bun and yarn-berry lockfiles). 74 flipped to `pass` keeping `fix` as provenance; two stayed `fail`: `DOC-01` (`scripts/lib`'s entry declares `since: "0.8.14"` while `VERSION` is 0.8.13) and `TRACK-10` (the PR added `split:<id>` to the template but never touched `cycle-guard.mjs`'s staleness check). `tools/validation.mjs`: the punch list filters on raw `verdict` (a fail still carrying a `fix` reads "attempted, still open"); a "Fixed and re-verified this round" section (78 = 74 + 4 pre-existing); `--check` requires non-empty `evidence`. Counts regenerated: 78 fixed across 18 merged PRs, 84 open, 2 attempted-not-held.
- Five spec-engine files carried no provenance marker (2026-08-05): `specs/spec-engine/spec.md`'s Provenance duty requires every file under `scripts/spec/` or a `spec-*` skill to name `github/spec-kit v0.13.2` or carry `PATCHED(repository-standards)`; `setup-plan.sh`, `setup-tasks.sh`, `common.sh`, `check-prerequisites.sh`, `tasks-template.md` carried neither (provenance read off git history: the first two are upstream scripts patched with the ADR-010 clarify-gate call; `common.sh`/`check-prerequisites.sh` carry older ADR-014/ADR-015 extraction patches; `tasks-template.md` lacked a marker for its "user story" -> "requirement slice" rename); `spec-impact`, `spec-reconcile`, `spec-update` (standard-authored) got markers. `tools/provenance-check.mjs` fails any spec-engine file carrying neither; wired into `checks.yml`.
- The clarify gate's bridge precondition was prose, not code, and its policy doc did not exist (2026-08-05): `enforcement.md` described a "before_plan / before_tasks hook plus a bridge precondition", but neither `setup-plan.sh` nor `setup-tasks.sh` called `check-spec-clarified.sh` (only the skills' prompts documented a "MANDATORY PRECHECK"). Both scripts now call the gate on `FEATURE_SPEC` and exit 1, re-checked every run (a spec can regain open markers after planning); `specs/spec-engine/spec.md` gets the invariant, exit codes, acceptance criteria; `tools/clarify-gate-test.mjs` gets five cases driving the real scripts. `standard/specs/README.md` was cited by ADR-002, ADR-003, ADR-004, `docs/method/taxonomy.md`, the greenfield skill, this repo's own `specs/README.md` and the case study - never created (citations dead or quietly re-pointed at `docs/tree/specs.md`); written now (structure, spec depth, coupling, loop layers). "Hook" reworded: the two real layers are the skill prompt's precheck and the scripts' bridge precondition (not `.claude/hooks/`); a `UserPromptSubmit` nag hook considered and rejected (cannot judge whether a skill covers a request; that belongs to loaded context via `@AGENTS.md`).
- A published validation suite - real cases, run against real repositories (2026-08-04): `docs/validation/`: `suite.json` (174 cases), `targets.json` (105 targets: 103 real public repositories + 2 synthetic fixtures), `runs/` (176 observations across the 2026-08-03 and 2026-08-04 rounds); every fixed failure names the merged PR (body checked against the finding); every open failure carries an explicit waiver. `tools/validation.mjs` renders `README.md` (headline numbers, coverage by area and rule incl. zero-case rules, failures with fix) and `benchmark.md` (the `portable: true` subset); `--check` fails on a stale render, an observation pointing at an unknown case/target, a case with no verdict, a fail with neither fix nor waiver; wired into `checks.yml`. Both pages state what is not proved: assessment is not adoption (103 of 105 at L1); both fixtures share an author; agent-executed observations carry agent error, published with disconfirmed hypotheses (a "maintenance mode" misread on two repos; a version-string red herring; the showcase's unchecked commit counts). Five headline numbers registered in `docs/facts.json` via generated `docs/validation/counts.json`. `FIELD-1`/`EXHIBIT-1` updated (a large L1 evidence base; a real L3/L4 adoption of a repo nobody here wrote still missing).
