- A stack's own recorded deviation had nowhere that read it (2026-08-04): `self-verify.mjs` folded a stack manifest's `files`, `sections`, `guards` but never its `exceptions` (where the stack's `ADAPTING.md` tells adopters to record one); now merged with the other three arrays.
- Four skill descriptions led with the mechanism, not the trigger (2026-08-04): blind-routing 20 realistic utterances produced 7 disagreements with the routing docs, always "the description does not fire on the request that needs it". `spec-update`, `spec-tasks`, `spec-impact`, `add-to-backlog` rewritten to quote a concrete trigger phrase; `timeline-update` already quoted four and is left; `spec-clarify` rewritten instead (zero quoted phrases, purely procedural). `specs/spec-engine/spec.md` gains an acceptance criterion.
- Six method docs were never delivered to an adopting repo (2026-08-04): `docs/method/agent-work.md`, `dev-work.md`, `lead-work.md`, `product-work.md`, `tracking-work.md`, `working-language.md` are adopter-normative (by reference, ADR-023) but absent from `standard.manifest.json`'s `references[]`; added (fourteen existing); `docs/file-map.md` regenerated (also fixing a stale `docs/facts.example.json` entry).
- Removing a required skill or the unprompted-behaviour section changed no drift number (2026-08-04): `.claude/skills` was checked as a bare directory; the `AGENTS.md` heading that makes the loop self-trigger had no `sections` entry. `self-verify` now checks each name in the entry's `sha256` map exists inside the resolved path (`path` or matching `altPath`) - a missing skill is its own FAIL; two new `sections` entries (unprompted-behaviour heading; the volunteer section); `tools/self-verify-fill-test.mjs` cases; `specs/verify-engine/spec.md` documents it.
- A bug mentioned in passing reached no skill and no row (2026-08-04): "btw the export is broken" matched nothing (no description mentions a bug; `taxonomy.md`/`checklist.md` had no row); both name the case, routing to `add-to-backlog` (fixing it right now is just the change); `add-to-backlog`'s description quotes the phrase.
- Checking whether a skill fits never covered an ambiguous mention (2026-08-04): new `AGENTS.md` section "Volunteer, don't wait to be asked": name the candidate skill and ask, rather than silently doing the extra work or silently filing nothing.
- `CLAUDE.md` pointed at the unprompted-behaviour rule instead of loading it (2026-08-04): a markdown link an agent can leave unread; now imports with `@AGENTS.md`.
- A cycle-boundary split had no status value, and cold-start timelines promised a number ADR-029 forbids (2026-08-04): `split:<id>` added to the template's vocabulary (matching `blocked:<id>`); `cycle-close` writes it. `timeline-update`'s cold-start said "project from sizes" and "label the result an estimate" (reads as a fabricated date, contradicting ADR-029 and `standard/docs/backlog.md`); reworded: a cold start may produce a size-based shape ("heavier than usual"), never a duration or date; both cold-start branches give no date (matching passages in `tracking-work.md`, `docs-cycles.md`).
- `cycle-open` and `cycle-close` assumed the backlog's primary path (2026-08-04): both resolve `docs/backlog.md` first, then `backlog.md`, as the guard does.
- A column prepended before `id` disarmed `cycle-guard` (2026-08-04): id column resolved from the header row's `id` label, falling back to column 0; test case added.
- `cycle-guard` checked for too many places, never for zero (2026-08-04): reads each closed cycle's `## Outcome` block for a `Returned to the pool: <ids>` line and fails naming any id not in the backlog; template and `cycle-close` say to name the ids.
- Four of the six things `tracking-work.md` promised had no owning skill (2026-08-04): the rendered 3-lane board, moving a status mid-cycle, reassigning a holder had no skill; `cycle-open` now documents reading an open cycle back as a board and mid-flight edits as plain table edits followed by the guard; the fourth ("what did we write down during onboarding that nobody picked up") is a direct read; the honest limit named: no per-row `source` field (fixed 2026-08-06).
- Every run in every adopted repo printed a path that does not exist there (2026-08-04): `self-verify.mjs` ended "see docs/self-verify.md" (the page is `docs/method/self-verify.md`, by reference); third instance of the class, so the tree was swept: `lib.sh`'s `jq`-missing denial pointed at `docs/prerequisites.md`; `docs/facts.example.json` used the same path as an example claim; R16 cited `docs/method/prerequisites.md` as if in the reader's repo - all now the by-reference form. The placeholder scan catches a table row whose cells are all ellipses (`| ... | ... |`); a row of empty cells is deliberately not flagged.
- A deleted script was still promised in two places (2026-08-04): `scripts/changelog.mjs` (gone with the per-PR fragments folder two months earlier) still described in the method page and the shipped `README.md`; both corrected; not coming back ("promoting one prose `## Unreleased` section is a copy, a heading and a date").
- `self-verify` never looked at what a file contained (2026-08-04): three testers reproduced `drift 0 - 100% adopted` with 19 of 20 skills and the previous `SPEC.md`; the showcase reported `49/49` missing a skill and carrying 12 drifted; with the stack layer, a pnpm supply-chain policy block deleted (`minimumReleaseAge`, `saveExact`, `enablePrePostScripts`) passed on filename; bumping version and manifest with no file changes reported compliant. Now every `copy` entry carries a `sha256` (one per file; one per member for a directory), generated by `tools/manifest-hashes.mjs` and asserted by `tree-check`; `self-verify` hashes the local file and reports a difference as drift (worded as content difference); no network, no shipped tree needed. A deliberately changed `copy` file is what `exceptions` are for (`.nvmrc` the worked example). A `merge` entry may declare `requiredKeys` (dotted paths that must survive, JSON and YAML, presence only) - how the stack asserts its three supply-chain keys. An `altPath` satisfied by any directory (a monorepo symlinked `.claude/skills` to an unrelated 31-skill system and reported 100%): a ported directory is checked by name (bytes cannot be, R22). Existence decided by directory listing (case-exact; `existsSync` is case-insensitive on macOS/Windows: `readme.md` passed on a Mac, failed on Linux CI).
- The exception hatch was unbounded, and excepting entries raised the score (2026-08-04): thirteen `kind: "file"` exceptions took a tree with no `AGENTS.md`, no personas, no capability map and all five guard scripts deleted to `self-verify: OK - drift 0 - 100% adopted (32/32)`: excepting a guard's script disabled a live check, and an excepted entry left the denominator (49 -> 32, so the percentage rose). Now: a guard's own script cannot be excepted by `kind: "file"` (an edited guard uses `kind: "content"`, which keeps the guard running); deliberately no `guard` kind (the docstring advertised one the loop never consulted); an exception with no `reason` is refused and each refusal is drift; an excepted entry stays in the denominator and does not count as adopted (excepting can only lower the percentage); the count always prints (including zero); a no-longer-needed exception is reported stale. The method page documents the hatch and withdraws its claim that comparing two repos' numbers is sound (each repo's denominator is its own manifest, version, profile, stack, exceptions). A `content` match may end in `/**` to scope a subtree (only `content` - a presence waiver on `scripts/**` would sweep every guard). Also: the built-in fallback skeleton (unaligned repo printed `drift 4-5` where the manifest gives `13-15`) is named in the warning and verdict; `facts-check.mjs` compiled a fact's `home` pattern without the multiline flag; `docs/file-map.md` regenerated.
- Nothing authored the capability map, and the example described a rejected layout (2026-08-04): `capability-map` appeared seven times in brownfield onboarding and zero in greenfield or the router (`spec-guard --audit` blocks once the first spec exists). Greenfield writes it during scaffolding, holding no capabilities and no `$unclaimed` (the audit says the unclaimed check is off); two writers only: `spec-specify` registers the capability when it mints the directory; `spec-reconcile` reconciles the map before the PR (catches a refactor). The shipped example used root-level `**/payment/**` and "app / service / shared" prose while the paved monorepo shape is `apps/*` and `packages/*` (`services/` turned down); rewritten with `couples: "shape"` kept and a `$unclaimed` starting list; a single-package repo writes `src/**/payment/**`.
- Translating a heading switched the persona check off (2026-08-04): `spec-structure` reads the roster from `docs/personas.md`'s `## The roster`; an absent heading fell back to a whole-file scan (widening the roster); now an absent required heading is a failure naming the heading.
- The placeholder warning was both too loose and too tight (2026-08-04): `<picture>`, `<source>`, `<code>`, `<kbd>` in finished READMEs tripped it (four foreign repos); ASCII-only, so `<角色名>` and `<нужно заполнить>` were invisible; now the two shipped shapes, Unicode-aware, with single-word angle tokens naming an HTML element excluded.
- A spec's Status was decorative (2026-08-04): nothing read or wrote `**Status:**`; the structure lint re-runs the real gate on every spec claiming `ready-to-develop` or `live` and fails the PR when refused (a gate that cannot run counts as unproven); `spec-reconcile` owns writing the field. Found four instances in this repo's own specs: four `live` capabilities with no `## Clarifications`, one missing `## Open questions`, one carrying an upstream-sync note in open questions; specs now say they were retrofitted from the implementation.
- The normative page miscounted its own rules (2026-08-04): `SPEC.md` said "Rules are numbered R1-R24" while defining R25; the rule count is declared as a fact with its home a count of rule definitions; fourth home form `countMatches` (how many times one file declares something; `count` counts files, `match` captures one occurrence).
- `cycle-guard` read zero rows and reported OK (2026-08-04): scoping to a literal `## Intents` H2 is right (a closed cycle's `## Outcome` reuses ids) but a file with `### Intents`, `## Work` or no heading yielded zero rows = indistinguishable from clean; ids in backticks or bold invisible. Both fail closed: no `## Intents` heading is an error; rows under it with no id error (header, underline, template blank row excepted); `` `PAY-2` `` is `PAY-2`. The folder manual documented an unreadable shape; rewritten; the test runs the guard over the page's own example.
- The clarify gate never read the template's own open-questions section (2026-08-04): `## Open questions` (required, free prose) was ignored; four shapes reproduced passing (prose markers, a statement-phrased question, a table, an item answered under Clarifications but still listed). Rule: the section passes only when it says there are none ("None known."); HTML comments and fences stripped first; any other visible line is an open item; a missing or translated heading fails. One home per thing: an unresolved gap is a typed marker in the section it affects; a settled note goes into the section it describes; a known non-blocking gap goes to the backlog with a link; `spec-specify` no longer records assumed defaults there (a default taken is an answer under `## Clarifications`).
- A spec whose open markers were translated passed the clarify gate (2026-08-04): the gate grepped one literal ASCII string; a Chinese spec with four unresolved items printed `ready-to-develop`; the missing-`## Clarifications` error names the English string, inviting translation. The call (working-language guide, conventions table, spec template, `spec-clarify`): the four marker forms and the headings the gate reads are syntax (ASCII in any language); the text inside a marker is prose in the spec's language. The gate enforces it: a bracketed token shaped like a typed marker but not one of the four fails; markdown links, checkboxes and footnotes are not markers. "The clarify gate had no test at all until now, and it is the mechanism with the worst history here."
- The coupling guard could not match a capability at the top level (2026-08-04): both glob-reading guards translated `**` into `.*` without consuming the slash, so `**/payment/**` needed an extra directory and `shared/**/payment*` missed `shared/payment.ts` - the first two entries of the shipped example map. One translator now (`scripts/lib/glob.mjs`); `**` matches zero segments as well as many. `--audit` checks four silent things: a spec with no map entry; a map entry naming a capability with no spec; a glob matching no file; code belonging to no capability (bounded by a declared `$unclaimed` list; where none, the check reports it is off); a retired capability's empty globs exempt.
- `AGENTS.md`'s "Checks before any PR" list had drifted from `checks.yml` (2026-08-04): `prose-check.mjs` (+ `--self`), `self-verify-fill-test.mjs`, `site-behaviour.mjs`, then `clarify-gate-test.mjs` added.
- Two of four documented backlog feeders wrote nothing (2026-08-04): `spec-impact` and `spec-update` never mentioned the backlog; both file via `add-to-backlog` when real work the current change will not address surfaces; `add-to-backlog`'s automatic triggers grow two -> three.
- `Revisit when` had two writers and no reader (2026-08-04): `discovery-digest` is the reader - new material checked against every record's `Revisit when` (grep-able text vs each record's `Status`/`Revisit when`, not semantic), hits recorded in the dossier and the readiness report; limits stated.
- `CHANGELOG.md` was normative (R18/R25) but absent from the manifest (2026-08-04): `standard/CHANGELOG.md` ships a skeleton (title, semver blurb, empty `## Unreleased`); required `files` and `sections` entries; `docs/CHANGELOG.md` accepted as altPath (as `backlog.md`/`docs/backlog.md`); `docs/tree/changelog-md.md` written.
- `spec-reconcile` never reconciled decision records (2026-08-04): new step: for each spec in scope, check every ADR/BDR it names (prose and code comments) against the record's current `Status`; a citation to a superseded record is repointed and the prose flagged for a human; advisory, grep-able.
- `pre-pr-review` did not run the gate CI actually blocks on (2026-08-04): step 3 named `spec-guard.mjs --audit` without `--block` and no `--base`; now names both invocations with flags (`--base origin/main --block` and `--audit --block`); core's workflow only blocks on the audit - run both locally anyway.
- Record numbering had no source of truth (2026-08-04): following `bdr-write` minted a second `BDR-004`; an Accepted `BDR-004` was missing from the index. `adr-write`/`bdr-write` find the next free number by reading the directory and re-check before committing; new guard `scripts/decision-records-check.mjs` (wired into self-verify and this repo's checks) fails on a duplicate id, a file with no index row, an index row with no file; layout-agnostic (shipped `adr/` + `bdr/` split and this repo's flat directory).
- An idea had nowhere to land (2026-08-03): new skill `idea-write` captures an idea end-to-end without minting a record and drives `idea -> exploring -> approved | parked | dropped -> graduated`, handing off to a backlog intent, spec and records on approval; R14 names `graduated`.
- `spec-plan`, `spec-tasks`, `spec-implement` could silently act on a stale feature (2026-08-03): `check-prerequisites.sh` resolves the active feature from `specs/feature.json` or an env var; all three confirm the resolved feature matches what was asked (`spec-implement` highest-stakes).
- Five smaller gaps from wave 5's repo sampling (2026-08-03): the lifecycle-signal check reads for a move/mirror statement (`archived: false` but "Moved to Codeberg"); the no-AI-agents stop gains a conditional middle case (agent involvement allowed if a human authors every artifact); archetype guidance for a single-package micro-library (one header, one build target); the licensing row covers an open-core repo split by directory into two licenses; entries still saying 19 skills after `idea-write` made it 20.
- `SPEC.md` itself never said Layer 1's guards need a Node runtime (2026-08-03): found by three of four repos on uncovered languages (Swift, Elixir, C#); R16 states: shipped guards are dependency-free Node scripts regardless of stack, "a real cost for a non-Node repo, not a rounding error".
- A capability's code could be deleted without ever touching its spec (2026-08-03): `spec-guard.mjs` used `--diff-filter=ACMR` (excludes deletions); `D` added. Paired: the spec template gains terminal `Status: retired` (flip status, link the retiring BDR, leave the `capability-map.json` entry so `--audit` does not misread an orphan); the backlog template gains an escape hatch for an item whose target retired before its DoD could be met.
- Two sources landing in the same handover were only diffed against the dossier (2026-08-03): each source is its own dated entry, filed and diffed in order, including against entries the same handover just added.
- The tracker list, and one onboarding paragraph link, went stale (2026-08-03): R15 and the intake tracker question named three brands; GitLab Issues and mailing-list-plus-Bugzilla are real; reworded open-ended. `AGENTS.md` linked `docs/method/self-verify.md` under visible text `standard/docs/self-verify.md` (nonexistent); fixed.
- The clarify gate could be satisfied by a spec whose open items were never in bracket form (2026-08-03): `check-spec-clarified.sh` counted only `[NEEDS `; a numbered list in the marker family's names (`- **CLARIFICATION-1 (owner: ...).**`) was invisible; adding an empty `## Clarifications` flipped FAIL to PASS; the gate now catches that unbracketed shape, naming what to rewrite.
- A recorded manifest exception had no mechanical effect (2026-08-03): R17 and `align-to-standards` described recording a deviation as an `exceptions` entry; `self-verify.mjs` never read the array; now checks each required `files`/`sections` failure against `exceptions` first and reports it as excepted.
- Three smaller gaps between what the docs promised and what fires (2026-08-03): `docs/method/taxonomy.md` and `checklist.md` were never linked from the shipped `AGENTS.md`/`conventions.md`; `checklist.md`'s onboarding-flow example prompts say so, and its third names `add-to-backlog` (not an implied `adr-write` for an undecided fork); `prerequisites.md` names `cycle-guard.mjs` and no longer claims local pre-commit scanning is lost without gitleaks (no pre-commit hook ships); `timeline-update`'s three-closed-cycles threshold is per team, never blended.

### 0.8.13 - 2026-08-03

- Ten fixes found by acting as an adopting user/agent through greenfield and brownfield flows on eighteen real repositories (Rust, Go, C, PHP, Python, Ruby, Java/Kotlin, Node/TS; flagship, mid-size, small, deliberately weak or abandoned) and by walking the discovery-to-spec loop and the work-cycles loop end to end, re-verifying each fix concretely.
- The clarify gate could be walked past by following the template's own wording: `capability-spec.template.md`'s Open Questions comment named the marker family (CLARIFICATION, DECISION, INPUT, ASSET) by bare word, never the literal `[NEEDS TYPE: ...]` bracket form the gate greps; the comment now spells it out.
- The node stack's starter pointed at a `DECISIONS.md` that composition strips away: six `../DECISIONS.md` / `../../DECISIONS.md` references (README, Docker test-stack comment, vitest config, Next config, Fastify server, env config) resolved outside the composed repo; now absolute links into `repository-standards/node` with anchors; `config.ts` cited decision #5 (Fastify) for what is #6 (env config).
- Manifest `since` fields, and the same-day discovery that the first fix was wrong: every past bump did a blind string replace across `standard.manifest.json`, corrupting any `since` equal to the version bumped from. Reconstructed from git history twice: the first pass used `git log --follow --reverse`, which returned one commit instead of the rename-aware history and stamped one commit's version across most of `files`; caught in self-review; the second pass covered `guards` and `references` too.
- Five places a doc and the mechanism silently disagreed: `docs/prerequisites.md` named twice as if shipped (never does); the no-stack offer said Layer 1 is "unaffected" for a non-Node repo (false for the guards); `docs/facts.example.json`'s purpose text said to copy it into `docs/facts.json` with placeholder content (fails `facts-check`); `.github/workflows/spec-guard.yml` read as a mandate to use GitHub Actions (two large repos use neither); two `references` entries missing `id`/`since`/`rule`.
- Five escape hatches for real repo shapes: an open-question marker lives in exactly one place (not echoed in both its section and Open Questions); a persona roster of one is legitimate (mirroring the decision catalog's "does not apply"); an existing package/crate boundary can serve directly as a capability map; the churn-hotspot pass says when a shallow clone makes it unrunnable; a repo whose goal is to stay frozen or deprecated is a legitimate intake answer; new decision-catalog category: numerical/semantic compatibility policy (a library whose contract is computed output).
- `cycle-guard` read a correctly-closed cycle's own close table as duplicate intents: `cycle-close`'s `## Outcome` table reuses intent ids by design; row matching scoped to the `## Intents` section (as `spec-structure.mjs` does for the roster).
- Intake now reads a repo's own lifecycle signal before asking: four repos (a neglected tool, an archived library, a defunct product's SDK, a deliberately-sunset repo) stated it in README/CONTRIBUTING; step 1 reads first, step 2 leads with confirming a strong signal; a repo's explicit no-AI-agents policy (`AI_POLICY.md`) is a red-flag stop, same tier as a committed secret; a counting error fixed ("fifth answer" was the sixth); `adoption.md`'s Gate 0 listed four intents after `SKILL.md` grew a fifth.
- The technology step accounts for archetype and multi-stack repos: a registered stack targets an application archetype; offering it wholesale to a 66-package library monorepo, or detecting only a root manifest in a three-stack repo, misfires; the step names the archetype mismatch, offers the pieces that transfer, names every stack. `security-baseline.md` gains a "negative scope" axis (what a repo deliberately does not treat as a vulnerability).
- R18 and R25 directly contradicted each other: R18 (a PR must not bump; maintainer cuts from `## Unreleased`) vs R25 as written (a PR that changes what ships must move the version). No entries between 0.8.0 and 0.8.12 - "twelve version bumps with no record" (corrected later); `scripts/changelog.mjs` described in `docs/method/changelog-process.md` does not exist. R25 now describes what the release does; the missing script and unrecorded versions logged as a backfill/tooling task.

### No entries were written for 0.9.0, 0.9.1 and 0.8.2 - 0.8.12 (recorded 2026-08-06)

- Thirteen commits moved `VERSION` between the 0.8.0 and 0.8.13 releases and not one wrote an entry; nothing backfilled - this heading is the record of the gap and the pointer to where the truth lives.
- From `git log -p -- VERSION`: versions held, in order: 0.9.0, 0.9.1, 0.8.2, 0.8.3, 0.8.4, 0.8.5, 0.8.6, 0.8.7, 0.8.8, 0.8.9, 0.8.10, 0.8.11, 0.8.12 - thirteen bumps, every one on 2026-08-03. `VERSION` never held 0.8.1: two ordinary PRs came out as 0.9.0 and 0.9.1 because R25, on the day it was added, matched its own trigger; `a82c470` walked the number back and wrote 0.8.2 directly (its message names 0.8.1 as what the first should have carried). Unrecorded span `07ad235..4b1179d`: 32 commits; exactly one touched the changelog (`4e038e5`, organization rename inside older entries).
- The 0.8.13 note is wrong in both directions ("twelve version bumps", 0.8.1-0.8.12). The released section is left as cut ("a release is history, not a draft"); this is the correction.
- Nothing reconstructed on purpose: twelve bumps are one commit each (could be transcribed) but 0.9.0 arrived twenty commits after 0.8.0 and splitting nineteen unversioned commits across headings would be a guess "in the one file whose entire job is to be checkable". Range: `git log --oneline 07ad235..4b1179d`.
- Cannot repeat: `tools/tree-check.mjs` fails when `VERSION` names a version with no matching heading.

### 0.8.0 - 2026-08-02

- "The first stable line" (as labelled before the 1.0.0 renumbering): the simplification wave - the standard put on one page, in one tree, with one engine copy - plus everything since 0.7.2: the lifecycle, the guided loop, the align engine, Layer 2 and the product spine. Entries dated 2026-08-02 unless stated.
- Folder READMEs: the ones that teach stay, the ones that restate are gone: five shipped READMEs (`.claude/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.claude/hooks/`, `.github/`, `.github/workflows/`) restated numbered rules (R21 exact pins; R22 partial port is drift; `AGENTS.md`'s live-on-merge note) and nothing linked them; deleted. Two facts lived only there and moved: how a skill's `description` is written (names the situation a user would type, never the artifact - now in `AGENTS.md` after the sentence that a skill failing to fire is a bug in its description); agent guards fail closed and a broken guard is silent (prints only when it refuses; run `scripts/verifyAgentGuards.sh` after any change under `.claude/hooks/` - now a section of `docs/conventions.md`). Folder manuals stay (specs, decision records, cycles, ideas, runbooks, research, journeys, discovery), each now a manifest entry (deleting a manual previously left drift at 0); `docs/discovery/README.md` is the only file holding that folder open in a fresh clone.
- A placement audit that mostly found nothing: `tree-check` already verifies every by-reference path resolves (count 9 -> 12 after the move); no shipped file links outside the tree (checked by resolving links, not grep - `link-check` runs against this repo where an escaping link still resolves); tree and manifest cover each other both ways; `docs/method/` is broader than its name (tool reference and a decision menu) and renaming it is not worth it (every by-reference link in every adopted repo names the path); three moved documents joined the method index.
- The pinning language was hiding in the two files an agent loads first: the tree is clean of mixing (every `copy` entry functional or the spec page itself); four more "pinned version" places on the third sweep: `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, the manifest's `$about`, a usage comment in `self-verify.mjs`; pattern: "a term sweep finds prose and misses the places that are not prose" (JSON description fields, script comments, template opening lines). `SPEC.md` stays copied deliberately (it is what the manifest projects; a local copy states the rules the repo was checked against).
- The shipped tree stopped mixing our documentation with the adopter's: `changes/` is gone (per-PR fragments; the owner's verdict: a change already goes to a concrete place, a second place is a divergence; a changelog conflict resolves in seconds; nothing enforced it; `required` at scale while the practice was unchecked); `scripts/changelog.mjs` went with it; R18 has one path at every profile (describe under `## Unreleased`); ADR-018 keeps its text with a revision. Three of our documents left the adopter's `docs/`: `self-verify.md`, `prerequisites.md`, `security-baseline.md` were `copy`-class and became references (read at their home, always latest); their internal links became plain paths (`.nvmrc`, `scripts/` mean the reader's). `analytics.template.md` -> `analytics.md` (shells named plainly; real templates are `_template.md`). The shipped `docs/` holds only what the adopter authors plus one config shape and one merge-class conventions file; file map 52 -> 47 entries, three references.
- The rendered docs are invisible to agents, and the map was two hops away: `site/docs/` is gitignored (an agent never sees it; "explain it in the docs" is safe only when docs means markdown); `AGENTS.md` links the file map directly (the map answers what is this and why, never what do I put here); a folder `README.md` needs no pointer; `MAP-1` recorded (an adopted repo gets no map; shipping means a freshness check counted as drift).
- Every file explains itself, and the map is generated: orientation (`docs/file-map.md`, generated from `standard.manifest.json` by `tools/file-map.mjs`: every shipped path with purpose, required and at which profile, how it lands, link to the rule; `--check` fails CI on a stale copy) vs the local rule (the folder's own `README.md`, short, must not restate the map). Five folder READMEs written carrying only what a map cannot (hooks fail closed and are silent when broken; skills are matched on a description that must name a situation no other names; workflows pin exact SHAs). Generating the map found four manifest purposes with pinning language. `folder-readmes` revised with the surviving doubt (nothing checks a README stays in its lane). Correction same day: that revision did not land (a failed assertion in a script writing three files stopped after the first); written the next commit, the miss left visible.
- The landing showed agent sentences as shell commands: `$ scaffold from repository-standards` and `$ assess -> align -> onboard` are not commands; the terminal block's convention is `>` for what you say to the agent and `$` only for `node scripts/self-verify.mjs`; the Update card still carried `@next`; neither `@latest` nor `+ node` is real syntax.
- The docs were behind what shipped the same day: the FAQ's tracker answer now names three postures (tracker-bridged, in-repo, both) and what in-repo does not give (previous holders, per-person throughput, burndown, time tracking). Pattern: "the surfaces lag the decisions by hours, and nothing checks that" (nothing compares a FAQ answer to an ADR accepted after it).
- The messaging had no pillar for the thing nobody else does: decision records appeared once in a list; "The why survives" is pillar 2 (the one the field does not have; the pillar that pays back latest); the "Unlike" clause lands on what actually differs ("say what the walk ends at, never that the walk itself is unusual"); "point a repo at a version and it aligns" gone from positioning; README gains the four-things paragraph incl. naming a competitor as the better answer for a spec workflow; one-liner unchanged (`site-check` asserts it verbatim).
- The update channel watches releases; the standard lives on main: the shipped watch compares `.standards-version` against `releases/latest` while the target is always latest and `main` is the living standard (recorded as a question, not patched; watching `main` would fire on a reworded FAQ); five options in `staying-current` incl. the owner's framing (drop the channel and re-run alignment on a cadence); settled by FIELD-1; the watch also still said "pins" (corrected; the known gap is a comment in the workflow).
- Layer 2 ships data and no procedures, and has never once run: the offer is made properly (intake looks the technology up in `stacks.json` first; a miss is called a miss with a consent-gated upstream request; a route exists for adding a stack later). Asymmetry: Layer 1 ships 19 lifecycle skills; a stack ships four data files (`stack.manifest.json`, `DECISIONS.md`, `starter/`, `ADAPTING.md`); `stack.md` is 41 lines vs the core router's 313; a technology's recurring work is covered by nothing. The mechanism for stack skills exists unnamed (`stack.manifest.json` = core schema plus two fields; `.claude/skills` is an ordinary `files[]` entry). `STACK-ALIGN-1` still `todo`; recorded as `stack-lifecycle-skills` / `STACK-LIFE-1` with four questions.
- The five-question cap was manufacturing a false green: upstream's limit of five questions per clarify session is wrong twice: five is not enough for a buildable spec (R9 demands verbatim data contracts, interface contracts, invariants, algorithms, acceptance criteria); the loop's own findings could evaporate (gaps `spec-specify` marked are safe; ambiguities `clarify` discovers in its taxonomy scan are not markers and were reported in chat then gone). Corrected the day it shipped: the first version claimed a half-interrogated spec reached a passing gate outright (overstated; specify's markers hold; found by running the loop against the fixture). The cap is gone; the loop ends when every section the declared tier requires carries a real contract or a typed marker; nothing unresolved may exist only in the conversation (written as a typed marker naming what is missing and who brings it, before the skill returns); questions batched by contract, a stop offered between rounds with a count of what remains and what it blocks; the five-word answer limit gone for contract answers. "the largest single divergence from upstream"; `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` says so.
- There is no version to pin to - the pinning language leaves every surface: ADR-025 (2026-07-30) said the phrasing had left every surface; it had not. `SPEC.md` contradicted itself in two sentences ("a repo complies at the version pinned in its own `.standards-version`"); R2 required pinning - now requires recording the state last aligned to, never a version to stay at or a compatibility requirement. A guard enforced the deleted model (`tree-check` asserted the README quick start contains `@<VERSION>`; removed with the reason in code and spec). The landing taught it (`scaffold from repository-standards@0.7.2`, `--version 0.7.2`, an Update card calling it "the way a dependency bump works"); the version badge and footer stay. Swept: `README.md` (quick start names no version), `AGENTS.md`, `llms.txt`, `docs/faq.md`, `docs/method/adoption.md`, `standard/README.md`, `standard/docs/self-verify.md`, `update-to-version`'s description, the backlog item waiting on a tag. Historical records keep their wording (ADR-004's index row carries its revision). `facts-check` caught surfaces reworded past their declarations; stale restatements deleted, not re-patterned.
- The comparison was wrong about the field, and brownfield is not the difference: measured from the GitHub API: Spec Kit 125k stars, OpenSpec 63k, BMAD 51k, all pushed within two days (the FAQ had dismissed them as "a workflow or a scaffold"). OpenSpec's philosophy is "built for brownfield not just greenfield"; the FAQ says so and that the earlier phrasing got it wrong. Four things none of them does: decisions recorded; an undocumented repo walked into line with a standard; that standard carrying a version the repo pins; compliance as a number CI asserts. ProductSpec named as the nearest neighbour by scope (schema-validated intent format with parser, CLI, Action, MCP server and skills; postdates every design decision). `docs/open-questions/npm-as-a-channel.md` opened: the backlog assumed an npm package and there is no `package.json` (writing one decides by accident: a Node front door on a stack-agnostic standard; a second version number).
- The fill warning could never be cleared, so it was noise: the placeholder scan matched generic notation (`specs/<capability>`, `docs/discovery/<topic>/`, `blocked:<id>`) a correctly filled repo keeps - the shipped `AGENTS.md` carries `specs/<capability>` in its altitude ladder. Convention: angle brackets in prose mean replace me; inside code formatting they are notation; the scan strips code spans and fenced blocks first; `AGENTS.md` states the rule. The tree's genuine marker moved out of backticks. `tools/self-verify-fill-test.mjs` asserts both directions (verified by reverting the fix).
- Cycles get precise enough to manage a project, and say what they refuse to do: ADR-029 measurement is the forecast, sizes only cover the cold start (story points were a proxy calibrated by a stable team's throughput; with an agent the same three-pointer is twenty minutes or two days); `size` is `S`/`M`/`L`, optional, a splitting trigger (an `L` means split before pulling); below three closed cycles the timeline may reason from sizes labelled estimate; at three it switches to measured durations; no blended mode (stops `S`=1, `M`=3, `L`=5 rebuilding the currency). An overrunning item is split, not re-sized. ADR-030: `assignee` names who holds an intent now (ADR-010 narrowed by tense: one current holder on cycle rows, empty in the pool, overwritten on reassignment, archived as written; history stays the tracker's). `blocked` takes a reference (`blocked:PAY-1`); `cycle-guard` fails a block naming an intent that exists nowhere, is the row itself, or is already `done`. The guard runs its block checks without cycles. Status read as the last cell, not a fixed index (asserted with tables of two widths). `cycle-close` and `timeline-update` show their result (a close table; a pasteable timeline summary). Deliberately not built: burndown charts, velocity trends, time tracking, per-person throughput, per-item state history (review rules, not script-enforced).
- Documents get written with someone, not handed to them as a form: four authoring skills ship (`adr-write`, `bdr-write`, `product-write`, `personas-write`); each opens by checking it is the right skill (`adr-write`/`bdr-write` run the overrule test first - an architect means ADR, a product owner means BDR); both draft from the code and `docs/discovery/` before asking. The BDR template stopped being the ADR template with different nouns: gains who it serves, what this rules out, how we would know we were wrong (only in the BDR). `PRODUCT.md` gained "What people do today instead" ("nothing" is valid). The roster's "3-6" is a ceiling, not a quota. Found in self-review: `personas-write` elicited five fields against a template storing seven and described the roster as sections when the gate reads a table - "the same document-to-tooling seam that has produced most defects here".
- The skill ceiling was the wrong constraint, and it was deciding things: the owner challenged the eight-to-ten ceiling; it had no source (the collection cited ships 17); the cost was never measured: all 15 skills' name + description = 4,772 characters, roughly 1,190 tokens, about 0.6% of a 200k window (four more add ~300 tokens). The real failure: `spec-specify` did not fire on "we need refunds" at twelve skills because its description defined the artifact; rewriting one description fixed it with the count unchanged. Constraint = discriminability ("Ten blurred descriptions are worse than twenty sharp ones"); inverts `authoring-skills` to one skill per document type (a routing `record-decision` must match both "we picked Postgres over Mongo yesterday" and "we decided to charge per seat"); surviving doubt: four sibling files can drift; what reopens it is an observation, not a number.
- The cycles work is audited and repaired: sixteen defects every gate passed: the shipped cycle template was HTML-entity-escaped (`&lt;!--`), so its comment was not one - `cycle-open` tells the user to instantiate it at a real path, injecting two example intent ids (the test now instantiates the template as the skill does); the guard hardcoded the backlog's alternate path (`docs/backlog.md` only; manifest primary is `backlog.md`) and passed silently without it (cycles with no backlog now an error); `/timeline-update` wrote `TIMELINE.md` into the directory the guard walks; the comment scanner deleted a real row on an inline comment (`| PAY-7 | fix <!-- was PAY-4 --> the export |`) - fixed; a `-->` in commented prose genuinely ends the comment (asserted, not engineered around); `_`-prefixed directories recursed into and fenced blocks contributed ids; three enumerations listed twelve skills vs fifteen (incl. the shipped `standard/README.md`; `cycle-guard.mjs` missing from its scripts row); the marker-family rule not applied to the four files it names (`spec-tasks`, the entry point, ways-of-working said "zero open `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]`"); a cycle "carries an owner" nowhere in the artifact (Owner row added); the ceiling argument understated by three in four places; the backlog epic intro said "nothing here is built yet" above four done rows and claimed ten test cases vs eighteen; smaller: `standard/README.md` on `CLAUDE.md`; `timeline-update` absent from the coupling map; `docs/README.md` citing an adopted-repo script path; the timeline split cycles by outcome block while `cycle-close` uses `Status`; `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` missing Impact Mapping, Story Mapping, INVEST, jobs-to-be-done, NN/g. One audit finding wrong and recorded (the tree-guard spec lists eight checks; a duplicating fix was reverted).
- Attribution gets a page a person can read: credit was legally sound (MIT text, provenance lines, ADR-013, ADR-015, a guard) and unreadable; `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` keeps three kinds apart; OpenSpec, BMAD, Backstage, adr-tools only in the compared list; says what the spec-kit relationship is not and how far it diverged; borrowed ideas credited (MADR, Conventional Commits, Semver, Keep a Changelog, RFC 2119, Backlog.md compatibility, Linear for the word *cycle*); invites correction.
- The timeline states its evidence, or gives no date: `/timeline-update` regenerates `docs/cycles/TIMELINE.md` whole from closed cycles' outcome blocks; below three closed cycles it refuses to project (below which one unusual cycle dominates the average); reports the spread, not just the mean (0.4/0.4/0.5 supports a date; 0.2/0.4/1.1 does not); unplanned work counts toward throughput; an overrunning open cycle is named with the overrun, never softened or called a failure; refuses to project from open cycles; six acceptance criteria.
- Opening and closing a cycle become procedures: `/cycle-open` moves rows (never copies; ends by running the guard); proposes from the pool's risk x leverage order; pushes back once on a goal that restates the item list; proposes a target date from past cycles. `/cycle-close` cannot rubber-stamp: per intent met / not met / cannot tell from here; unfinished work returns to its position; asks what got done that was never in the cycle. The pool gained an "In flight" section (one line per active cycle). Skill count 12 -> 14 and four prose restatements failed immediately (derived-facts check working); `shipped-skills` records the third growth.
- The cycle artifact ships, and its invariant is checked: `docs/cycles/<team>/<slug>.md` (scale only; owner, goal, agreed end date, intents; several in parallel). One rule mechanical: an intent is in `docs/backlog.md` or in exactly one cycle, never both, never two, including across teams; `scripts/cycle-guard.mjs` reports every violation. The spec came before the code (`specs/work-cycles/`, buildable, mapped). Ten test cases; the template's example rows live inside an HTML comment and the test drives the real template; the test found a bug in itself (advisory violations go to stderr while exiting 0); the guard skips itself without `docs/cycles/`.
- The authoring-skills fork is written up, not decided: four options, one recommended, decision open (against the then-ceiling); the argument for splitting (an ADR wants forces/options/what reopens; a BDR wants who it serves/cost/how we would know; a product frame wants outcome and non-goals; a roster wants jobs, pains, tie-breaks); testable: whether one routing description can fire on both decision sentences; elicitation in templates rejected on evidence (a template cannot ask or notice a user agreeing with the worked example).
- The shipped tree stops citing a document it does not ship: `docs/research/README.md` said "the case-studies rules apply" (`case-studies/` is zone 1 only); the anonymization rule stands alone with its example; a sweep for `case-studies`, `open-questions`, `positioning`, `manifesto`, `ecosystem`, `tools/`, `site/` across the shipped tree returned exactly this one hit.
- The skills say when to reach for them, not what they are: every description was a definition (`spec-specify`: "Create or update a capability spec from a natural-language description" does not match "we need refunds"); descriptions now open with the situation. Five descriptions carried the spec-kit licence line (fifty characters of matching surface; compliance rests on `scripts/spec/LICENSE` and provenance notes). `CLAUDE.md` stopped being a six-line router (the check-whether-a-skill-owns-this instruction lives there). The entry file states three layers: a skill fires on its description; `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` cover what descriptions miss; the coupling guard catches the outcome - only the third is deterministic. "a user should never have to say 'remember to use the skills'. If that becomes necessary, the description is the bug" - with an acceptance criterion using a real user sentence.
- The wizard asks, confirms and suggests - instead of instructing: an audit walked the adoption flow three times as three people, marking every step ASK / CONFIRM / SUGGEST / INSTRUCT / ASSUME: eleven silent decisions, eight questions with no candidates, six dead-ends. Fixes: the flow demanded an unreachable gate ("Do not open the PR on a red self-verify" vs "a brownfield repo never reaches drift 0 in one PR") - greenfield requires drift 0; brownfield closes wave one red by design, states the number, opens the PR. The step whose blast radius is other people (workflows live on merge redden colleagues' PRs) now offers the three real options and never lands them silently; prerequisites checked before the guards (without `jq` the guards deny every command). Intake looks the stack up before promising one; greenfield gains the no-registry branch. Intake asks where else the project writes things down (tracker, wiki, `rfcs/`, tickets) - suggested, paired with "can you reach it, may it be quoted"; what arrives lands in `docs/discovery/` with provenance and is read as a claim about the code (code wins; divergence reported - "a written decision the system stopped honouring is usually the most valuable finding"). Asks where work is tracked (repo, tracker, both bridged), detecting from ticket keys first. Brownfield interviews for personas (code yields roles, not who wins ties); greenfield proposes a roster rather than asking cold (`provisional` is a complete answer with a backlog item). The capability map is played back before it is written. The behavioral tier stops reading as an accusation. The counted backlog is handed over with its antidote (each item names how it gets done; nothing authored from scratch; stopping carries no penalty). Coming back is a first-class intent ("review how we are doing" routes to the assessment, not a version bump). The authentication method left the do-not-ask list (an ADR-grade fork). `pin` and `waves` glossed inside the questions.
- The gate list gets one home: the PR template was the fourth copy and already wrong (nine commands, missing `spec-guard --base ... --block`); it points at `AGENTS.md`. `docs/README.md` described nine of ten things in its folder (missing `facts.json`).
- Work cycles get a decision, not an implementation (ADR-028): a cycle is a goal-bearing, dated grouping of backlog intents at `docs/cycles/<team>/`, several in parallel, ending when its owner says; one intent in the pool or exactly one cycle (mechanically checkable). Reopens something ADR-010 rejected ("everything stays in the repo, including done-work history") on an argument ADR-010 lacked: the grouping is not recoverable afterwards (git counts commits; it cannot reconstruct what a team believed it would finish) - one block per closed cycle is permanent; ADR-010 revised, not superseded. A projection states its confidence or gives no date. Scale only. The capability spec deliberately not written here (graduates via `/spec-specify` - "using the loop on the loop's own features is the only honest way to find out whether it works"). Path: `docs/teams/<team>/cycles/` lost to `docs/cycles/<team>/` (every `docs/` folder is named for the kind of thing inside). Field test dated shortly before the first tag.
- The indexes stop misdescribing their own contents (eight documents): `tools/README.md` listed four of seven and a pre-PR block skipping every shipped guard (list removed; lives in `AGENTS.md`); `CONTRIBUTING.md` parenthesised a wrong shorter list; the zone paragraph said "nine documents" listing eight (dropped the repo assessment); the tree-guard spec stated its check count three ways, all wrong (eight); `standard/docs/README.md` named seven of nine method docs (ships); three version restatements undeclared (eleven agreeing now); `docs/PRINCIPLES.md` shipped as authored content (an adopter inherited a seven-day cooldown and a remote-DB ban as ratified principles; now a template, `self-verify` warns while unfilled); the clarify gate described two ways (script counts the family; `enforcement.md` said CLARIFICATION only); the decision-records doc drew five altitude levels where the rule draws four; `changelog.mjs` cited a nonexistent path; the backlog's "what remains" omitted the missing adoption evidence and satellite alignment, and the three-columns-short exemption is stated; the landing's soft disclosure ("pre-1.0, field-run on the author's production repos") replaced with what README/FAQ/`llms.txt` say.
- The shipped procedures start doing what they say: the spec engine's shell scripts shipped mode `100644` while skills invoke them by path (clarify gate exited 126 on a fresh adoption; `/spec-plan`/`/spec-tasks` STOP on nonzero, reporting a permissions problem as a failed clarification) - now `100755` with an acceptance criterion. The persona gate passed every spec from the template (template carries `**Serves:** <persona from docs/personas.md>`); escape gone. Greenfield told agents to write behavioral specs - now buildable. The scaffolding rule got an owner: `/spec-reconcile` removes plan/task files (what they recorded and is still true moves first: decision to a record, loose thread to the backlog, open question to the spec). "The workflows ship disabled as templates" was false (all three live: pull request, push, weekly cron) - text now says live on arrival, why, and how to opt out. `pre-pr-review` required `/code-review` (exists only in the author's environment) - now describes the property (review in a context that does not already believe the code is right); findings list gains narration, duplication, scope creep. The comments rule line now ships in the entry file. The story-to-slice rename was half done (fourteen survivors in the task template incl. all six per-phase Goal/Independent-Test lines; `spec-tasks` label block; `spec-implement` phase list; one said tests generated "if tests requested"). The changelog checklist row marked `-> scale` (per-PR fragments). The transition router lost the `disable-model-invocation` flag.
- The project says what it is looking for, and from whom: four things named (practices that beat these; people who think in-repo/executable-over-prose; maintainers; AI practitioners); technology expertise routed to the stack repo; `open-questions/` named as the maintainers' front door; new entry work-periods (`cycle` provisional over `sprint` (ceremony), `wave` (collides with alignment waves), `track` (loses time bound and goal); and whether work periods belong at all, scale-only). The changelog says it will be rewritten before the org move. The reference checkout `standards-ref/` becomes `.repository-standards/`, gitignored. `site.config.json` moved into `site/` (root path still resolves). The contribution guide stops overstating: a contribution never edits `VERSION`; the maintainer bumps it when cutting a release.
- The security baseline gets a menu, without a new rule: "Security baseline" was a required decision described in one checklist cell. `docs/security-baseline.md` ships thirteen axes, each a question the record must answer: secrets, authn, authz, input validation, injection, transport and headers, rate limiting, logging and privacy, dependencies, CI permissions, agent boundaries, data at rest, a one-paragraph threat model. "Not applicable" is an answer; blank is not (the record must say where each axis lands). No new rule number (obligation attaches to R19; a twenty-fifth entry "would have bought a heading rather than a mechanism"). Technology depth stays in stack repos. Nothing machine-checked, and the page says so.
- The surfaces stop claiming more than the repo can back: the landing said twenty rules against a grown spec (`20<small>rules</small>` - number and word either side of a tag; `tree-check` strips markup before matching; the landing states no number). The terminal showed "drift 0 - 25 checks" (real runs report 52). "No framework to learn, nothing to install" was not true (Node, `jq`, a port for non-Claude agents). "Community-vetted" in the present tense removed (one committer, no external reviews). `llms.txt` gains "Status and limits" (pre-1.0, no tags, no public adopter, one stack, what drift 0 certifies). The FAQ answers "who is using this?" and "what does drift 0 actually certify?". The verify command was presented as if it ran here (adopted-repo command). README version restatements declared. `PRODUCT.md` says which rules it follows (own decisions, specs, backlog, personas, guards; no pin, manifest copy or `CLAUDE.md`; aligning on itself is open).
- Navigation leads where it promises: `AGENTS.md` never named the rules it cited (R23, R24) nor linked `SPEC.md` - now links spec, manifest and verification doc in one sentence. The tree ships knowing it is not compliant yet (owes `.standards-version`, a filled roster, a capability map); a "First 30 minutes" section replaces the dead pointer to the transition skill with seven steps ending at drift 0, then enabling workflow templates. Three skills could not be started by the agent (`spec-update`, `pre-pr-review`, `update-to-version` carried `disable-model-invocation`) - flags gone; the loop section names all three plus `add-to-backlog`. The docs hub listed 8 of 17 entries. `ADR-0NN` meant two things (the standard's decisions vs the adopter's from `ADR-001`) - one line says which. The supersede rule had no slot (`Superseded by` row added to both templates). The coupling guard blocks at `scale`, advises at `core`; the full-tree audit blocks at both (`enforcement.md` corrected). The taxonomy sent adopters to a nonexistent `docs/open-questions.md` (two working forms offered). `docs/method/` is adopter-normative by reference (exception carried in README and `AGENTS.md`). This repo says it is not an aligned repo (no pin, manifest copy, `CLAUDE.md`; altitude order in README). A blank line had pushed ADR-027 out of the ADR index table.
- The tree stops shipping fiction as content: the persona roster shipped three invented people (`Owner-operator Olga`, `Agency admin Adam`, `Guest Gabor`) under "List the real customer/user types" - an adopter inherits them and the gate reads them; now a placeholder row with the filled version under "Worked example (delete after filling your roster)"; the roster scan bounded to `## The roster` (whole-file behaviour kept for files without the heading). The backlog shipped three invented items (`SPEC-1`, `ADR-1`, `DRIFT-1`) - now an example block outside the table. The placeholder check matched `<Repo>`/`<Product>` case-sensitively (entry file ships `<repo>`) and scanned three of seven files; bracket form excludes `:` and `/` (autolinks); on the pristine tree it warns on all seven. `add-to-backlog` wrote rows failing the backlog's own Definition of Ready (listed four of seven columns, omitting `cap`, `persona`, `owner`).
- The spec engine speaks the standard's language: the engine filled User Scenarios, Functional Requirements, Success Criteria, Key Entities (not in the capability template); its quality checklist gated against buildable specs ("no implementation details", "written for non-technical stakeholders"; upstream's example of a bad success criterion - a p95 latency target - is exactly a buildable requirement) - now gates on the declared tier. Tests stopped being a per-feature preference (`/spec-tasks` said tests OPTIONAL unless requested; `/spec-implement` mandated TDD): tasks follow the recorded testing strategy; money, security, external-contract and data-integrity paths non-negotiable; a missing decision emitted as a task. One asking protocol (specify marks gaps and hands off; clarify owns the asking - previously both asked and answers landed outside `## Clarifications`). Clarify lost its bypass (a spike is a recorded deferral, not an unwritten question). Answers route to Requirements, Data contracts, Interface contracts, Algorithms & rules, Invariants, Edge cases, Trust boundaries (error table part of the contract). Tasks group by requirement slice ordered by risk x leverage (upstream's unit was a user story with P1/P2/P3).
- The agent guards stop failing open: without `jq`, `read_command()` returned empty, each guard cleared its `[ -n "${CMD}" ]` check and exited 0; `deny()` was built on `jq` too. Now `deny()` escapes its JSON with `sed`/`awk`; a missing `jq` is itself a denial naming the install command; an unloadable `lib.sh` is a denial; `verifyAgentGuards.sh` runs a guard on a `PATH` without `jq` and requires a denial (and allows with it). The gitleaks allowlist waived every markdown under `docs/` (where `docs/discovery/` holds pasted mails and runbooks hold command lines) - removed; placeholder-shaped findings covered by the pattern allowlist. `docs/prerequisites.md` ships (what has to be installed and what each absence costs; a page, not a check: a laptop missing `jq` is not repository drift).

- The stranded stack phase comes home (2026-08-01): the align router's third phase `stack.md` sat on a branch from 22 July while the router described stack adaptation inline (of eight stale branches, seven were fully landed under different SHAs and one held this). What it adds: the wave order by blast radius (what protects, then what shapes code as its own PR because the diff noise is real, then what proves, last what automates); a kept competing tool is a recorded exception with its trade-off named; the starter is a reference, never a second app beside the code. Reconciled with the current router (the branch's older router edit dropped); technology knowledge never enters this repo - the phase reads the stack repo's `ADAPTING.md` and `DECISIONS.md`.
- Adopters get the derived-facts check, and R4 says why (2026-08-01): `scripts/facts-check.mjs` ships: a repo declares what it restates in `docs/facts.json` (shape in `docs/facts.example.json`); `self-verify` runs it as a manifest guard; a stale restatement becomes drift with a number; a repo declaring nothing passes quietly. R4 gains the hook: a fact has one home, and a restatement either links to it or is declared and verified ("A rule that says this without a check is the kind of sentence everybody agrees with and nobody obeys"). One implementation, dogfooded (this repo deleted its private copy). `docs/facts.json` joins the author-it-yourself set (like the version pin and the capability map; the tree-guard spec says why that set exists).
- The upstream scan runs, and the postmortem gets its template (2026-08-01): github/spec-kit v0.13.2 -> v0.15.1 reviewed - mostly CLI, presets, extensions, packaging (not consumed); one command-template change taken: the clarify loop asks a question, not a label (cherry-picked from upstream commit `39f2ac3`: lead with a full interrogative, never a topic label or requirement id - "Retention policy" and "FR-023" are subjects - followed by one sentence on what changes depending on the answer; hunk marked `CHERRY-PICKED` naming the upstream commit beside the `PATCHED` ones). Read and not taken: a duplicate step-numbering fix (the extracted specify prompt has none), constitution-command fixes (command not shipped), the port of setup scripts to Python (shipped scripts are bash with graceful fallbacks). Postmortem template ships (the runbooks README prescribed blameless postmortems in a fixed order with actions becoming backlog items in the same PR, while shipping nothing); carries the actions that were rejected and why, and what is deliberately not being changed.
- A repo can find out the standard moved (2026-08-01): `standards-update-watch.yml` ships as a template - weekly, compares `.standards-version` against the standard's newest release, opens one issue per target version with the line to say to take the update. It never edits the pin ("an alignment that happens while nobody is looking is not an alignment"; a human runs `update-to-version`). Installed before the first release it says so and passes; an open issue for the same target version ends the job (a weekly cron must not mint a weekly issue). Renovate gets the same job where a repo runs it (a documented custom manager treats the pin as a dependency; the PR is a proposal and half the work - merging a bare bump leaves the repo red because the manifest copy arrives with the update). Still waiting on the first tag.
- Every doc opens with your case, not with the concept (2026-08-01): the "You have this case - say this" pattern of `working-with-specs.md` and `discovery.md` now opens all nine method docs and the shipped folder READMEs (backlog, decision-records, ideas, runbooks, journeys, analytics, research): the situations people are in, the exact line to say, where the result lands. Corner cases are part of the answer (update vs new spec; business vs technical record; an idea that is really a decision waiting; a rename that is a migration; an assessment that skipped half a repo). The method index leads with a table keyed by what you are holding.
- A fact restated in prose now has to agree with its source (2026-08-01): `specs/capability-map.json` made "these move together" a declared edge for code and specs; prose was left out ("twenty rules" while `SPEC.md` had 21; "11 lifecycle skills" while the tree held 12 - both found by a person, late). `tools/facts-check.mjs` + `tools/facts.json`: a fact has one home (a file to read, a glob to count, a pattern in a source) and every restatement is declared; three wired to start (how many skills ship, the GitHub path a client degits, the version across `SPEC.md` and the landing). A surface that stops matching its claim fails ("Silence there would look exactly like agreement"). It found the 12th skill (`discovery-digest`) on its first run; the open question `eleven-skills.md` renamed `shipped-skills.md`; `tools/facts-check-test.mjs`, 6 cases. Adopters do not get it yet (repo-own until a home, a manifest entry and a normative hook exist).
- The schema pair stops being a promise (2026-08-01): `scripts/schema-pair.mjs` ships and `self-verify` runs it as a manifest guard (a repo with no `database/schema/` reports that and passes). The pair is a declared edge: each file names its counterpart in a `pair: <path>` comment, resolved both ways; three distinct failures (naming nothing; naming a missing file; naming a twin that does not name it back). Proves every name the DDL defines (table, column, enum type, enum label) appears in the twin, compared case- and separator-insensitively; type agreement not checked (reading Zod/Pydantic structurally is a stack-repo concern); a twin field with no column is not drift. `tools/schema-pair-test.mjs`, 9 cases, found a false negative (the `pair:` path's words vouched for a table called `bookings`; declarations stripped before reading the twin).
- The database schema becomes something the repo holds (2026-08-01): new rule R24 - a repo that owns a database carries that schema as executable DDL under `database/schema/`, complete enough to rebuild the database from a checkout alone (the shipped guard already told the agent to leave a reviewed `.sql` there). It exists twice on purpose: also a typed, documented definition in the stack's idiom (Zod in TypeScript, Pydantic in Python); every path reading/writing the database goes through it; a declared 1:1 pair (every table, column, constraint, enum in both; each names its counterpart; a change to either lands in the same PR); either side may be generated where the generator does not drop what DDL can express. ADR-027 rejects: migrations (the delta, not a readable state), an ORM model (binds the most durable asset to a library's lifetime), a `pg_dump` artifact (machine output nobody reviews - fine as a check against authored DDL). Held by review at first (no manifest entry can prove a conditional path).
- The agent guards stop being walkable, and the coupling gate stops firing on data (2026-08-01): two reproducible bypasses in the shipped `PreToolUse` guards (the remote-database guard grepped the whole command, so `psql -h localhost -c 'select 1' && psql -h prod-db... -c 'DROP TABLE users'` vouched for itself; its localhost match was unanchored, so `localhost.evil.example.com` read as local); a force-push guard that was never there (the deny list blocks literal spellings; `git commit -m "..." ; git push --force origin main` passes a permission pattern). The guards are scripts now, not 900 characters of shell inside JSON; each splits the command on `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|` and judges segments separately, anchors the hostname end, strips no quotes (two unrelated apostrophes had paired up and swallowed a `--force`). `scripts/verifyAgentGuards.sh` drives all three with 32 real commands (a remote write denied, a SELECT against it allowed, a localhost write allowed, a plain push allowed, seven force-push spellings denied); writing the cases found two more holes: git accepts any unambiguous abbreviation (`git push --force-with-l`) - deny on the `--force` prefix; the host flag matched case-insensitively so curl's `-H` read as psql's `-h`. The coupling gate stopped demanding a spec update for data: a map entry may be `{ "glob": "<glob>", "couples": "shape" }` - the file's key shape is the contract (added entry or edited value passes; a key path appearing or disappearing demands the spec; anything uncomparable couples). The coupling guard now sees untracked files locally. `tools/spec-guard-test.mjs` runs the shipped guard against real diffs in a throwaway repo, 11 cases.
- The local link gate stops under-checking (2026-08-01): `link-check` read only `git ls-files`; now scans untracked, non-ignored markdown too and names the untracked count; the fix had been written on a stale branch and never merged.
- Working with AI gets an evidence layer (2026-08-01): new method folder `docs/method/working-with-ai/` - seven notes, each opening with the complaint and closing with the rule: comments that earn their tokens, context as the budget, felt speed vs measured speed, a check the agent can run, review as the place the cost lands, instructions that survive, blast radius before autonomy. Every note declares a confidence (`strong` / `mixed` / `thin`), lists sources with what each shows, carries `Last checked`; vendor docs count as evidence of intent, surveys as perception, only studies or large code datasets as outcomes. The first entry: "AI writes ten lines of comments for two lines of code" is true and measurable, and the counter-argument (comments carry context to the next run) is half right - models read comments as semantics strongly enough that misleading ones hurt more than misleading variable names; resolution is a lifetime test (expiring context belongs in the plan, durable non-obvious *why* in the code, structure in the spec); "I will remove them later" is the failure mode. Wired in: a manifest reference, a docs-site page, a link from `ways-of-working.md`. Citations by key into `sources.md` (authors/publisher, date, stable identifier such as `arXiv:2507.09089`, the sample, accessed date); the key's prefix is the evidence class (`Study`, `Data`, `Survey`, `Vendor`, `Incident`, `Field`). Every load-bearing figure re-read and two were wrong: Stack Overflow trust in AI accuracy is 32.7%, not 29% (the *somewhat trust* band alone); GitClear duplication replaced with the measured series (cloned lines 8.3% -> 12.3%, moved code 25% -> under 10%, 3.8% in the follow-up dataset); entries declare `primary`/`secondary`; pseudonymous posts cited by thread id. Notes declare a `Decays:` class (slow / medium / fast) and "what would change this"; README carries the three-level re-check; a claim confirmed on our own code graduates to a case study. An eighth note from practitioner threads read directly: `the-cleanup-comes-later.md` (consultants' inbound shifting from building to repairing AI-built systems; marked `mixed`); four notes sharpened (comment density inverted in agent-written code; a large green suite can carry a tenth of its value; an engineer publicly refusing to review code its authors cannot explain; a 300k-line setup reaching the skills-plus-hooks split by exhaustion). README table of what each source kind may prove. Same entry: the pre-PR check list in `AGENTS.md` claimed "the same set CI runs" while omitting the spec-coupling guard - both `spec-guard` invocations added, with the line that a check in `checks.yml` absent here is a bug; the web-surface spec stops hand-writing the page-map length; the verify-engine spec gains the criterion that manifest data growing is not an engine change (the false positive behind it opened a gate-health epic: the map cannot tell a file's schema from its data).
- The mainline gets a shape (2026-07-31): branch and history is normative (ADR-026, new R23): a branch is updated by rebasing onto its base and the base is never merged back; every PR is based on the mainline, never on another open PR's branch (this repo had three PRs' commits stranded on a rewritten base); a PR lands as one readable unit; a branch may be rewritten only while it is the author's alone. Rebase-merge is the paved road, squash the sanctioned alternative (not a ban on merge commits - the braid of back-merges is what breaks history); rebase-merge publishes every commit, so each must be complete, buildable, reviewed; a repo that will not hold that bar squashes and records it in its branching ADR; rejected: merge commits by default, squash-everything, semi-linear where GitHub cannot offer it; accepted costs: new SHAs, no signature survival, commits CI never tested in that form. Lands in `docs/conventions.md`, the shipped PR template (rebased-on-`main` check), the red flags (force-pushing a branch others build on), the decision checklist's integration-method row; open question `rebase-merge`.
- The standard declares itself living - latest is the only target (2026-07-30, ADR-025): no version ranges or requirements anywhere; every align and update targets latest; the pin (`.standards-version`) is a bookmark of the last aligned state (makes updates deltas and self-verify meaningful), never a constraint; references resolve at `main`; canonical phrase "adopted by reference from the living standard - always latest" (ADR-004/023 keep their text with revision notes); staying current is a notification proposing a pin bump (watch workflow, Renovate rule - backlog until the first tag).
- Discovery has a home, specs draft early, the front door leads with usage (2026-07-30): ADR-024 discovery dossiers at `docs/discovery/<topic>/` hold provenance-stamped extracts of meetings and mails (raw transcripts stay out); a dossier is never normative (where it differs from a spec or record, the spec has already won); the `Last reconciled:` stamp in the dossier README makes "explain it once" mechanical (agents ask only about entries newer than it); entries `new -> folded-into-spec | superseded-by-record | open`. Typed open markers: a spec can draft on day one (`in-refinement`) holding each gap as a marker naming what is missing and who brings it (a question, a missing ADR/BDR, a missing input such as UX design, a missing asset such as credentials); the clarify gate blocks the whole `[NEEDS ...` family, so the open markers are the gap list. `discovery-digest`: new lifecycle skill, the dossier's curator (ingests notes/mails, writes the essence with provenance, flags contradictions between entries, reports when a topic is ripe for `/spec-specify`); never writes specs; spec skills never curate; `spec-specify`/`spec-clarify`/`spec-plan` read the dossier first and move the stamp. Docs lead with usage: the README opens with the real asks; two worked-example method docs (`working-with-specs.md`, `discovery.md`); the landing's hero becomes a live agent session; the prior landing stays at `site/previous.html`.
- The wizard, the feedback loop, the honest gates (2026-07-29):
  - ADR-020 intake-first adoption: `align-to-standards` opens with step 0 - measure the repo's state, then one question round (intent, technology with Layer 2 consent up front, appetite, plan-only vs execute); assessment-only is a legal named outcome; both phases walk one gate spine (`adoption.md`); brownfield reconstructs personas from the assessment's evidence and gets the stack offer right after the assessment; greenfield gains a starter-composition rule (degit into the root first, Layer 1 lays over it, collisions per adapt class); a pinned repo wanting a stack later has its own route; every assessment finding and backlog item names the owner role (product/business, architect, dev, agent).
  - ADR-021 adoption feeds the standard: align and update runs close with a consent-gated upstream offer (a registry miss -> stack request issue; friction -> adoption friction report; a doc fix -> PR); new `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` forms (stack-request, adoption-friction, bug); CONTRIBUTING gains "Feedback from adopters"; `llms.txt` tells agents the channel exists.
  - ADR-019, new R22: lifecycle procedures are normative and agent-portable (the spec loop, backlog capture, pre-PR review, version updates MUST ship agent-executable; `.claude/skills/` + `scripts/spec/` is the reference form; a non-Claude repo ports them strictly; the manifest accepts `.agents/skills`); manifest entries cite the rule demanding them.
  - ADR-022 (revises ADR-016): stacks linked, not version-locked - the stack manifest's `standards` version range is gone; the registry back-pointer is the linkage; a core manifest-contract break is an explicit recorded migration.
  - ADR-017 discharged: everything consumed pinned exact (both this repo's workflows and the shipped templates pin actions by full SHA, fixed runner images, exact node versions; `tree-check` gains a pin lint).
  - The manifest covers the whole tree (the shipped-but-unlisted class is gone: `spec-guard.yml`, `docs/personas.md`, SECURITY, the PR template, process docs, journeys/research/runbooks; `tree-check` verifies the reverse direction - every shipped file is an entry or an explicit exemption); the intake asks core-vs-scale and align writes it into the manifest copy's `profile`, which `self-verify` uses as default (a solo repo no longer red out of the box); the shipped `spec-guard.yml` blocks on coupling only at scale and runs the full `--audit` on every PR.
  - Derived facts stop being hand-written: rule counts and ranges left every surface ("the numbered rules"; the number lives in `SPEC.md`); `tree-check` fails a surface hardcoding one; `site-check` derives the docsite page count from the PAGE MAP and asserts the landing advertises `VERSION`; the persona gate cannot evaporate (specs without a roster fail the structure guard; the roster is a required manifest entry); committed plan/tasks scaffolding is warned about (R13).
  - ADR-023 (extends ADR-004): method docs live beside the tree - the adoption checkmap, repo assessment, taxonomy, decision checklist, ways of working and changelog process moved to `docs/method/`; the shipped tree is literally the client repo at day zero; clients adopt the method by reference at the pinned version (manifest `references` section; `self-verify` notes it and never file-checks it; `tree-check` fails a dead reference).
  - The generator serves any repo: page titles, sidebar footer links and the generated README come from `site.config.json` ("one form, many sites").
  - Repo hygiene: own `CODE_OF_CONDUCT`, own `SECURITY.md` (the shipped one is the template), own PR template; `update-to-version` states where the two manifests come from and updates the stack layer; `docs/self-verify.md` says which rules the drift number covers and which stay review-verified; FAQ and PRODUCT catch up; garbled shipped texts (checklist, enforcement, conventions) read clean; the ADR/BDR templates no longer hardcode the author's name.
- The spec, the tree, the engine (2026-07-22):
  - `standard/SPEC.md` new: the whole normative core on one page - numbered MUST/SHOULD rules (RFC 2119), versioned with the standard; where any other document appears to add a requirement, the spec wins; the manifest is its machine-readable projection (every entry cites `rule: "R#"`).
  - One authored tree (ADR-014): `standard/` is the single committed consumable form at real-repo paths; the old source/dist pair and `tools/reflect.mjs` are gone (the reflect build and its four mapping classes were introduced and retired within this span); `tools/tree-check.mjs` guards the tree (no repo-own leaks, every manifest promise present, the pristine tree passes `self-verify --skeleton`); adoption is one line: `npx degit repository-standards/core/standard`.
  - Spec engine extracted (ADR-015, supersedes ADR-013): the five load-bearing Spec Kit prompts are the standard's own skills (`spec-specify`, `spec-clarify`, `spec-plan`, `spec-tasks`, `spec-implement`; provenance github/spec-kit v0.13.2, MIT, `scripts/spec/LICENSE`), runtime at `scripts/spec/`; deleted: the vendored area, `.specify/`, the ten `speckit-*` skills, the dead `create-new-feature.sh`, ~600 lines of extension-hook boilerplate.
  - Skills consolidated - one family, eleven shipped: `backlog-from-specs` merged into `add-to-backlog` (two automatic triggers); `spec-analyze`/`spec-converge` stubs folded into `spec-reconcile`'s cross-spec consistency step; transition flows collapsed into one router (`skills/align-to-standards/` with greenfield and brownfield phase files; `modernize` lives as adoption's plan-then-refactor pass, ADR-007 unchanged); `disable-model-invocation` matches what `AGENTS.md` orders (impact/reconcile/backlog self-fire, update stays gated).
  - The repo gates itself: first own CI `.github/workflows/checks.yml` (tree-check, link-check, docsite build + site-check); the web surface moved to `site/` (landing committed, `site/docs/` generated and gitignored); `apps/` and the wheel experiments gone.
  - `changes/` retired for this repo (the maintainer edits `## Unreleased` directly); team repos keep the fragments mechanism as a scale-profile prescription (`standard/docs/changelog-process.md`; the assembler ships as `scripts/changelog.mjs`).
- Lifecycle and the guided loop (wave 2): ADR-010 artifact lifecycle - ideas live in `docs/ideas/` (status-driven, no records until approved, graduation on approval); specs/records/docs are permanent and living; plan/tasks are ephemeral and removed at close; enabling work (tokens, access) goes front-matter -> tracker as blocking stories, never spec prose; tracker posture: GitHub Issues default, Jira and Linear as adapters behind a one-way bridge with key write-back.
