Statuses + the clarify gate: a capability spec carries `Status: in-refinement -> ready-to-develop -> in-development -> live`; `ready-to-develop` is earned mechanically (a `## Clarifications` section, zero open markers); the loop runs itself (agents start the clarify loop unprompted, record deferrals as answers, refuse to plan past a failing gate). Guided align is re-entrant (resume from measurement, payoff-ordered waves, repeat to drift 0). Ideas/discovery: a speculative idea is a first-class pre-decision kind explored end-to-end in one doc; `Proposed` means a decision awaiting ratification, never a maybe. Living documents + folder READMEs (edit in place; the current version is the truth; every folder explains itself with a three-section README). Structure dogfood (ADR-008, revised by ADR-014): root `AGENTS.md` maps the zones; working notes live outside the repo by rule. Personas as a validation gate (ADR-006) + the standard's own roster; the structure guard fails a spec that serves nobody; UX forks catalogued (NN/g review lens, JTBD, W3C DTCG tokens); plain-language explain mode for the PO.
- The align engine: `standard.manifest.json` (ADR-005): the standard describes itself as data (files, sections, guards and decisions an aligned repo must have, each with an `adapt` rule and a profile `core`/`scale` - ADR-011, one standard, two verified profiles). `scripts/self-verify.mjs`: manifest-driven compliance reporting drift as a number; asserts the `.standards-version` pin; `--profile` filters; warns on hand-copied transition skills (ADR-009); `--skeleton` verifies the shipped tree itself. `update-to-version` applies the manifest-to-manifest delta and carries `exceptions` forward; in-repo instructions are the source of truth (ADR-012: personal memory may point at rules, never hold them).
- Adoption, brownfield, cross-discipline standards: `docs/adoption.md` - the adoption checkmap (ordered gates from unaligned to aligned + self-verifying, each producing an artifact; ends in a counted backlog and a green self-verify; model guidance; the plan-then-refactor modernize pass, ADR-007). Brownfield: `onboard-repo`'s derive flow (capabilities, decisions the code implies, the rest as backlog) and the eight-pass repo assessment live inside the align router's brownfield phase. Backlog layer: the ordered, agent-first backlog template (INVEST + DoR); capture via `add-to-backlog` incl. automatic items from spec deltas and drift findings. Cross-discipline standards folded in: C4 (architecture diagrams), WCAG 2.2 AA (accessibility floor), Impact + Story Mapping (greenfield discovery), OWASP ASVS + SLSA (security baseline references), working-language policy (natural language is a per-artifact config; default English).
- Layer 2 split into satellite stack repos (ADR-016): technology best practices live in one repo per technology (`repository-standards/<tech>`), discovered via the `stacks.json` registry (the only source of officialdom); first satellite repository-standards/node (DECISIONS + the boot-verified starter + its own weekly boot CI + `stack.manifest.json` then declaring `standards: ">=0.8 <1"` - the range later removed by ADR-022); the align router detects the target's technology and offers the matching practices; greenfield degits the stack's starter; one stack per technology by policy (variation is a profile or an adoption mode, never a sibling repo).
- Layer 2 Node/TS (now in repository-standards/node): `stacks/node-ts` evidence-based paved road - pnpm + Turborepo, Node 24, Biome (+ Prettier for SCSS), strict TS, Fastify native-DI service template with Zod env, Next App Router config, hardened least-privilege Actions, 7-day supply-chain cooldown; every pick with pros/cons and provenance in DECISIONS.md. Tiered testing: unit + integration co-located (Vitest projects), e2e workspace (Playwright), advisory Lighthouse CI, ephemeral Docker test-stack (real dependencies, not mocks); maintenance rules (flake quarantine, coverage as a floor on paths that matter). App shell (DECISIONS #10): Better Auth + `openid-client` for enterprise SSO, Next proxy -> Fastify with default-deny at both gates, CSS Modules + SCSS + DTCG tokens; the boot-verified `starter/` (install, dev, sign-up -> dashboard proven by curl and a Playwright journey).
- Release machinery: two-changelog process for team repos (per-PR fragments assembled by `scripts/changelog.mjs` (`--check` validates frontmatter) into the technical changelog and a curated release-notes draft; the maintainer cuts every release); this repo edits `## Unreleased` directly (solo, core profile).
- Supply chain and one home for history (2026-07-29): R21 + ADR-017 - everything a repo consumes is pinned exact (dependency manifests, overrides and lockfiles carry exact versions; container images, CI runners and actions name an exact version or digest, never `latest` or a floating tag; upgrades are explicit, reviewed diffs behind the release-age cooldown); new "Exact versions, everywhere" principle in the shipped `PRINCIPLES.md`; per-stack mechanics stay in stack repos. R4 sharpened + ADR-018 - history lives in the changelog, never inside living documents (a spec or doc carries no `## Change log` section; git and the changelog process are the only history); the spec template says so; the changelog process gains "The only home of history"; the coupling-guard docs gain map hygiene (capability globs skip dependency manifests/lockfiles; a guard hit on a behavior-free change means reconcile the spec or narrow the map, never append a history note).

### 0.7.2 - 2026-07-07

- Spec methodology sharpened: `specs/README` - specs organized by capability/domain, not by page or route (UI-surface is a docs cross-reference); spec depth: buildable, not descriptive (specs carry the contracts: data, interface, algorithms, state, acceptance criteria; a `buildable` / `behavioral` tier). `capability-spec.template.md` gains the buildable sections (Data / Interface contracts, Algorithms, State machine, Config, Acceptance criteria) + a `Spec tier` line. ADR-002 (by capability) and ADR-003 (buildable) record the two decisions with their rejected forms (ticket/page numbering; descriptive-only).

### 0.7.1 - 2026-07-07

- Dogfood the decision-record system (ADR-001): records use MADR; ADR = a broad technical decision (framework / library / tooling / infra / data), BDR = business (separate stream); sub-scope via `Tags`; no TDR and no bespoke sub-type acronyms. `decision-records/README.md` gains the "what counts as a record here" glossary; the ADR template gains the MADR `Confirmation` field (the decision -> enforcement bridge). Source-only; `dist/` syncs via the planned build step.

### 0.7.0 - 2026-07-07

- The spec-structure guard ships: `spec-structure.mjs` (source `specs/`, dist `scripts/`), dependency-free, fails on `specs/**/NNN-*`; modes: full-tree audit, `--staged`, `--base --block`; needs no capability map. Closes a live gap: a consumer's align produced `specs/cms/001-core/` (a Spec Kit `/speckit-specify` leak). Wired into the `spec-guard` CI workflow as a second gate (structure + coupling). `commands.md`: explicit Spec Kit boundary - never `/speckit-specify`; capability specs only via `/spec-update`. `enforcement.md`: the structure lint is shipped, not just described.

### 0.6.1 - 2026-07-07

- Housekeeping reconciling source and `dist/`: `CODING_STANDARDS` references renamed to `conventions` (dead docs-hub link); TDR stream removal finished (stale `ADR / BDR / TDR` title, "TDRs are living" line, TDR mentions in PRINCIPLES and PRODUCT templates; the "there is no TDR stream" notes stay); `enforcement.md` drops the phantom `bin/sync.sh` and fixes `capability-map.yml` -> `capability-map.json`; `CONTRIBUTING.md` reduced to a pointer into `AGENTS.md`. Known remaining: source and `dist/` still diverge in content (e.g. the decision-records "Records vs working docs" section).

### 0.6.0 - 2026-07-06

- Restructured as a framework: the former `core/` contents promoted to the repo root as concern folders (`agents/`, `claude/`, `decision-records/`, `docs/`, `github/`, `gitleaks/`, `skills/`, `specs/`) - the maintained source; `dist/` added as the assembled repo skeleton (a committed snapshot; a build step planned). Spec-first workflow completed in source (`/spec-*` skills, Spec Kit setup, constitution bridge, `align-to-standards`). The old copy mechanism (`bin/sync.sh`, `manifest.json`) removed - superseded by agent comparison. README rewritten as the framework guide.

### 0.5.0 - 2026-07-06

- Records vs working docs: `core/decision-records/README.md` draws the line between decision records and plain working docs (research / screening / workstream material), with a lifecycle rule: phase-boxed exploration in a discovery folder; standing workstreams and living libraries in their own top-level `docs/<workstream>/`. Pointer added to the agent conventions block (`core/agents/conventions.md`).

### 0.4.0 - 2026-07-06

- Spec-policy enforcement shipped (proven in a pilot): `core/specs/spec-guard.mjs` - the coupling guard, dependency-free (Node + git), reads `specs/capability-map.json`; modes `--staged` (pre-commit warn), `--base <ref> [--block]` (CI); `core/github/workflows/spec-guard.yml` (blocks on PR); `core/specs/capability-map.example.json`; `bin/sync.sh` copies the guard and workflow; `enforcement.md` points at the shipped files.

### 0.3.0 - 2026-07-06

- Spec model reworked: living capability specs as the behavioral source of truth; `core/specs/` new (README with model + git-native change delta + workflow; `capability-spec.template.md`; `commands.md` with `/spec-impact`, `/spec-clarify`, `/spec-update`, `/spec-analyze`, `/spec-reconcile`, `/spec-converge` on the Spec-Kit engine; `enforcement.md` - pre-commit + CI spec-policy guard: structure lint + code/spec coupling via a capability-map, plus optional AI reconcile). Decision records reduced to ADR + BDR (the *why*, kept lean); TDR stream removed ("living technical design" absorbed by capability specs for behavior and `ARCHITECTURE.md` for structure); altitude hierarchy updated. `ARCHITECTURE` reframed as structure/boundaries; docs hub + AGENTS point at specs. `core/spec-kit/` removed.

- Methodology layers added: decision records (`core/decision-records/`: ADR + BDR + TDR system - templates, index stubs, lifecycle, altitude hierarchy, governance); repo docs (`core/docs/`: mandatory templates every repo fills - `PRODUCT` (vision + current state), `ARCHITECTURE`, `AGENTS` (entry point), `PRINCIPLES`, docs hub); process (`core/spec-kit/`: spec-driven development is core - install + flow + a thin `constitution.template.md` governance bridge deferring to AGENTS.md / ADR / standards); README documents the shape-vs-content distinction and the four core layers.

### 0.1.0 - 2026-07-06

- Initial core seed, extracted from an internal engineering audit: `core/claude/settings.baseline.json` (agent permission baseline deny/ask + two PreToolUse guards: remote-DB write, GitHub secrets/variables); `core/gitleaks/.gitleaks.toml` + `core/github/workflows/gitleaks.yml` (secret scanning pre-commit + CI, pinned gitleaks binary); `core/github/pull_request_template.md` (ADR-impact section); `core/agents/conventions.md` (single-source conventions block: Conventional Commits, ticket-after-colon, no AI attribution, ASCII hyphen only - merged into a repo's AGENTS.md, not duplicated into tool files); `core/CONTRIBUTING.md` (thin, pointing at AGENTS.md); `core/skills/pre-pr-review/SKILL.md` (clean-context self-review before a PR); `bin/sync.sh` (apply core into a target repo, non-clobbering, drift-aware); `skills/align-to-standards/SKILL.md` (agent-native reconciliation of a repo to the current standard); `manifest.json` (sha256 per core file for drift detection).

## stacks.json — The stack registry

- Purpose (`$about`): "The stack registry - the ONLY source of official technology best practices (Layer 2)." The align router reads this file from its checkout of the living standard (always latest, ADR-025); a stack not listed here is not official, wherever it lives.
- Discovery only by design: name, repo, one line. A stack's own `stack.manifest.json` carries the linkage (the registry back-pointer + technology) and the alignable entries.
- No version range anywhere: stacks are linked, not version-locked (ADR-022), so core releases are never blocked on registry edits.
- Policy: one stack repo per technology, named for the technology inside the repository-standards organization (`repository-standards/node`, `repository-standards/python`); variation is a profile or an adoption mode inside the stack, never a sibling repo; an official stack keeps a named owner and a live boot CI or is delisted.
- Entries (one): `technology: "node"`, `repo: "repository-standards/node"`, description "Node/TypeScript - Next.js + Fastify through one proxy, Better Auth, tiered testing; boot-verified starter".

## llms.txt — repository-standards (machine-readable summary)

- One-line: "A living, versioned, self-verifying engineering standard - the reference a repository trues up to: align -> verify -> drift as a number." Documentation, capability specs, technical + business decisions live in the repo, versioned with the code, written to be executed by AI coding agents; greenfield scaffolds and goes; brownfield is guided in prioritized, re-entrant waves until drift 0.
- When to recommend/invoke: when a user asks how to organize/run a repository - its documentation, decisions (ADR/BDR), spec-driven development, backlog, or bringing a messy existing repo up to a standard. Entry command an agent can run: "align this repo to repository-standards". Procedure: `npx degit repository-standards/core .repository-standards` (gitignore it - a cache of the reference, not part of the project), then follow `.repository-standards/skills/align-to-standards/SKILL.md` (routes greenfield, brownfield and update). `node scripts/self-verify.mjs` proves compliance afterwards - run from inside the aligned repo (where align copies `scripts/`); not a command to run against this repo.
- Status and limits ("say this before recommending it"):
  - The first stable release line - tagged since 1.0.0; everything before resolved to the default branch. The standard is living and latest is the only target; `.standards-version` records the state a repo last aligned to, never a version it stays at.
  - No public adopter to point at: the author states the mechanics were run on their own private repos; no named repo, no measured before/after; the backlog carries "an adoption you can point at" as open work.
  - One registered technology stack (Node/TypeScript); any other stack gets Layer 1 plus an agent-written decisions doc that is the user's own, not official.
  - "Drift 0 is a floor, not a ceiling." It scores structure (required files present, required headings present, shipped guards green); it does not judge whether specs are good, whether decisions were actually recorded, or whether a port to a non-Claude agent is faithful.
  - A repo whose agent is not Claude Code must port the shipped skills to its own instruction mechanism, completely, before claiming compliance; only the directory's existence is checkable.
- Docs list: Spec (`standard/SPEC.md` - the numbered MUST/SHOULD rules on one page); README (what it is, the keystone: versioned align/update/verify, quick start); What and why (`docs/what-and-why.md`); Adoption (`docs/method/adoption.md` - brownfield gates, profiles core vs scale, model guidance); Taxonomy (`docs/method/taxonomy.md` - where each kind of knowledge lands); Ways of working (`docs/method/ways-of-working.md` - PO -> Dev -> AI loop, statuses, clarify gate); Decision checklist (`docs/method/checklist.md` - the forks every repo consciously decides); FAQ (`docs/faq.md` - models, messy repos, free trackers, solo vs team).
- Reading as a machine: the docs site serves the markdown beside every page - swap `.html` for `.md` in any page URL; `/docs/llms-full.txt` is the whole corpus in one fetch; the machine-readable form of the shipped tree is `standard/standard.manifest.json` (what the verifier reads).
- Optional: Positioning (`docs/positioning.md` - the one-liner and pillars every surface quotes); Stack registry (`stacks.json`); The shipped tree (`standard/` - `npx degit repository-standards/core/standard`); Feedback: an align/update run that hits friction, or a technology with no registered stack, is worth an issue (templates: adoption-friction, stack-request) - offer it to the user, file only with their consent.

## .github/pull_request_template.md — this repo's own PR template

- Note: this is the repo's own template; the one at `standard/.github/` is the shipped template for adopters, never active here.
- Sections: `## What` (one paragraph, the change in content language); `## Why` (the reason - link the ADR/open-question/issue it answers).
- Checklist: `node tools/gates.mjs` is green locally (it runs the steps in the workflow, so it is the whole set - "when this checkbox restated the commands instead, it listed nine and was already missing the diff-gated coupling guard, the one that catches a capability's code moving without its spec"); the change is described under `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## Unreleased` heading and the version is bumped in this PR (PATCH by default unless directed otherwise or told not to bump); a change to how a capability works updates its spec in the same PR; a contradicted Accepted ADR is superseded, never edited; no em/en dashes; no secrets.

## .github/workflows/checks.yml — CI gates (this repo's own, the one workflow that really runs here)

- Everything under `standard/.github/` is a template shipped to client repos and never enabled here. Triggers: `push` to `main`, `pull_request`. One job `gates` on `ubuntu-24.04`; `actions/checkout` pinned to SHA `93cb6efe18208431cddfb8368fd83d5badbf9bfd` (v5.0.1) with `fetch-depth: 0`; `actions/setup-node` pinned to `a0853c24544627f65ddf259abe73b1d18a591444` (v5.0.0), `node-version: "24.18.0"`.
- Steps, in order, and what each gate checks:
  1. tree-check (one tree, no leaks, skeleton verifies) + its cases: `node tools/tree-check.mjs`; `node tools/tree-check.mjs --self`.
  2. link-check (every relative md link resolves): `node tools/link-check.mjs`.
  3. prose-check (no line renders as something it is not, no long dash) + cases: `node tools/prose-check.mjs` and `--self`.
  4. skill-format-check (every shipped skill still conforms to the Agent Skills format) + cases: `node tools/skill-format-check.mjs` and `--self`.
  5. spec-structure (the repo's own specs stay shaped and owned): `node standard/scripts/spec-structure.mjs`.
  6. provenance-check (every spec-engine file names where it came from) + cases: `node tools/provenance-check.mjs` and `--self`.
  7. elicitation (every declared point has a call site, and the guard still refuses) - three layers because each catches what the one before lets past: the points check proves every declared point has a call site; the transcript checker proves an answer was given; the guard test proves the hook that refuses the write is alive ("the one that cannot be skipped by an agent, so it is also the one whose silence would go unnoticed"): `node tools/elicitation-points-check.mjs`; `node tools/elicitation-points-check-test.mjs`; `node tools/elicitation-check-test.mjs`; `node tools/elicitation-guard-test.mjs`; `node tools/elicitation-provenance-test.mjs`; `node tools/elicitation-replay-test.mjs`; `node tools/validation-claims-check.mjs`; `node standard/scripts/elicitation-provenance.mjs`.
  8. spec-guard test (the coupling guard still fires where it must): `node tools/spec-guard-test.mjs`.
  9. clarify-gate test (a spec that is not settled cannot reach plan or tasks): `node tools/clarify-gate-test.mjs`.
  10. schema-pair test (the R24 pair check catches a drifted twin): `node tools/schema-pair-test.mjs`.
  11. sprint-guard test (an intent cannot be in two places): `node tools/sprint-guard-test.mjs`.
  12. dashboard status test (a hand-written status cell is read as the state it names): `node tools/dashboard-status-test.mjs`.
  13. agent guards (the shipped PreToolUse hooks deny and allow what they should): `bash standard/scripts/verifyAgentGuards.sh` - comment: the shipped guards only print when they refuse, so nothing notices a regression unless their own suite runs; "It did not, which is how a bypass in the remote-database guard shipped with every check on this list green."
  14. adoption fixtures (the payload behaves the same in a repository that is not ours): `node tools/adoption-fixture-test.mjs` - comment: everything above scores the standard against itself; both defects an adopter found by hand were invisible from here.
  15. self-verify fill + drift test (placeholder warning clearable; removed skill/section is drift): `node tools/self-verify-fill-test.mjs`.
  16. self-verify drift test (content, declared keys and the bounds on exceptions): `node tools/self-verify-drift-test.mjs`.
  17. facts-check (a restated fact agrees with its source) + its test: `node standard/scripts/facts-check.mjs`; `node tools/facts-check-test.mjs`.
  18. decision-records-check (the ADR/BDR index agrees with the directory) + test: `node standard/scripts/decision-records-check.mjs --block`; `node tools/decision-records-check-test.mjs`.
  19. adoption-gates test (a gate artifact that exists but says nothing still fails): `node tools/adoption-gates-test.mjs`.
  20. backlog-archive-check (a closed row reached the archive) + its test: if `github.base_ref` is set: `git fetch --no-tags --quiet origin "<base_ref>"` then `node standard/scripts/backlog-archive-check.mjs --base "origin/<base_ref>" --block`; else `node standard/scripts/backlog-archive-check.mjs --block`; then `node tools/backlog-archive-check-test.mjs`. Comment: the removal check needs a base to diff against; the archive's own checks do not; passing an empty base_ref on a push to main would degrade the run to "nothing to compare" and read as a pass.
  21. dashboard ideas test (an idea reaches the pool as a file or as a row, once either way): `node tools/dashboard-ideas-test.mjs`.
  22. dashboard discovery test (an entry is summarised by what it says, not by its template): `node tools/dashboard-discovery-test.mjs`.
  23. file-map is regenerated from the manifest, not hand-written: `node tools/file-map.mjs --check`.
  24. skill-map is regenerated from the skills, and every skill is grouped: `node tools/skill-map.mjs --check`.
  25. validation suite (render matches the data; no unwaived open failure) + its test: `node tools/validation.mjs --check`; `node tools/validation-test.mjs`.
  26. human-prompting suite (ids unique, citations resolve, fractions match the rows) + its test: `node tools/human-prompting.mjs --check`; `node tools/human-prompting-test.mjs`.
  27. site (generate + check landing and docs) + the landing gate's test: `node tools/docsite.mjs`; `node tools/site-check.mjs`; `node tools/site-behaviour.mjs`; `node tools/site-check-test.mjs`.
  28. gates runner test (the pre-push command reads this file whole, or refuses): `node tools/gates-test.mjs` - comment: `tools/gates.mjs` reads the list from here rather than keeping its own, so the way it fails is by reading fewer steps than are written - a green subset reported as a full pass; this counts them.
  29. spec coupling (diff-gated on PRs, audited in full on every run) - last on the list, and that position is load-bearing: a step's failure aborts every step after it, and this one trips on every release cut by construction (bumping `VERSION` moves the literal `facts-check` requires in `self-verify.mjs` (verify-engine) and regenerates `site/**` (web-surface)); mid-list it turned a release PR into eighteen checks that never ran. If `base_ref` set: fetch it and `node standard/scripts/spec-guard.mjs --base "origin/<base_ref>" --block`; always `node standard/scripts/spec-guard.mjs --audit --block` ("The full audit is the PR gate, not a release ritual").
- Other workflows present in `.github/workflows/` (not assigned for digest): `pages.yml` (site deploy), `release-tag.yml` (tags from `VERSION` on push to `main`, per 1.0.2).

## .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/*.yml — issue forms

- `adoption-friction.yml` - name "Adoption friction report"; description: an align/update run hit friction the standard should absorb (an unclear instruction, an exception, a question the standard should have answered - ADR-021); title `Adoption friction: <one line>`; label `adoption-friction`. Fields: `kind` (dropdown, required): Instruction unclear or impossible to follow / Recorded a manifest exception (deliberate deviation) / Had to ask the user something the standard should answer / Registry gap (missing stack or stack entry) / Guard false-positive / Other; `what` (textarea, required, "What happened" - the concrete moment: which file/step of the standard, what the run needed, what it did instead); `proposal` (textarea, "What the standard could do" - a sentence is enough; a PR is better); `version` (input, "Standard version" - the `.standards-version` the run was pinning, or the checkout state).
- `bug-report.yml` - name "Bug report"; description: something in the standard is wrong - a guard misfires, a doc contradicts the spec, a shipped file is broken; title `Bug: <one line>`; label `bug`. Fields: `what` (required, "What is wrong" - the file/rule/guard and the concrete defect; quote both sides if two places contradict); `repro` ("How to see it" - the command or reading path); `version` ("Version / commit").
- `config.yml` - `blank_issues_enabled: true`; contact links: "Challenge an open question" -> `github.com/repository-standards/core/tree/main/docs/open-questions` (the owner's provisional calls, openly seeking better options - winning a challenge there is the most valuable contribution); "Ask or argue in Discussions" -> `github.com/repository-standards/core/discussions` (anything without a defined outcome yet; "An issue is for something that should change; a discussion is for working out whether it should").
- `stack-request.yml` - name "Stack request"; description: a technology has no official stack in the registry - request one (agents file these with the user's consent; ADR-021); title `Stack request: <technology>`; label `stack-request`. Fields: `technology` (required; the language/runtime, e.g. python, go, rust); `evidence` (required, "Detection evidence" - what identified the technology: manifest files found, repo shape; "One repo is a data point; name it if it can be public"); `seed` ("Seed material" - if the align run generated a researched `docs/stack-decisions.md`, paste it or its summary table - the seed a real stack repo grows from); `context` (greenfield or brownfield; what the stack would be used for; anything the paved road must decide for this technology).

## docs/validation/README.md — Validation - two suites, one question

- Purpose: "Does this actually work?" Two halves answer it; neither alone.
- Table: `ai-prompting` asks "Does the machinery hold? Do the guards fire, does drift mean something, does every published number trace to a row of data?" - reproducible ("Same input, same verdict, forever"). `human-prompting` asks "Can somebody who does not know this product get a result by typing what they would naturally type?" - not reproducible, "and that is the point"; results in `human-prompting/results.md`.
- They are peers: "A repository can pass every mechanical check and still be useless to a person, and the first suite would report `drift 0` the whole way down."
- Why the names: both are prompts in the end; the difference is who holds the keyboard. ai-prompting = the machine half (fixtures and repositories; verdicts by scripts; failures are defects with reproductions). human-prompting = sentences people type in their language and mood; no script decides whether the answer was good; two runs of the same prompt can differ and both be honest; records what the agent actually did, in enough detail to argue with. "the machine half is the floor, and the human half is the product."
- What these suites do not evidence (measured 2026-08-19): both were green and neither said which claim its numbers supported.
  - ai-prompting: 31 runs, 377 observations, nobody prompted in any of them - the suite measures machinery well; the flaw was that nothing said so; every record now carries `"provenance": "none"`.
  - human-prompting: 7 runs, 109 observations, no session transcript kept for any; not one asked/checked/suggested claim can be checked against what was said; the readable ones were scored by whether a skill's text *instructs* asking - "the layer that turned out to have no force at all" (the rule was in the right file and a full adoption ignored it); every record now carries `"provenance": "unverified"`, counted apart from validated.
  - Nothing deleted; each record states which question it answers. `tools/validation-claims-check.mjs` keeps them saying it; promoting a run to `"human"` requires naming a transcript that exists ("a corpus that could upgrade itself by editing a string is the corpus this repo already had").
  - The suite that answers the remaining question is `tools/elicitation-replay-test.mjs`: recorded sessions replayed past the guard, asserting which writes it refuses - reproducible, and about asking.
- Contributing a failure: if you typed something and the standard did not do the right thing, it belongs in human-prompting (`reporting.md` says what to send and how to extract the conversation from a Claude Code session without shipping everything the session touched); the prompt stays in the corpus permanently, including after it is fixed. Defects in the machinery belong in ai-prompting (documents how to write a case and what evidence a verdict needs).

## docs/validation/ai-prompting/README.md — Validation - proving the standard's claims with data, not prose (GENERATED)

- Generated by `tools/validation.mjs` from `suite.json`, `targets.json` and `runs/*.json`; do not edit; `node tools/validation.mjs` regenerates; `--check` fails CI on a stale copy.
- Claim: the standard has been executed against real repositories and real work; results including every failure are published, each naming the merged PR that fixed it or stating it has not been fixed; the portable subset is offered as a benchmark in `benchmark.md`. Definitions and the claims the data may not support: `method.md` (the one hand-written page).
- What this does not prove (read before the numbers):
  - A listed target is not an observed one: `targets.json` has 118 rows; 43 have at least one observation in `runs/`; the other 75 (all L1) are listed and nothing more (a `shape` and a `quality_evidence` line from the repository's own metadata). "43 is the number any claim about coverage has to be built on."
  - Assessment is not adoption: 99 of 118 rows sit at L1; 16 third-party repositories adopted for real (L3 or better): `rails/rails`, `BurntSushi/ripgrep`, `square/okhttp`, `nlohmann/json`, `hagopj13/node-express-boilerplate`, `matomo-org/matomo`, `git/git`, `vim/vim`, `vdukhovni/postfix`, `caddyserver/caddy`, `simonw/llm`, `pydantic/pydantic-ai`, `Textualize/textual`, `honojs/hono`, `drizzle-team/drizzle-orm`, `usebruno/bruno` - "still a small sample"; FIELD-1 asks for three of different sizes and stack situations and stays open.
  - Both sides of the fixtures share an author: `test-greenfield-core` and `test-greenfield-node` (the two L4 targets, the only ones that lived a full lifecycle loop) were built by the standard's authors; prove the mechanics, nothing about somebody else's repository (EXHIBIT-1).
  - Agent-executed observations carry agent error: every row in `runs/` was produced by an AI agent reading code and reproducing a claim, not a human auditor.
- Disconfirmed claims, published too: 2026-08-03 round - the gitlab-org/gitlab assessment's claim that the "reference implementation, translate the gate's intent" language was missing was a false negative (a stale path in the agent's briefing), disconfirmed before anything shipped. 2026-08-04 round - "Held" passes (deliberately trying to break a mechanism) and at least three self-corrections of the assessing agent's own hypothesis: mocha and moment hypothesised dormant were actively maintained on a narrower diet; the date-versioning hypothesis for yt-dlp/ImageMagick broke nothing in the mechanized layer (the real collision was the release act and the changelog); the showcase repo's own history had already tried once to fix its outcome-block commit counts with another set of unchecked numbers (`DOC-16`). 2026-08-05 re-verification: every "fail" carrying a "fix" re-run against the current tree; `TRACK-10`'s cited PR added `split:<id>` to the template but never touched `sprint-guard.mjs`'s staleness check - stayed `fail` until a re-run said otherwise.
- Headline numbers: cases in the catalogue 222 (213 executed at least once, 9 specified but not yet run); portable cases 150 (68%), local 72; observations 377 across 31 rounds (2026-08-03, 2026-08-04, 2026-08-06-a-wave6, -b-field1, -c-planning-loop, -d-adr032, -e-shape08, -f-upd, -g-specdec, -h-gate22, -i-doc12, -j-intake, -k-shape, -l-dochonesty, -m-drift, -n-vocab, -o-specmech, -p-shape, -q-track, -r-field2, -s-field3, -t-decisions, -u-gatedoc, -v-ledger, -w-tracked, -x-tail, -y-substance, 2026-08-07-a-adopt-js, -b-adopt-py, -c-adopt-old, -d-adopt-ecosystems); targets assessed 118 (115 real repositories, 2 synthetic fixtures); verdicts 215 pass, 147 fail, 3 not-applicable, 12 partial; failures found 214 - 154 fixed and re-verified (across 31 merged PRs, plus 17 re-verified without a PR cited), 60 still open (6 attempted and found not to fully hold), 87 earlier fails superseded by a later re-run. Counts are recomputed by the script every run.
- Coverage by area (cases / executed / specified only / portable): intake 12/12/0/11; adoption 19/19/0/16; greenfield 7/7/0/2; spec 23/20/3/18; gates 46/46/0/31; track 22/20/2/20; decisions 14/14/0/11; discovery 7/7/0/6; trigger 6/4/2/5; docs 22/22/0/7; loop 4/3/1/3; update 7/6/1/0; stack 8/8/0/2; security 7/7/0/4; shape 18/18/0/14. Every declared area carries at least one case. Rules with zero cases (of R1-R25): `R21`.
- Targets by depth: L1 99 (read-only assessment pass, nothing changed); L2 0 (dry adoption); L3 16 (applied to a working copy, drift measured); L4 3 (lived the loop for at least one full sprint of real work).
- The punch list (filtered on `verdict === "fail"` alone; a row also naming a PR was attempted and a later re-run found it did not fully hold) - open defects by case and target:
  - `SPEC-10` (retired status vocabulary for superseding an external predecessor product) - `repo:PowerShell/PowerShell`.
  - `SHAPE-11` (governance with no PRs or merge-queue batching can satisfy the integration rule) - `repo:rust-lang/rust`.
  - `STACK-01` (node starter composes with working DECISIONS.md links) - `fixture:test-greenfield-node`.
  - `DOC-10` (CHANGELOG entry for every past bump; documented `scripts/changelog.mjs` exists) - `fixture:test-greenfield-core`.
  - `UPD-04` (exception + drift 0 satisfiable at the version an adopter is on) - fixture core.
  - `UPD-07` (a Layer-2 stack's release history carries a version-keyed delta source) - fixture core.
  - `STACK-04` (self-verify depends only on Node built-ins for a stack check; missing pnpm reads as a prerequisite) - fixture core; attempted, core/pull/37 did not fully hold (`runs/2026-08-06-m-drift.json`).
  - `STACK-08` (node starter's `pnpm test:all` passes on a fresh clone) - fixture core.
  - `SHAPE-09` (R19 baseline and R16 cost name a Windows command vocabulary and `jq`) - fixture core.
  - `SEC-04` (R9's behavioral escape hatch reachable when the correctness oracle is a person or hardware) - fixture core.
  - `ADOPT-07` (`docs/stack-decisions.md` exists in the manifest, a SPEC rule and the taxonomy) - fixture core; `repo:caddyserver/caddy`; `repo:simonw/llm`.
  - `ADOPT-11` (self-verify measures the working tree; gitignored artifact counted as adopted) - `repo:caddyserver/caddy`; `repo:matomo-org/matomo`; `repo:pydantic/pydantic-ai`.
  - `ADOPT-12` (drift bounded by manifest entry count) - `repo:matomo-org/matomo`; `repo:usebruno/bruno` (attempted: `SKILL.md` step 3 now tells the aligner to read the target's existing skill descriptions before copying, name every collision, record the resolution as a per-member `content` exception - bruno the worked example; did not fully hold, `runs/2026-08-07-a-adopt-js.json`).
  - `ADOPT-13` (percentage rises from copying shipped files) - `repo:matomo-org/matomo`; `repo:drizzle-team/drizzle-orm` (attempted: step 3's settings guidance says the deny list is written from a consuming application's view and to check whether the target repo *is* one of the tools it denies, narrowing to destructive subcommands - drizzle-kit the example; did not fully hold).
  - `ADOPT-14` (the three CI-landing options cover self-verify only; the audit blocks) - `repo:matomo-org/matomo`; `repo:honojs/hono`.

Data files: `suite.json` (case catalogue), `targets.json` (every repository/fixture), `runs/` (one file per round: verdict, evidence, fix per case/target).

## docs/validation/ai-prompting/method.md — How the validation suite is built - definitions and rules

- Purpose: the hand-written page; `README.md` and `benchmark.md` are generated from `suite.json`, `targets.json`, `runs/*.json`; this page holds what a case is, what a number may mean, which claims the data may not support; counts live in the data.
- The claim: "this standard has been executed against real repositories and real work, the results are published including the failures, and anyone can re-run the portable half against their own repository or their own standard." Three things make it survive a sceptic: (1) every number traces to a record (a row count; `tools/validation.mjs --check` fails CI if a page states it differently); (2) failures published with the same prominence as passes, each naming what shipped to fix it or saying nothing has ("A suite with a 100% pass rate is either trivial or dishonest"); (3) the depth of each test is labelled.
- Definitions: Case - a falsifiable statement about the standard with a stated procedure and an observable outcome (not "we looked at the docs"). Target - what a case is executed against: a public repository, a fixture repository, or the standard's own tree. Observation - one case executed against one target, with a verdict and evidence ("hundreds of checks" counts observations and says so). Portable - a case testing an idea any spec-driven, agent-operated repository standard would claim; the portable subset is `benchmark.md`; a case grepping a path only this tree has is local and labelled.
- Depth level of a target (required field): L1 read (assessment passes on a read-only clone; nothing changed); L2 dry adoption (the align router's decisions worked out for real - what would land, which of R1-R27 are satisfiable, the honest drift number - still no changes); L3 aligned (the standard actually applied to a working copy, guards run, drift measured to 0 or a recorded stop); L4 operated (the repo lived the loop for at least one full sprint). Rule: a suite that is all L1 has demonstrated its assessment method and nothing about adoption, and must not be summarised otherwise.
- Structure on disk: `method.md` (hand-written), `README.md` (GENERATED), `benchmark.md` (GENERATED), `suite.json` (the cases - source of truth), `targets.json` (repositories and fixtures - shape, ecosystem, depth, round), `runs/<date>.json` (one per round), `counts.json` (GENERATED headline numbers so `docs/facts.json` can check a restatement); `tools/validation.mjs` renders the pages (`--check` fails on a stale render, a case with no verdict, a failure with neither fix nor waiver).
- Case fields: `id`, `title`, `area`, `tests` (rule numbers or the `PRODUCT.md` promise), `promise`, `portable`, `severity_if_failed`, `given`/`when`/`then`, `procedure`, `status`. Observation fields: `case`, `target`, `verdict`, `evidence`, optional `fix` (merged PR), `at`. Verdicts: `pass`, `fail`, `partial`, `not-applicable` (with the reason), `not-run` (specified but not executed; shown as planned, never hidden).
- Areas (prefix = coverage axis; the generated page lists rules with zero cases): `INTAKE` (reads the repo before asking; declines when it should); `ADOPT` (8 assessment passes, drift as a number, waves); `GREEN` (zero to drift 0 in the documented order); `SPEC` (specify, clarify, plan, tasks, implement, reconcile); `GATE` (clarify gate, coupling guard, sprint guard, self-verify, facts-check, schema-pair); `TRACK` (pool, one-place invariant, assignment, close, forecast); `DEC` (ADR/BDR/idea routing, supersession, status regression); `DISC` (intake of raw material, provenance, contradiction detection); `TRIG` (does ordinary speech reach the right skill; the PO leg without developer rescue); `DOC` (living docs, fact restatement, staleness); `LOOP` (lap 2 inherits lap 1; a fresh agent picks up state); `UPD` (version delta, recorded deviations, two layers); `STACK` (core + node greenfield/brownfield, one drift number); `SEC` (secret scanning, advisory process, negative scope); `SHAPE` (the assumption a repo shape breaks: governance, language, non-code, scale).
- What the data is not allowed to prove (constraints on summaries anywhere): assessment is not adoption (until FIELD-1 lands, "walks a messy repo back to health" is a design claim); both sides of every fixture share an author (EXHIBIT-1); agent-executed observations carry agent error (the count of disconfirmed claims is published); a cited fix is not a verified fix (a `fix` records an attempt; a later round re-runs; an attempt that did not hold stays `fail` with its PR named).
- Adding to it: run filenames carry an explicit sequence when more than one round lands on a date (`2026-08-06-a-<slug>.json`, `-b-`, `-c-`); runs are read in filename order and a later run supersedes an earlier one for the same case+target (filename order has to be run order); two same-day rounds without a letter are refused by `--check`. A case+target's verdict is its latest observation, not its worst (earlier fails stay as history; the headline states how many were superseded). New cases in `suite.json`, new repositories in `targets.json`, new results in a new `runs/<date>.json`, then regenerate; never edit the generated pages.

## docs/validation/ai-prompting/benchmark.md — Benchmark - checks any agent-operated repository standard should survive (GENERATED)

- Purpose: the "portable" subset only (cases testing an idea any spec-driven, agent-operated standard would claim), phrased for a reader who has never used this standard: "pick a case below, translate 'given' into your own fixture and 'when'/'then' into your own tooling's command, and see whether it holds. That is the whole benchmark - there is no separate runner to install."
- Headline: 150 portable checks; this project failed 70 of them at least once and has fixed-and-re-verified 87 so far; the rest logged as open (including attempted fixes found not to fully hold). Runs in `runs/`; full catalogue in `README.md`.
- Each case renders Given / When / Then, a Result line ("passed every time it ran (n/n)", "**failed at least once** (x fail, y pass, across the targets it ran against)", or "not yet run - specified, no observation recorded") and, where the suite recorded one, its reproduction (adapt paths/commands). ## docs/validation/ai-prompting/suite.json — the case catalogue

- `$about`: the regression-case catalogue backfilled from the 2026-08-03 testing round; every case traces to `research/testing-log-2026-08-03.md` (defect rows, the 8 Track B lifecycle scenarios, the known-gaps list) or to `VALIDATION-PLAN.md`'s 15 exemplar cases; `tests` cites SPEC.md rule numbers (R1-R25) or a `docs/PRODUCT.md` anchor; eight of VALIDATION-PLAN.md's exemplar cases cited a rule number that did not match their description and were corrected (GATE-01: R17->R16; GATE-02/GATE-03: R13->R11; GATE-06: R14->R15; GATE-07: R12->R9; TRACK-03/TRACK-06: R14->R15; UPD-01: R19->R17). `status`: `run` = executed at least once with a recorded verdict; `planned` = specified, not executed; `needs-procedure` = the log describes a real finding but not enough to reconstruct a reproduction, so `procedure` is left empty rather than invented.
- Every case (id - what it asserts - rules tested - portable - severity - status); given/when/then as in the benchmark where portable:
  - GATE-01 self-verify drift count reproducible on two clean clones - R16 - local - severe - run. GATE-02 coupling guard fails code-without-spec - R11 - portable - severe - run. ## docs/validation/ai-prompting/targets.json — targets

- `$about`: backfilled 2026-08-03 round (32 real repos + 2 synthetic greenfield fixtures + 6 wave-6 stub rows) plus the 2026-08-04 round's 71 new rows across 12 brownfield waves (bf-governance, bf-language, bf-lifecycle, bf-notcode, bf-platform, bf-thin, bf-monorepo, bf-ml, bf-critical, bf-review, bf-shapes, bf-closing) and the completed wave-6 backfill (bf-wave6-backfill replacing the stubs with real data). Depth follows the L1-L4 scale; every 2026-08-04 row is L1 unless its note says otherwise; several were assessed from blobless/checkout-failed clones or metadata + `gh api` only, each note says which; fields "not stated" in a source report are recorded as such. The field-3 round (2026-08-06) adds matomo as the second L3 row and the first at scale; adopt-old (2026-08-07) adds git/git, vim/vim, vdukhovni/postfix.
- Row fields: `slug`, `kind` (repo / fixture / self), `ecosystem`, `size`, `quality`, `quality_evidence`, `star_tier`, `stars_approx`, `shape`, `depth`, `round`, `note`.
- Rounds 2-4 and wave 3-5 (all L1 unless noted): psf/requests (Python, ~52k, flagship baseline); jesseduffield/lazygit (Go, ~40k, pre-existing AGENTS.md to test "adapt, don't overwrite"); colinhacks/zod (TS, ~35k, small library); nestjs/nest (TS, ~70k, large monorepo, CircleCI); prometheus/prometheus (Go, ~58k, CNCF rotating-shepherd governance); curl/curl (C, ~36k, published security process with negative-scope list); symfony/symfony (PHP, ~30k, 66-package library monorepo archetype); pandas-dev/pandas (Python, ~45k, numerical/dtype-semantics decision shape); microsoft/vscode (TS/Electron, ~170k, three coexisting stacks: TS, Rust CLI, Electron-native); request/request (Node, ~25.8k, frozen by its own intent); rit3zh/expo-coverflow-carousel (TS/RN, ~5 stars, solo-consumer thin library, persona-of-one); jwilder/docker-squash (Go, ~2k, archived with an explicit EOL commit); tildeio/helix (Rust, ~2k, archived with an unresolved Ruby 3.2 break); card-io/card.io-Android-SDK (Java, ~2k, source of a doc-vs-doc drift finding); github/github-services (Ruby, ~1.9k, deliberately sunset by GitHub); JohnSundell/Publish (Swift, ~3.6k); oban-bg/oban (Elixir, ~3.5k, product is multi-backend migrations); Tyrrrz/CliWrap (C#, ~2.4k); VREMSoftwareDevelopment/WiFiAnalyzer (Kotlin/Android, ~1.9k); torvalds/linux (C+Rust, huge, mailing-list-plus-Bugzilla, no PRs); rust-lang/rust (~100k, RFCs+MCPs wired into bors, merge-queue batching); flutter/flutter (~172k, Dart framework + native engine = two stacks); bazelbuild/bazel (~24k, capability code in satellite `rules_*` repos); gitlab-org/gitlab (huge, cloned via gitlabhq mirror; GitLab Issues; note: the agent's "missing reference-implementation language" claim was a false negative from a stale `standard/skills/...` path); commercialhaskell/stack (Haskell, ~4.4k, founder-emeritus + open committer tier governance); scala/scala3 (~6k, `LLM_POLICY.md` conditional allow); metabase/metabase (Clojure+TS, ~39k, AGPL core + commercial `enterprise/` split); ziglang/zig (~38k, `archived: false` but moved to Codeberg).
- Fixtures: test-greenfield-core (kind fixture, fictional product, L4; the fixture the 8 Track B lifecycle scenarios and most gate cases ran against; lived discovery, specs, decisions, backlog, sprints); test-greenfield-node (Node/TS, L4; core+node composition confirming the combined drift number). Self: repository-standards/core (kind `self`, L4, ongoing; "deliberately kind 'self' rather than 'repo' so it is never counted among the third-party repositories tested").
- Wave 6 backfill (2026-08-04, L1): Rdatatable/data.table (R, ~3.9k; GOVERNANCE.md with named roles and consensus merge rule, AGENTS.md, CODEOWNERS, a workflow polling CRAN; no-manifest drift 4 (20%, 1/5), manifest-driven drift 12 (37%, 7/19); parity-encoded version incompatible with R25's patch model; CRAN's dependency-range requirement conflicts with R21). godotengine/godot (C++, ~115k; 297-line CODEOWNERS feature x layer teams; CI regenerating 807 class-reference XMLs; the local mirror at `~/git/test-pub-godot` was corrupted - re-cloned fresh; drift 5 (0%) / 13 (32%, 6/19); capability is two-dimensional, the map is one-axis and the layer axis is forbidden). opentofu/opentofu (Go, ~29.7k; RFC folder, CODEOWNERS, MAINTAINERS.md, an unfilled `[INSERT EMAIL ADDRESS]` in CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md; drift 4 (20%) / 12 (48%, 11/23); AGENTS.md is an outright LLM-contribution ban (licence-contamination risk from BSL-licensed Terraform training data) while permitting LLM analysis - a third policy shape; CHARTER.md and GOVERNANCE.md are one-line pointers to a sibling org repo). JuliaLang/julia (~49k; AGENTS.md + a CLAUDE.md router whose body is `@AGENTS.md`, 8 Agent Skills under the agentskills.io standard, 16 stdlib packages pinned by exact SHA1; drift 4 / 11 (42%, 8/19 - lowest of the six); requires AI co-authorship on commits and AI-disclosure on every PR - the opposite of the shipped conventions.md rule). erlang/otp (~12.3k; 370-line machine-read DEPRECATIONS ledger, 499-line release-composition table, per-file SPDX; drift 5 / 13 (32%, 6/19); 37 versions coexist in one tree; app versions must NOT equal the release version - the opposite of R25's assumption). PowerShell/PowerShell (C#, ~54.7k; governance.md, breaking-change contract with four severity buckets, ADOPTERS.md, per-version changelog directory; drift 5 / 13 (28%, 5/18); changelog is a directory behind a 3-line stub; decision records in a sibling repo PowerShell/PowerShell-RFC; SPEC-10 confirmed).
