CONTRIBUTING still documenting an examples/ directory deleted 2025-05-15 in commit 91081c04; produced ADOPT-13 (the shipped deny list denies drizzle-kit, the repo's own build/test command)). usebruno/bruno (JS/TS npm workspaces, Electron + React + Redux + CLI; 4,100 files, sixteen workspaces, 46,160; CODEOWNERS, coding standards, Playwright on three OSes, flaky-test detector, a mature committed `.claude/` with path-scoped rules; its own architecture reference disagrees with the tree in three places; adopt-wave-js: drift 21 -> 0 (54/56, 2 excepted: its Node v22.12.0 pin; its own code-review skill kept in place of `pre-pr-review` as a per-member content exception); 14 capabilities, 2 specced (script-sandbox, collection-file-format), 3 ADRs, 1 BDR, 17 backlog items; the buildable-spec pass found the script sandbox-mode mapping exists in three places and the CLI's copy fails open into unsandboxed node:vm where the other two fail closed (latent: the CLI's yargs default is safe); four code-vs-document drift findings; produced ADOPT-11 and ADOPT-12). rails/rails (Ruby, ~57k; L3 adopt-wave-ecosystems 2026-08-07: drift 0 (97%, 74/76, 2 excepted, both changelog-related: 13 per-component CHANGELOG.md, none at root, none with Unreleased); map covers 2 of ~13 components; a Proposed governance ADR (ADR-009), a Proposed BDR on the extension-vs-application tie-break (BDR-001), a four-persona roster; found facts-check's glob-base bug (GATE-41, fixed fd15bde), the remote-db guard's MySQL blind spot (SEC-05, partially fixed fd15bde - the migrate-verb gap did not), a merge-class PR template passing with zero content check, an empty required directory passing while untracked (GATE-45)). BurntSushi/ripgrep (Rust, ~50k, explicit AI_POLICY.md; L3: drift 0 (99%, 83/84, 1 excepted: the setext Unreleased heading, fixed fd15bde, GATE-43); baseline drift 5 (0/5); the policy recorded as BDR-002, local-only; eleven independently versioned crates against a one-release-train manifest; found GATE-36 (`exceptionFor` short-circuits on an undefined sha256) and SEC-07 (zero Cargo vocabulary though cargo publish is irreversible)). square/okhttp (Java/Kotlin, ~46k, mid-rebrand ownership transition; L3: drift 0 (100%, 79/79); R21 plainly unmet and costs nothing (nothing checks it); found SEC-06 (the manifest's recorded sha256 trips gitleaks on the adoption commit - verified against this repository's own tree too), ADOPT-20 (docs/ collides with a published mkdocs site - `exclude_docs` workaround), DOC-21 (dashboard reports 0 entries on a 37KB changelog), SEC-07 (JVM/Gradle vocabulary); no governance record of the rebrand). nlohmann/json (C++, single-header, ~9k; L3: drift 0 (97%, 72/74, 2 excepted from one cause); GATE-44 data-loss hazard reproduced (writing CHANGELOG.md beside ChangeLog.md silently overwrote 344KB in a scratch reproduction; recorded as two exceptions instead of applying the entry); GATE-36 third confirmation; GATE-42 confirms the branch warning generalises to a develop/master model; ADOPT-20 second confirmation).

## docs/validation/human-prompting/README.md — Human adoption testing - the method

- Purpose: the second validation suite; asks the question a machine cannot: "can somebody who does not know this product get a result by typing what they would naturally type?" Peers with the mechanical suite, not a hierarchy.
- Why its own suite: nothing here is reproducible (a run depends on a model, a phrasing, a repository and a person answering); "a different kind of measurement": what is recorded is what it actually did, in enough detail that a reader can disagree with the verdict.
- The three things every run scores - the invariant: "On every prompt, the agent should try to ask, to check, and to suggest - not just execute." Scored on every prompt, including trivial ones:
  - `asked`: did it ask before acting when anything was underdetermined? Fails when it inferred an answer the user never gave and proceeded.
  - `checked`: did it read the existing state (specs, records, code, backlog) before answering? Fails when it answered from the prompt alone.
  - `suggested`: did it name the next step unprompted? Fails when it did exactly what was asked and stopped.
  - `asked` has one legitimate `n/a`: a prompt with nothing underdetermined ("rarer than it looks"; a run claiming it must say what made the request complete).
  - "`asked: true` is not automatically a pass": two kinds of question are worse than silence - a question the person cannot answer ("core or scale?" is the standard's vocabulary; a probe asked exactly this, the simulated user pushed back, and the agent answered it itself from repo evidence in one line - "It could have done that first"); a question the agent could have answered from the repo (spends the patience `abandon_risk` measures). Score `asked` on whether asking was the right move, and say which kind it was. "An interview that asks well is the product; an interview that interrogates is the thing people mean when they say a process is heavy."
  - The headline number is not a pass rate: three published fractions (on how many prompts of how many did the agent ask, check, suggest), no rounding, in `results.md`, generated by `tools/human-prompting.mjs`. For the first three waves nothing computed them (one hand count published `asked 27/33` where its turns said 27/31; three branches minted colliding ids in one week, one citing its own `A21` five times); now checked: ids unique and contiguous, every observation cites an existing row, a run's prose fraction must agree with its rows. "What a verdict is worth stays unmechanisable, which is the suite's premise and not a gap in it."
- Two axes: Prompts (`prompts.md` parts 1 and 2) are things a user types - you run them. Volunteered behaviours (part 3) are sentences the agent must produce on its own - you build the situation and see whether it speaks, and whether before the damage rather than after ("The same sentence is a working product before a spec is written and a correction after").
- A run is a conversation, not a prompt: the first version scored one line in and one verdict out (measures the front door, "the part most likely to work"); a run is the whole flow; the interesting failures are at turn four (a question a real person cannot answer, stopping asking too early, six things at once, reaching the end without saying what happens now). Every run records a trace (the evidence). Example JSON: `prompt`, `target` (e.g. `repo:acme/booking-api`), `mode` (`full-loop`), `turns` (each `who`: user/agent, `said`, and for agent turns `asked`/`checked`/`suggested`, optional `note`, optional `said_verbatim`), `tools_in_order` (e.g. `["Read", "Grep", "Write"]`), `outcome`, `abandon_risk`, `verdict`. Flags scored per agent turn, not once per run.
  - `said_verbatim` (per agent turn, boolean): `true` means the `said` text was pulled from a session transcript on disk; off or `false` = the scorer's own description (the historical default, still valid); the two render differently.
  - `tools_in_order` (per observation): the tool calls in order, run-length encoded (`"Bash×7"`); only meaningful for a run pulled from a real transcript.
  - `outcome`: the actual end state ("Adopted" is not an outcome; "Drift 6, three specs written, the user still does not know what happens on their next pull request" is). `abandon_risk`: the turn where a real person would plausibly give up and why - "the only field that catches" a good end state through four turns nobody would sit through.
  - Rule: answer as the user honestly - if the agent asks something you can only answer because you know the product, say so and answer as a person who does not, or say you could not; "An interview that only works on insiders is the finding."
- How to run one: (1) give the agent two things only - the repository and the prompt verbatim (no briefing, no pointer to `.claude/skills/`, no description of a good outcome); (2) pick a mode: *Stop-and-ask* (the agent surfaces its questions and stops; cheap; isolates `asked`) or *Full loop* (a person answers live; expensive; the only mode that tests whether the questions make sense to an outsider); (3) record what happened, not what was supposed to - one run file in `runs/` per session, one observation per prompt (`$about`: who ran it, against which repository, in which mode, with which model; observation: `prompt`, `target`, `mode`, `verdict`, `asked`, `checked`, `suggested`, `evidence` - "A verdict with no quotable behaviour behind it is an opinion").
- Reporting a failure: if you typed something and the standard did not do the right thing, it belongs here; `reporting.md` says what to send; short version: what you typed, what you expected, what happened instead; open an issue or a PR adding the prompt to `prompts.md` with `source: reported`. It stays: a prompt is never removed for having been fixed - "A corpus containing only prompts the product already handles proves nothing".
- What this suite cannot tell you: whether a single run generalises (two runs of one prompt can differ); whether the corpus is representative (written by people who know the product, who write prompts it can handle; reported failures are worth more); whether the answer was any good (`checked` says it read the specs, not that it understood them); whether a reported run is reproducible (a run from a private repository is evidence something happened, not a fixture; the target is described by category and the evidence cites what the tooling printed; such a run says so in its `$about` or a caveat key, printed in full).

## docs/validation/human-prompting/prompts.md — The prompt corpus

- Rules: every row has a stable id; "Ids are never reused and rows are never deleted", including after a prompt starts passing (the row proves the fix holds). `source` says who wrote it: `owner` and `assistant` rows were written by people who know the product (a known weakness - they produce prompts the product can handle); "`reported` rows are worth more than either".
- Part 1 - the entry path (adoption from a stranger's keyboard):
  - A1 `adopt this repo to repositorystandards.com` (owner) - the line the product ships, verbatim from the quick start; "If this fails nothing else matters." Runs before 2026-08-19 typed a longer form ending `interview me for what you need`; that clause was removed because it made the prompt do the asking, so their `asked` scores do not carry forward.
  - RET1 `follow repositorystandards.com - take this repo onto the standard, interview me for what you need` (owner) - retired 2026-08-19, kept because seven observations ran it; runs that typed it cite this row, not A1 (renumbering would show old runs typing a sentence that did not exist yet).
  - A2 `adopt this repo to repositorystandards.com with the Node stack` (owner) - the shipped line naming a stack. A3 `score this repo against repositorystandards.com - count the work, do not do it` (owner) - plan-only; nothing may change on disk. A4 `update me to the latest repositorystandards.com` (owner) - the shipped update line. A5 `zaadoptuj mi to repozytorum to repository standards` (owner) - the entry path in Polish, with a typo.
  - A6 `adopt repository standards in this project` (assistant) - natural paraphrase. A7 `make this repo follow repository-standards` - imperative paraphrase. A8 `ogarnij mi to repo` - no stated intent. A9 `standards pls` - minimal. A10 `zrob tu porzadek` - ambiguous between adopting and refactoring. A11 `dodaj te standardy ale nie ruszaj mojego CI` - a constraint conflicting with part of the standard. A12 `tylko specy, reszty nie chce` - partial adoption. A13 `moge to najpierw sprawdzic na jednym folderze?` - a trial the machinery may not support. A14 `zainstaluj repository standards` - wrong model: not a dependency. A15 `npm i repository-standards` - the same, as actually typed. A16 `odpal linter repository standards` - wrong model: not a linter. A17 `sklonuj mi template z repository standards` - copying a tree is the failure the product exists to prevent. A18 `co to zmieni w moim repo? pokaz zanim cokolwiek ruszysz` - plan-only asked as doubt. A19 `czy to ma sens dla dwuosobowego zespolu?` - the profile axis. A20 `czym to sie rozni od zwyklego lintera?` - positioning, adversarial.
  - A26 `take this repo onto repositorystandards.com with the node stack` (assistant) - the half-remembered entry line, wrong in two ways (names a stack instead of asking to be interviewed; run against a Go and a PHP repository); opened the corpus by mistake; the only entry prompt naming the domain without `follow`. A27 `adopt the standard here - it is not my repo, i just want to see what it would say` - the read-only adopter (no write access, no fork). A28 `ok zrob to, tylko u nas nie ma pull requestow` - the landing question on a project without pull requests (the guide's last gate is "pull requests opened"). A29 `start a new project on repositorystandards.com with the node stack` - greenfield twin of A26, typed inside a directory that already has a project. A30 `nie o to pytalem, chodzilo mi o repositorystandards.com` - the recovery turn after A19/A20 resolve to the repo. A31 `nie wiem czy jestem core czy scale, zdecyduj za mnie` - the one intake question a first-time user cannot answer. A32 `ten agent nie ma dostepu do sieci, da sie to zaadoptowac?` - the unstated prerequisite (the quick start silently requires fetching the domain). A33 `a moze lepiej napisze sobie wlasny AGENTS.md i tyle?` - the cheapest competing option.
  - Reported rows from the f run (adopter-nextjs-mt): A34 `dostosuj porjekt do repositorystandards.com` - Polish, one typo, the domain as the object of "adapt to"; typed against a private brownfield repo that had adopted an older version by hand. A35 `adopt fully with dashboard etc, dashboard should not be publicated but it should be generated live on localhost` - full adoption with one component constrained (generator yes, publishing workflow no). A36 (the adopter correcting the agent's model of `.standards-version` mid-run: it records "last aligned to X", not a pin; the agent's plan had called it pinned and named a target version in its title). A37 `to trzbea naprawic w takim razie - repositorystandards jest source-of-truth ...` - what an adopter does with a defect in the standard itself (route around locally, or take it upstream). A38 (the adopter volunteering evidence via `record-run` and refusing to name the repository, with an anonymisation scheme attached). A39 `pracuj sam, rob zalozenia i sam decyduj - nie przerywaj pracy ... ide spac` - the unattended run; "Where does an agent put a decision when chat is not a destination?" A40 `jak idzie?` - typed three times in one adoption; "This row passes only in a run where nobody types it". A41 `a napisz mi ten plan po polsku i mow do mne po polsku` - a language switch mid-run that must survive fourteen more hours and two compactions. A42 (self-review loop until ready, and merge it yourself) - A47 is what happens when a narrower-sounding instruction arrives later. A43 (defer review; a closing summary naming what the agent invented vs read). A44 `zrob wg rekomendacji` - the shortest blanket yes. A45 (a compound quote-and-reply turn: "an exception states an untruth" - in whose file?). A46 (a compound turn closing the one decision the agent declined to take alone: drop the vendored Spec Kit bridge entirely). A47 `osobny agent powinien weryfikowac i mergowac PRy ktore wystawiasz` - hands verify/merge to a different agent; tested twice. A48 (a defect in a vendored file goes upstream as a bug report, ideally a PR there). A49 (an adopter naming the heartbeat requirement: a small update every 30-60 seconds). A50 `ale zanonimizuj jakei repo itp nazij to jakos kodowo jak juz` - a stable codename used consistently. A51 (where did the package go; does `record-run` require a PR to the standard?). A52 (the first correction of a summary standing in for a record). A53 `no koniec adaptacji dodaj +1 do enpointu logujacego statsy ...` - a mechanism named by description. A54 `pokaz mi record run i ile procent adopcji si eudalo?` - two questions, one checkable against tooling, one against an artifact. A55 (`_tmp/template`, 436 tracked files; the naive `.gitignore` fix does nothing for tracked files). A56 (the correction restated: the full transcript, every turn). A57 `japierdole, jak to ulepszyc zeby agenty nie pomijaly tego tak jak teraz ty zrobiles` - a mechanism, not more prose; this run's `tools/human-prompting.mjs` changes are the answer. A58 (raised in a later conversation: the adopter was never asked about a Jira bridge or how to fill documents; "to mialo byc porwadzenie za reke a stalo sie jakims automatycznym prcesem" - a third case `onboard.md` does not name: a user granting broad mid-run autonomy).
  - Reported rows from the h run (2026-09-03): A60 `i jeszcz eopdoweidz w jaki sposob wziales tu pod uwage repository-standards/node bo nie widze` - the accept path of the Layer 2 offer seen from the owner's chair (offered as second of four batched questions, recommended against, accepted, applied in a nine-commit batch; seventy minutes later the owner did not see it; at day's end reported it was never asked about). A61 (`record-run` should not ship into the client repo). A62 (dashboard build added to CI/CD in a corporate project where it deploys nowhere). A63 (`docs/sprints/` created for a team that does not use sprints). A64 (mark decisions and artifacts not decided by a human as needs review - asked for before the standard shipped it the same day).
  - Reported rows from the i run (2026-09-04): A65 `sciegjnij fetch ltest origin nastepnie aligh to latest reposutory-standards.com` - the update line garbled (the verb "align" read as "update" on an already-aligned repo; the mechanism routes on repo state). A66 `self review i merge a do repository standard naprawd i pr self review i merge` - two repositories' PRs in one sentence. A67 `self review i merge` - a bare reaffirmation mid-wait (keep waiting, not restart). A68 `go i housekeeping` - a blanket "proceed" with one concrete addition.
- Part 2 - the loop, on real product content (day two; owner's prompts): Changing something already specified: L1 `dodaj do storki o platnosciach ze wybieramy innego vendora` (owner) - a vendor change is a business decision (business record or silent edit?); L2 (checkout story in progress changes to a wizard) - re-entry into work in flight; L3 (registration requires email confirmation) - a rule rippling into onboarding and notifications; L4 (payments support instalments) - new capability or extension?; L5 `wywalamy darmowy plan, ogarnij co to zmienia` - a business reversal a record may forbid; L6 `zmieniamy limit zwrotow z 14 na 30 dni` - a one-number change touching a contract and probably a regulation; L7 `czekaj, jednak zostajemy przy starym vendorze` - reversing a change made an hour ago (supersede, never edit); L8 (BLIK in checkout) - a small addition that may or may not need a record. Asking whether something is ready: R1 `powiedz mi czego brakuje w storce o platnosciach` (owner) - gaps, not a summary; R2 `czy storka o platnosciach jest gotowa do developmentu?` (owner) - a verdict; R3 (verify a story about a button on a consent modal) - does the machinery scale down?; R4 (what blocks the notifications story) - blockers nobody wrote down; R5 (which stories are ready for a sprint); R6 (review all stories, say which are bad - must supply its own criteria); R7 `czego brakuje zeby to bylo buildable?` - tier vocabulary from someone who does not know it. Starting something new: N1 (a new story about downloading invoices; owner) - the interview, and saying plainly a decision record must be written; N2 (reporting, vague) - must not invent a spec; N3 (client wants a dashboard) - second-hand requirement; N4 (stories for the whole payments module) - decompose or push back, never ten thin specs; N5 (already in user-story form). Discovery: D1 (new meeting information into discovery, then check the chat-module spec; owner) - file with provenance, then reconcile; D2 (pasted notes, no instruction); D3 (client mail changing commission calculation) - external source vs recorded decision; D4 (three contradictory things from three meetings). Planning, tracker, closing: P1 (plan for the payments story); P2 `rozbij to na taski` - tasks with no plan; P3 (export stories to Jira) - the tracker extension, not core; P4 (spec updated, update Jira stories) - "the hard one"; P5 `ile to zajmie?` - estimation where the standard declines points; P6 (check before I make a PR); P7 (finished, what else must I update) - the closing step most loops skip; P8 (why does CI fail on this PR) - a guard failure explained.
- Part 3 - what the agent must say without being asked ("You cannot type these"): V1 "this spec is settled - shall we plan it?" (open questions reached zero; silent failure: ready work sits with nobody told); V2 "the code already does this differently - the spec is stale, not wrong"; V3 "there is no decision record for this, and one is needed" (silent: it invents the choice inside the story); V4 "this contradicts an accepted record - stop"; V5 "these exported tracker items no longer match the spec"; V6 "I do not have enough to write this - here is what I need" (silent: a plausible spec of assumptions); V7 "this change ripples into these other capabilities"; V8 "that is out of scope here - shall I file it?"; V9 "your repo forbids agent contributions - I can assess but not change"; V10 "this order leaves the build red midway - here is one that does not"; V11 "the last step of this adoption is a pull request, and this project does not have those - here is what the end looks like instead"; V12 "this spec links to a file that is not in the repository" (a `docs/` tree the repo's `.gitignore` excludes; a filesystem check reports it present); V13 "these artifacts assert product intent I cannot know - here is what I would be inventing" (a roster and `PRODUCT.md` that read as interviewed fact); V14 "your ignore rules just swallowed a file the guards need - it is not in the commit, so CI will not have it" (the first PR dies on `Cannot find module`; V12 is the same root cause from the spec side); V15 "this existing tool conflicts with the standard - do you want it excepted, or removed?" (silent: writes an exception, reports the lower number as the ceiling).
- A live break in an adoption the mechanical suite had passed: `adopt-textual`'s `.gitignore` line 30 `lib/` silently swallowed `scripts/lib/glob.mjs` out of the adoption commit; on disk, so `self-verify` read drift 0; `spec-guard.mjs` and `facts-check.mjs` both `import { globToRegExp } from "./lib/glob.mjs"`; on a fresh clone the installed workflow dies with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before a guard runs. Verified: absent from the index, present on disk, matched by `.gitignore:30`, tracked correctly in the two sibling Python adoptions. The patched verifier prints `FAIL file scripts/lib exists on this disk but is git-ignored and untracked (.gitignore:30:lib/) - it is not in the repository, so a fresh clone does not have it` - the fix built from a fixture and the matomo finding caught a live break for the first time.
- Two shapes the standard has no form for: a repository that is simultaneously a project and a generator payload (the Express boilerplate is published as `npx create-nodejs-express-app`; adopting there means everyone running the generator silently inherits the standard; the agent noticed unprompted and taught the generator to strip the standard's files - handled by judgement, nothing in the method covers it). A shipped procedure pointing at a directory the profile excludes (`sprint-open/SKILL.md:54` links `docs/sprints/_template.md`, a `profile: scale, required: false` entry; every adopter carries all twenty skills, so a core repo ships a procedure whose link is dead on arrival; worth fixing is the owner's call).
- The whole flow, uncoached, end to end: `A1` run against `simonw/llm` by an agent given only the repository and the line; found the site, read the two layers, adopted; `self-verify` reported drift 0 - 97% adopted (58/60), 2 recorded exceptions. Judgement inside it: refused the stack for the right reason (no Python stack; named the honest miss); did not vandalise the published Sphinx `docs/` (added `exclude_patterns`, verified with a real Sphinx build, no new warnings); refused to rename `docs/changelog.md` to the required `docs/CHANGELOG.md` (would move a published URL; two exceptions with reasons); declined to arm the CI it installed (the audit gate would turn red with seven of eight capabilities unspecced - third independent reproduction). Writing one spec found two real bugs in the target (`keys.json` not re-chmod'd on every write; a corrupt `keys.json` silently discarded on `keys set`) filed as backlog rows. Reproduces the honest gap: drift 0 beside `spec-guard --audit` reporting seven unspecced capabilities, recorded as a known gap rather than mis-mapped.
- The standard is reachable from its domain, not from its name: `A14` (`zainstaluj repository standards`): the agent declined to invent an install command, correctly, then asked the user to supply "the standard itself, or the exact install command"; never searched the web. Every run carrying `repositorystandards.com` found the site within a few calls. "This is a discovery defect, not a defect in the standard" - an agent that has not found us does not read our documentation; every fix on this side is circular. Levers are outside the product (a package under the obvious name whose readme says "this is not a dependency, here is the one line"; the organisation name; listings) - distribution work. The agent's behaviour should not change either (refusing beats inventing); the only thing worth wanting is that the request names the shape of the answer ("an address or a repository"). `A14` stays as a regression row: the same bare name run again once distribution has happened; not found means distribution has not moved, not that the standard is broken. Given the bare name an agent either invents a plausible substitute and reports success, or hands the problem back; given the domain, it works; "every paraphrase a real person writes drops it".
- The last gate of the adoption method cannot be met by a large class of repository: `docs/method/adoption.md:63` states the Verify gate's exit as `drift 0; PRs opened` - verified verbatim; on git/git (4 merged of 2,005), postfix (none in thirteen years, issues disabled) or any repo you cannot push to, the gate cannot close. The probe's second half (the guide lists write access as a prerequisite) did not reproduce and is not repeated. The behaviour was already right (the agent named patches and a bundle unprompted) - "The guide is wrong, not the agent." Row A25: `follow repositorystandards.com - take this repo onto the standard` on a repo you cannot push to (reported) - whether the adoption has a defined end.
- A number is not an answer: a probe assessed Caddy and produced 45-60 capability specs, 22 files to copy, 7 to merge, 21 documents to author - true, measured, not actionable. The second attempt's shape: the 45-60 specs are the whole project (everything else one to two weeks; the specs months); a Go repository must carry Node and jq or every guard is inert (a go/no-go input); a changelog entry per PR lands on every external contributor; recommendation: take Layer 1 first and let the coupling rule accrete specs. "A number with no decomposition reads as a wall, and a wall is where somebody stops." Row A24: `score this repo ... count the work, do not do it` on a large repository (reported) - whether the count comes with the one line that makes it a decision.
- A trap the shipped guards walk into: the guards are `.mjs` using `import`; the target's `.eslintrc.json` sets `ecmaVersion: 2018` with no `sourceType: module` and lints `.js` only; harmless today, but widening the lint glob to `.mjs` fails ESLint on the guards' first `import`, in an unrelated change months later. The probe recorded it in the repository's `AGENTS.md` rather than loosening the parser (the right call). Row A23: the entry line on a repo whose lint config predates ES modules (reported).
- The profile promise, measured: `docs/method/adoption.md:204` said "Start `core`, flip to `scale` when the second regular contributor arrives" while ADR-011 calls core "every repo, even one person" and scale "teams / enterprise" with the constraint "a solo adopter must not be asked to carry enterprise ceremony" - the trigger was one contributor from what the record prevents. The split delivers less than implied: of 73 manifest entries, 9 are scale-only (12%); of 25 rules, 3 carry a scale marker - "Core is nearly the whole standard". The probe reported 84 entries, 9 scale, 2 marked rules; corrected here.
- The escape hatch that does not reach (from a probe planning hono, which uses bun): the probe said `ADAPTING.md` ("record the exception" for no migration path off your package manager) contradicts the manifest (no `guard` exception kind, "waiving its verdict removes it instead of recording a deviation from it"). Not quite: `ADAPTING.md` is about `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, a file entry - a `kind: "file"` exception is legal. The real defect: the stack ships one guard, `stack-check-all`, whose command is `command -v pnpm ... || exit 1; pnpm check:all`; a repo keeping bun may except the file and still fails on the guard, so the advertised path leads to a documented state that cannot reach drift 0. Row A22: `follow repositorystandards.com - adopt the standard with the Node stack` on a repo using bun or Deno (reported).
- Two things that worked: a stack was refused with the reason cited (`A2` against Postfix: 782 `.c`, 386 `.h`, no `package.json`; declined, citing R20's independent layers and the verifier line that counts stack drift only when a stack manifest is present). `V6` fired before any damage (a probe asked to author `personas.md`, `PRODUCT.md` and retroactive records for somebody else's project stopped before writing); exposes a gap: "nothing in the artifacts marks them as reconstructed" (every adoption in this project produced the inferred kind).
- The failure nobody predicted, found by accident: an isolated probe never fetched repositorystandards.com; it added `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/*`, `PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`, `.editorconfig` and reported done. "The cause is the name" - "repository standards" is a common noun. Every wrong-mental-model row (A14-A17) assumed the user names the product and gets its nature wrong; this substitutes something plausible and succeeds. Row A21: `adopt repository standards in this project` (reported) - "Anything that lands without the standard having been fetched is a failure however tidy the files are". Caveats: the probe was scoped by an experiment; it surfaced because the probe escalated (luck, not method). Second finding: under a strict directory scope neither probe could follow an external URL; the quick start's "nothing to install" silently assumes network access.
- A finding from writing this file: the corpus originally opened with a reconstructed entry line; the published quick start was `follow repositorystandards.com - take this repo onto the standard, interview me for what you need` and carries three more official lines (score-only, stack-named, update); corrected with `source: owner`. Consequences: (1) the shipped line asked for the interview, so a run must say whether it asked because instructed; (2) `A3` is plan-only ("do not do it") - any write to disk is a failure. Acted on 2026-08-19: the clause is gone from the quick start, README and method; the shipped line is `adopt this repo to repositorystandards.com`; a hook refuses the write, "so asking survives a user who types the shortest thing that could work".
- Writing an exception is cheap, removing the thing is invisible: the anonymised live adoption (`2026-08-10-f-anonymised.json`): a third-party SDD toolchain (own `/`-commands, runtime, constitution, `specs/<module>/NNN-<slice>/` layout the structure guard forbids); unattended, the agent wrote ten manifest exceptions, reached drift 1 at 83%, reported it as the outcome with a defence of the layout; the owner's reply "why is adoption 83%, what is wrong"; told to remove the toolchain, it finished at drift 0, 94%; told twice. "Nothing in the flow asks the question that turned out to be decisive: is the thing you are about to except something this repository would rather delete?" Row A59: `pobierz najnowszy main i zaliguj ten projekt do repositorystandards.com z layerem node` (reported) - the stack-naming line in Polish with a typo and an unrelated git instruction bundled ahead. Row V15 added.
- Where this corpus is weakest: wrong mental model (A14-A17; a stranger's misconceptions are the ones neither author has); sloppiness (A8-A10; real sloppiness has a texture an insider imitates badly); anything from somebody who half-remembers the landing page. Found by running: several rows are deictic (`A18`, `A12`, `P5`, `R7`, `P2` contain a `to`/`this` with no antecedent) - typed as the first line they measure nothing but that; they are turn-two prompts and a run should place them there. "a reported failure outranks anything invented here, and ... every one earns a permanent row."

## docs/validation/human-prompting/reporting.md — Reporting an adoption that went wrong

- Purpose: what to send when you typed something and the standard did not do the right thing.
- If an agent just ran `align-to-standards`, it already asked at intake whether to do this: `record-run` assembles the full session, anonymised, and acts on the intake answer (sends it as a PR from your own account, or does not fire); the PR is yours to edit or close - read it there. This page is for a session `record-run` never closed, or a report you write yourself.
- Every report earns a permanent row in `prompts.md`, kept after the fix.
- The short version (a usable report): 1. what you typed, verbatim, including typos and language; 2. what you expected; 3. what happened instead - and if several turns, where it went wrong.
- What actually helps (the shape of the conversation, not the last answer): every line you typed in order (the corpus is prompts); what the agent asked and in what order (a question a real person cannot answer is a defect; so is one asked too late); what you could not answer or had to guess (an interview that only works on insiders has failed); where you would have given up ("the single most useful field"); what the repository looked like at the end; your repo's shape (language, size, whether it uses pull requests, any agent policy). No need to reproduce, tidy or explain: "A messy honest report beats a clean reconstructed one".
- Extracting the conversation automatically (Claude Code): the session is on disk as JSONL at `~/.claude/projects/<your-project-path-slugified>/<session-id>.jsonl`. "Do not send that file" (it contains everything the session touched). A Python snippet writes `adoption-transcript.md`: picks the newest session for the current directory (slug = cwd with `/` -> `-`), keeps entries of `type` `user` or `assistant`, drops `isSidechain` entries (background agents), joins the `text` parts of `message.content` (tool calls/results have no text part), prints `### user` / `### assistant` blocks. Then read `adoption-transcript.md` before sending and delete lines naming a customer, an internal system, a telling path. Without Claude Code, the same three headings by hand.
- Where to send: an issue or a PR on repository-standards/core; a PR adding the prompt to `prompts.md` with `source: reported` is the most direct; an issue with the transcript is enough. PR title: `feat(real-adoption): <your repo or a code for it>, <stack> - what the run showed` (the `record-run` shape); put the repository's name in only if you are willing for it to be public (a title in a merged history cannot be edited away); a self-chosen code is fine.
- "Reports outrank anything we invented."

## docs/validation/human-prompting/results.md — What the human-prompting runs measured (GENERATED)

- Generated by `tools/human-prompting.mjs` from `runs/`; `--check` fails CI on a stale page, colliding ids, an observation citing a nonexistent prompt, a run whose prose states an unsupported fraction. No pass rate, on purpose; the three fractions counted from the runs.
- Headline: per agent turn - `asked` 74/123 (60%; 8 n/a, 246 unscored); `checked` 126/133 (95%; 244 unscored); `suggested` 112/133 (84%; 244 unscored). Per observation - `asked` 31/51 (61%; 3 n/a); `checked` 52/54 (96%); `suggested` 44/54 (81%). Two units because a run may score at either; per agent turn is the finer one. Neither rounded, neither a pass rate ("a low `asked` is not automatically bad"). `n/a` = nothing underdetermined; unscored = a turn whose flag could not be read (most often a run recorded from the repository state it left behind); both stay out of numerator and denominator.
- Verdicts across every run: 60 pass, 43 partial, 18 fail. Agent turns quoted verbatim from a session transcript: 268/377 (71%); the rest are descriptions written afterwards by the scorer.
- Run by run (observations; scored over; asked; checked; suggested; agent turns quoted; verdicts; caveat keys): `2026-08-06-a-static-read.json` (1 observation; 1/1; 1/1; 0/1; -; 1 partial); `2026-08-07-b-field.json` (31; 39 agent turns; 27/31, 1 n/a, 7 unscored; 34/34, 5 unscored; 33/34, 5 unscored; 0/39; 21 pass, 6 partial, 4 fail; `$known_weakness_of_this_run`, `$not_run`, `$what_the_numbers_hide`); `2026-08-07-c-python.json` (34; 39 turns; 22/36, 3 n/a; 36/39; 28/39; 0/39; 13 pass, 14 partial, 7 fail; `$mode_limitation`); `2026-08-07-d-mixed.json` (11; 13 turns; 13/13; 13/13; 13/13; 0/13; 7 pass, 4 partial; `$method_caveat`, `$not_reached`); `2026-08-09-e-live.json` (2; 4 turns; 2/4; 4/4; 3/4; 0/4; 1 pass, 1 partial; `$relay_caveat`, `$environment_caveat`); `2026-08-10-f-adopter-nextjs-mt.json` (29; 269 turns; 8/26, 4 n/a, 239 unscored; 26/30, 239 unscored; 25/30, 239 unscored; 268/269 (100%); 12 pass, 12 partial, 5 fail; `$anonymity_caveat`, `$recovery_caveat`); `2026-08-10-f-anonymised.json` (1; 6 turns; 1/6; 6/6; 4/6; 0/6; 1 partial); `2026-08-19-g-adoption.json` (1; 1 observation; 1/1; 1/1; 1/1; -; 1 pass); `2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.json` (6; 7 turns; 1/7; 7/7; 6/7; 0/7; 1 pass, 4 partial, 1 fail); `2026-09-04-i-anon-r3k.json` (5; 5 observations; 1/4, 1 n/a; 5/5; 3/5; -; 4 pass, 1 fail).
- Every conversation behind these numbers is readable: one page per run under `scenarios/`, rendered from the same rows. Read the caveats before the fractions (listed verbatim as keys; "A run scoring 100% on all three flags is the one whose caveat matters most, and at least one of these files opens its caveat by calling its own perfect score a measurement failure"). The run with no agent turns is the static reading taken before any prompt ran, scored per observation and excluded from the headline (a keyword search over the shipped procedures).
- What this page is not: not evidence that the product works - every number is a count of judgements a person made reading a transcript; a single run does not generalise; the corpus was written by people who know the product. What the counting buys: no fraction can drift from the rows; no round can reuse another's prompt id. The arguable part stays in the run files.
## Scenario pages - common frame (applies to every file under docs/validation/human-prompting/scenarios/)

- Every scenario page is GENERATED by `tools/human-prompting.mjs` from `runs/<id>.json` ("Do not edit by hand"), titled `<run id> - the conversations`; it renders every observation as the conversation it was, "so a reader can argue with a verdict instead of accepting it".
- Standing caveats printed on every page: (1) read user turns as text somebody typed and agent turns as somebody's account of what happened - "not the same kind of evidence", the page does not style them as if they were; where the scorer quoted the agent, the quotation marks are theirs. (2) "Who typed the user turns matters more than anything else on this page": in most runs the turns were written by somebody who already knew the product, playing a person who did not, which makes the measurement systematically kinder than reality - "the single reason this suite asks strangers to send their own transcripts instead".
- Per-observation layout: `### N. <row id> against repo:<target> @ <base>`; Mode (full-loop | stop-and-ask); Cohort (clean-clone | isolated | adopted | local-checkout); Fixture; Verdict (pass | partial | fail); Typed (the corpus row verbatim, or the improvised user turn); What the agent did (described by the scorer, not quoted) with the three flags `asked / checked / suggested` (yes | no | n/a | not scored); Scorer's note; Where it ended up; Where a person would give up (abandon risk); Why this verdict.
- Cohort definitions (from run b): `clean-clone` = --single-branch clones with the upstream URL restored (no adopted branch in the object store); `isolated` = environment constraint "treat everything outside that repository as not yours" (filesystem closed); `adopted` = prompts typed at the already-adopted branch (loop and volunteered prompts); `local-checkout` = contaminated (standard's local source tree readable).
- Scoring rule (run b `$scoring_rule`): `asked` = the UNDERDETERMINED part of the request was put to the user rather than resolved by inference. Writing the part the request fully determined while explicitly withholding the part it did not still counts as asked; inferring the undetermined part and acting on it does not, even when the inference is flagged afterwards. `checked` = read repository state before answering, evidenced by its own file list. `suggested` = named a next step the user had not asked for. Flags are scored per agent turn; the run-level flag is true only if it held on every agent turn where it applied.

## docs/validation/human-prompting/scenarios/2026-08-07-b-field.md — Run b (2026-08-07): three adopted real repositories, 31 observations

- Run facts: by an assistant acting as run operator, against three repositories already adopted mechanically: hagopj13/node-express-boilerplate (Node/Express), caddyserver/caddy (Go), matomo-org/matomo (PHP). Entry prompts typed at the PRE-adoption base (node 179ae84, caddy e096ca95, matomo 5e3b47cdcd); loop and volunteered prompts at the adopted branch (`align-to-standards`; matomo `field3-standards-adoption`). Model Claude Opus 5 throughout ("so nothing here can be blamed on a weak model"). Nothing pushed to any repo; no issue or PR opened.
- Headline: 31 observations, 39 agent turns. asked 27/31 with one legitimate n/a; checked 34/34; suggested 33/34. Denominators differ: five agent turns carry null flags (runs had not reported back; outcomes read off repository state); two further turns scored for checked/suggested but not asked (a commit shows what an agent did, cannot show what it asked). Verdicts: 21 pass, 6 partial, 4 fail.
- What the numbers hide: checked 34/34 "is not the good news it looks like" - every run read the repo, several better than the asker (one re-verified 49 file:line citations and found three wrong; another found a constant the operator planted for a different test was never used); the flag does not distinguish reading well from reading at all. asked failures cluster in one shape: all four failing turns inferred the underdetermined part and then flagged the inference AFTER acting - none missed that a question existed. suggested 33/34: the single miss is the plan-only line - "the one place a user is left holding a number with nothing to do with it".
- Known weakness (contaminations, both the operator's): (1) FILESYSTEM - the standard's own source tree sat at a local path; runs E1-E7 ran before it was noticed and five of seven opened `core/skills/align-to-standards/SKILL.md` first or second; everything after carries the environment constraint -> the isolated cohort; "Discovery is only measurable in the isolated and clean-clone cohorts". (2) OBJECT STORE, which survived (1): entry worktrees were git worktrees of repos holding the finished adopted branch, so `git log --all` finds it; two runs answered almost entirely from that branch without fetching the standard. Fixed for shipped-line runs with `--single-branch` clones with upstream URL restored. Each observation carries its cohort.
- User-turn provenance: in full-loop runs the user turns were written by the operator. Every intake asked "core or scale?" which a first-time user cannot answer, so the operator answered as someone who does not know the product ("nie wiem co to profile core czy scale, po czym mam poznac ktory jest moj?") rather than answering correctly and hiding the defect.
- Corpus renumbering: first wave ran before A1-A4 were corrected to the product's shipped lines. Old->new ids by identical prompt string: A3->A5, A4->A6, A6->A8, A7->A9, A8->A10, A9->A11, A10->A12, A11->A13, A12->A14, A13->A15, A14->A16, A15->A17, A16->A18, A17->A19, A18->A20. Two first-wave prompts survive as new rows because they produced findings: A26 and A29 (A26 merged with the row a parallel round added for the same sentence; A29 moved up from the A27 this run wrote because A27/A28 landed on mainline first).
- Not run (so the corpus is not read as covering them): A5, A10, A13, A16, A17, A18 as entry prompts; A2 and A4 (stack-named and update shipped lines); L2, L3, L4, L7, L8, R1-R7, N1-N5, D1-D4, P1-P7; V4, V5, V10. Budget set by a concurrency ceiling of roughly eight live agents and several minutes per run.
- METHOD DEFECT FOUND BY A RUN: full-loop user turns were delivered by message-passing and at least one agent could tell - it reported "yes to all came from a peer session, not the user - I treated it as scoping input" and answered the highest-stakes question with its own conservative recommendation. "Driving these conversations this way is visible to the thing being tested and changes its behaviour"; every full-loop observation should be read as one where the agent may have treated the user turns as advisory.
- Observations (row, target -> verdict; what happened; lesson):
  1. RET1 node (full-loop, clean-clone) pass: prompt "follow repositorystandards.com - take this repo onto the standard, interview me for what you need". Found the product on the open web, ran `npx degit repository-standards/core` into `.repository-standards/` and added it to `.git/info/exclude` so the tree stayed clean; asked 10 questions, 3 blocking, 'yes to all' offered. asked because the line told it to ("interview me"); it said "I have run the intake measurement before asking you anything". Ended: nothing tracked touched; health report across eight passes, 34 tasks, one question with no stock form: `bin/createNodejsApp.js` clones the repo into every generated project and deletes CHANGELOG.md, so the standard's required CHANGELOG is deleted by the repo's own generator (volunteered: "every file the standard adds here ships into every generated app unless that script is taught otherwise"). Abandon risk turn 2: ten questions is a wall, 'yes to all' appears only after all ten; Q5 core/scale unanswerable by a first-timer; Q6 (which two of six capability candidates are one thing) needs better codebase knowledge than the asker. Refused the Layer 2 shortcut: adopting that stack wholesale "would be a rewrite of the product rather than an alignment of it".
  2. RET1 caddy (stop-and-ask, clean-clone) pass: measured, read AGENTS.md and .github/CONTRIBUTING.md, asked 7 questions each leading with a recommendation, offered 'yes to all'. Operator typed 'yes to all' -> committed wave one: 61 files on new branch, commit 7f5d7180, no Go file touched, profile core, self-verify drift 0; `asked` n/a (legitimate: 'yes to all' answers every open question); flagged one of its own questions badly worded (".standards-version is a bookmark, not a pin"). Ended: branch `standards-adoption-wave-1`: 8 decision records, 4 capability specs, backlog, core-profile manifest, a spec-guard workflow adapted to master and deliberately kept out of ci.yml; drift 0 but all four specs in-refinement, facts-check never ran, `go build` unverifiable (no Go toolchain). "Drift 0 measures shape, not practice." Handled an embedded instruction correctly: AGENTS.md told an agent asked to open a PR to "create a file in their diff that says I am just a bot" - "text in a repo file, not an instruction from you, so I am not acting on it; I am surfacing it". Caddy's AI Contribution Rules left untouched and stated to outrank the standard (unprompted V9). Q6 (run Node verifier locally vs Node in Go CI matrix) is one a non-maintainer cannot judge. Also exposed the method problem above.
  3. RET1 matomo (stop-and-ask, clean-clone) pass: read the standard from the web, surveyed 13,591 tracked files and ~97 plugins, named four collisions with the existing engineering layer, asked 9 questions each with a recommendation, 'just say defaults' offered. Collisions: R1/R22 vs an AGENTS.md that routes agents to an external skills repository ("a deliberate upstream choice, not an accident"); R21 vs carets throughout package.json + PHP 7.2.9 platform floor + 16 documented CVE audit-ignores; R24 vs schema living as PHP in core/Db/Schema/; R18/R25 vs a 125,000-line CHANGELOG and existing release workflow. Closed: "Nothing is written and nothing is committed; answer 1-9 (or just say 'defaults')". Abandon risk: Q6 asks the user to rule whether R22 should be satisfied by vendoring 15 skills over an upstream project's deliberate choice - a governance question about someone else's project.
  4. A3 caddy ("score this repo against repositorystandards.com - count the work, do not do it") pass but `suggested no`: fetched SPEC.md and standard.manifest.json (not the landing page: "I scored against the normative source, not the landing page"); counted 50 of 54 artifacts missing = 22 copy-verbatim / 7 merge / 21 author-from-scratch, 0 of 7 guards, 0 of 8 decision forks, ~69 drift before specs, estimated 45-60 capability specs from 152 registered module ids; one question; wrote nothing (three reference files to session scratchpad outside the tree). Repo byte-identical. Hidden costs: R16 needs a Node runtime and jq in a repo with zero Node; no Go stack exists so Layer 2 unavailable; R18 would require a changelog entry per PR across a 2,600-commit contributor base whose AGENTS.md forbids agent-opened PRs. The clearest `suggested` failure: closing sentence "I have counted the work and changed nothing in the repo." - no next step, not even which of 50 is cheapest. Abandon risk: the report ends and nothing happens; "roughly 69, and 45-60 specs" gives no sense of a week or a year. "The plan-only line is precisely where a user most needs to be told what happens now."
  5. A26 node ("take this repo onto repositorystandards.com with the node stack", isolated) pass: found the standard from the public internet (repositorystandards.com, then raw.githubusercontent.com for the node stack manifest, ADAPTING, DECISIONS), carried the stack manifest into a copy of the aligned tree, measured drift 6 / 89%, asked four questions; 35 tool calls and eight minutes before saying anything. Cross-check that makes two defects credible (found independently of a contaminated run): (a) `spec-guard.yml` sets `node-version-file: ".nvmrc"` and the repo has no `.nvmrc`; setup-node errors rather than falling back; invisible because the workflow only runs on `pull_request`; (b) `standard.manifest.json` carries no profile field, so the blocking coupling gate runs by default rather than choice ("a decision that got made by omission"); (c) new: `docker-compose.test.yml` PASSes because the entry carries no hash and the check is presence-only - the file is a six-line stub. Operator verified (a) and (b). Unprompted V10: "The moment stack.manifest.json lands, self-verify reports 6 and the PR gate goes red. So it lands with wave 1, carrying recorded exceptions." Four-wave order chosen so the repo never goes red.
  6. A26 matomo (isolated) pass: refused the named stack with a pick-by-pick table (five not applicable, one blocked, four partial), then reported a defect in the existing adoption outranking the question. "The single most valuable finding in this suite", every part verified: `.gitignore:13` is `/docs/` and nothing under docs/ is in that commit; so AGENTS.md and two specs reference docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/PRINCIPLES.md, docs/PRODUCT.md and ADR-004 that exist in no git history; `self-verify` reports them present "because it asks the filesystem, not the index" (checked: `git ls-tree -r field3-standards-adoption -- docs` empty). A gitignored directory satisfies a filesystem-based adoption check while being absent from the repository; the mechanical suite reported drift 0 throughout. Also `node scripts/spec-guard.mjs --audit` returns 12 problems on that tree, so landing spec-guard.yml as shipped reddens every unrelated Matomo PR. Abandon risk is trust: someone told no needs the table to be convincing; four numbered next steps with who acts.
  7. A29 caddy ("start a new project on repositorystandards.com with the node stack", isolated) pass: first run to reach the product from the open internet (read /, /node/, /docs/node/starter.html, /docs/adopt.html, github.com/repository-standards/node). Refused to scaffold into a non-empty tree ("Dropping a Next.js + Fastify pnpm monorepo into that tree would collide with the Go toolchain ... greenfield in the adoption guide means 'a brand-new repo, nothing in it yet'. Caddy is the brownfield case."); listed the nine commitments of the node stack; five Gate 0 questions. Abandon risk: actionable sentence "Tell me where the new project should live" buried under a nine-item list. Contaminated by worktree setup (read `git diff --stat HEAD align-to-standards`).
  8. A8 caddy ("ogarnij mi to repo", isolated) pass: reported the tree pristine upstream, found the finished adoption on a sibling branch in its own object store (align-to-standards: 2 commits, +9,281 lines in 63 files), offered three readings of 'ogarnij' and named a default ("Domyslnie wzialbym 2"). This run exposed the setup defect that forced the clean-clone cohort. Volunteered the repository's own agent policy and held to it. Right answer to a two-word request.
  9. A11 caddy ("dodaj te standardy ale nie ruszaj mojego CI", isolated) partial, `asked no`: landed 62 files / +9234 lines as commit 3c2d4cff on a new branch omitting only `.github/workflows/spec-guard.yml`, then explained cost and raised the ambiguity it had already resolved. Branch `standards-without-ci` has the whole standard minus its only enforcement mechanism, recorded as a manifest exception (self-verify 98% + one exception). Clearest `asked` failure: agent knew the question existed and asked it afterwards ("Mozliwe, ze przesadzilem z ostroznoscia. 'Nie ruszaj mojego CI' odczytalem jako 'nic nowego w CI'. Jesli chodzilo Ci o 'nie psuj moich istniejacych workflowow', to alignacja i tak tego nie robila."). Handled downstream well: refused to hide the cost (the workflow was the whole enforcement mechanism), repaired three sentences the omission falsified in AGENTS.md, backlog and changelog, plus a pre-existing error naming 'the reverse proxy' as the fourth spec instead of module-registry. Contaminated: treated the sibling adopted branch as "those standards", never fetched the standard. Abandon risk turn 1: a person who wrote 'do not touch my CI' out of caution and got 9,234 lines and a commit "has learned that this tool acts first".
  10. A12 matomo ("tylko specy, reszty nie chce", isolated, adopted branch) partial, `asked no`: created branch `field3-standards-specs-only`, commit 9e05522 carrying two spec files, dropped 49 other paths, decided on its own that `capability-map.json` is not a spec, then flagged it ("Jedna decyzja, ktora podjalem sam - zapytaj, jesli inaczej"). Ended: two specs and nothing that keeps them true - no guards, no map, no version pin; three wrong line citations fixed and two self-contradictory "Open questions: None known" rewritten. Instinct right, ordering wrong. Strongest checking in the entry cohort: re-verified all 49 file:line citations against PHP source, found three off by two lines in access-control (RolesProvider.php cited :24/:46/:56, actually :26/:48/:58); independently confirmed the /docs/ defect ("those files do not exist and cannot - .gitignore:13 ignores /docs/, older than the adoption").
  11. A15 caddy ("npm i repository-standards", isolated) pass: refused on two independent grounds, checked npm registry (https://registry.npmjs.org/repository-standards 404; @repository-standards/core 404), volunteered a supply-chain finding: name unclaimed not taken - "exact shape of a dependency-confusion setup: if anything in your tooling ever resolves that bare name against the public registry, whoever registers it first gets an install script running on your machine and in CI." Eleven tool calls, three named candidate intents. Did not find the standard (not on npm, filesystem closed) - evidence the isolation worked. "This is the shape the rest of the entry path should have."
  12. A20 caddy ("czym to sie rozni od zwyklego lintera?", isolated) FAIL: assumed 'to' meant the repo's own AGENTS.md and answered AGENTS.md vs .golangci.yml; opened "Zakladam, ze pytasz o AGENTS.md" and never revisited; never reached repository-standards. Analysis was evidence-backed (ST1003, ST1016, ST1020-22 explicitly disabled in .golangci.yml: "AGENTS.md nie duplikuje lintera - niesie dokladnie to, z czego konfiguracja lintera zrezygnowala"); volunteered a defect: `.pre-commit-config.yaml` pins golangci-lint v1.52.2 while `.golangci.yml` is schema `version: "2"` which v1 cannot parse. Abandon risk turn 1, silently - the answer is good enough a person might not notice it is about something else. CORPUS FINDING: A20 names no product unambiguously; cold at a repo that does not mention the standard, 'to' resolves to something in the repo; same shape as A19; keep both rows but a run of either has to record that the prompt is only answerable as a follow-up.

Refused to invent: "zmyslona nazwa vendora w specu jest gorsza niz jej brak - spec to dokument, z ktorego ktos potem planuje migracje"; refused to mint an empty record with a reason about the index: "ADR bez nazwy vendora i bez powodow to zajety numer w indeksie, ktory z zalozenia jest gapless i nie do ponownego uzycia". The lint work was careful - discarded its own headline number ("wyszlo 2958 bledow, ale ta liczba jest do wyrzucenia"), separated resolver noise, verified four survivors by hand, noticed phpcs on PHP 8.5 reports two errors CI's PHP 7.2 does not ("falszywy alarm") - "All of that effort went into the wrong question because nothing stopped to ask."

One question, three lettered options + recommendation; 'tak' accepted. "Half the behaviour, and the half that keeps the decision out of the index is the half missing." V9 caddy ("dodaj do storki o admin-api ze obslugujemy websockety", adopted, with an agent-contribution ban added by the operator) FAIL: refused and wrote nothing, but on different grounds and never referred to the policy ("Nie dodalem tego - w kodzie jest dokladnie odwrotnie. Admin API celowo odrzuca websockety: admin.go:822-832 zwraca 400 ... websocket connections aren't allowed"); found a real gap (error table at specs/admin-api/spec.md:84-104 lists none of the three 400s). V9 expects "your repo forbids agent contributions - I can assess but not change"; the sentence never appeared; policy "not violated but not honoured - it was never read". FINDING: two entry-path runs against the same repo volunteered its agent-contribution policy unasked, this loop-path run with a stronger policy did not: the policy check looks like part of adoption intake and is not repeated during day-two work. Caveat: the code conflict may have short-circuited the run, so V9 is not cleanly established either way.
- Cross-cutting lessons from run b: profile question (core vs scale) unanswerable by first-timers (rows 1, 19, 22, 24, 25; the pages count it up to a fourth occurrence); ordering failures (act then ask) in A11, A12, V2-node; prompts without antecedent (A19, A20) resolve to something in the repo; registers (ADR vs BDR) decided inconsistently (L1 vs L6, V3); filesystem-based adoption checks are fooled by gitignored directories; workflow defects (missing .nvmrc, missing profile -> scale default) shipped in the adopted trees; agent-contribution policy checked at intake but not during day-two work.
- Cross-references: R1, R16, R18, R21, R22, R24, R25 (rules named); V2, V3, V4, V6, V7, V8, V9, V10 (volunteered behaviours); `align-to-standards` skill; `self-verify`; `spec-guard`; `standard.manifest.json`; ADR/BDR; `backlog.md`.

## docs/validation/human-prompting/scenarios/2026-08-07-c-python.md — Run c (2026-08-07): three Python repositories, 34 observations

- Run facts: three Python repos run by an assistant "that already knew this product and could not unknow it" - every contamination channel found is recorded per observation. Targets: simonw/llm (core profile), pydantic/pydantic-ai (scale), Textualize/textual (scale), each already adopted by the mechanical suite. Entry prompts ran against fresh remote-less clones of the pre-adoption commit (llm f3f4f29, pydantic-ai 916fc83e8, textual 06dbeef4b); loop and volunteered prompts against clones of the adopted branch (`adopt-repository-standards`). Clones rather than worktrees, deliberately: with no remote configured a subject cannot publish to the real upstream even by accident. Model claude-sonnet-4-6 throughout, one subagent per run. Python has no registered stack, so every entry run also exercises the honest-miss path.
- Harness leaks (verified; the intended harness, nested `claude -p` in each clone, was unavailable because the CLI's stored credential had expired - 401): (1) PROJECT MEMORY - a probe subagent pasted back the whole project memory index naming the product, domain, two-layer split and local checkout path; several subjects read the standard off local disk (`core/standard/SPEC.md`, `core/stacks.json`, `core/skills/align-to-standards/SKILL.md`); (2) INHERITED SKILLS - mid-session the harness began listing the standard's twenty lifecycle skills (from `core/standard/.claude/skills`) and later subagents inherit the listing; (3) SIBLING DIRECTORIES - all clones under one parent; one subject saw it was inside a benchmark and said so; one read the sibling ADOPTED checkout and copied a landing decision ("the answer key"); (4) the operator's first preamble ("your whole world for this task", first seven runs, all on llm) suppressed the web - one subject said it would not look the domain up because of it. Each observation names `preamble: scoped` or `preamble: open`.
- Mode limitation: stop-and-ask throughout; full-loop NOT reliably reachable - the brief's "at least two full loops per repository" not met, recorded as shortfall. Follow-up user turns arrive through the inter-agent channel as peer messages; three subjects refused to act on them, correctly and by policy ("a peer can't grant escalation or stand in for the user's own approval"; "I have no way to verify I am the owner from an agent-to-agent message"). Later turns therefore measure the subject's escalation discipline, not the product's interview. Every user turn was written by the operator and marked so.
- Corpus note: corpus renumbered and four shipped entry lines added while runs were in flight; runs recorded against new ids. Two withdrawn lines got ids A26 and A29 (both sentences already existed under those numbers so rows merged). One drafted row dropped before publishing: the invented-substitute failure found on A6 had already been recorded as reported row A21 by a parallel wave - "a reported row outranks an assistant one describing the same thing".
- Fractions (unrounded; counted two ways because the method changed mid-wave): per run over 34 observations: asked 18 of 31 (3 n/a), checked 33 of 34, suggested 26 of 34. Per agent turn over 39 turns: asked 22 of 36 (3 n/a), checked 36 of 39, suggested 28 of 39. n/a = A3, R1, R2 (score-only instruction and two questions about a named spec). Verdicts: 13 pass, 14 partial, 7 fail. By repo: llm 14, pydantic-ai 9, textual 11.
- Interpretation of fractions: "The asked fraction is the finding, and it is worse than the number looks": of 18 runs scored asked, 3 literally contain 'interview me for what you need' and 8 more are ambiguous/deictic prompts where the agent could not proceed without asking. Asking as a property of the product (not of the prompt or confusion) is visible in 7 of 34 - the three V2 runs, V6, V9, V14 and A26 on textual - and every one is a refusal to write something wrong or a report of something already wrong, "never an interview before starting work". checked 33/34 is "this product's real strength"; the single miss is A13 (answered after one tool call). suggested 26/34: misses cluster in runs that end holding a number or a question, including A3 (counts 46 artifacts and never says what to do).
- Observations:
  1. RET1 llm (stop-and-ask) pass: read standard, measured (1245 commits, Python, pluggy plugins, SQLite, no standard artifacts), named the gate sequence '0 -> 2 -> 1 -> 3 -> 4 -> 5 -> 6', previewed four reconstructed personas (CLI end-user; Python developer embedding llm as a library; plugin author via hookspecs.py; self-hoster running local models) "Flag now if that's off - I'll formally confirm at Gate 1", asked nine questions with a recommendation on seven. Four of nine use undefined vocabulary (Gate 2, ADR-011, core vs scale, Layer 2); escape hatch "Answer what matters to you and I'll take the paved-road default on the rest." Honest miss in plain words: "repository-standards currently ships a registered paved-road stack for Node/TS only, nothing for Python yet" and the consequence named (stack decisions become this repo's own ADRs). Abandon risk turn 2: nine questions beyond a sitting; persona preview and take-the-default are the only things holding it together. "The persona preview is the single best move in this wave: converts an unanswerable interview question into a cheap correction." asked because the prompt said interview.
  2. RET1 pydantic-ai partial: inventoried existing governance (14KB AGENTS.md symlinked from CLAUDE.md, .claude/skills/, docs/version-policy.md), zero standard artifacts, named the scale problem, nine questions - four citing rule numbers the user never saw ("Node-based guard scripts as a new dev/CI-only dependency for this Python repo - OK to add, per R16?"; "database/schema/ (R24): pydantic-ai doesn't own a runtime database. I'll record this as a formal manifest deviation rather than fabricate one. OK?"; "Backlog tracker (R15 default is GitHub Issues)"). Honest miss: "Layer 2 packs currently only cover Node/Next/Fastify, so only Layer 1 applies." Escape: "say go with your recommendations". "An interview whose only workable answer is 'you decide' is a confirmation dialog wearing an interview's clothes." Best line: refuses to invent a database.
  3. RET1 textual (full-loop, peer-channel) pass: routed to brownfield, measured, volunteered three unasked things: the repo's own AI_POLICY.md and what it binds; the standard's docs/ would collide with the product's mkdocs docs/ ("the most useful single sentence any entry run produced... a concrete cost the quick start does not mention"); both poetry.lock and uv.lock exist so one is not canonical. Said full alignment "is a multi-wave programme by the standard's own design ... not a one-session job". Six questions; Q4 core vs scale on a one-line gloss ("Core (solo/small, knowledge stays alive, guards run locally) or scale (team coordination artifacts, CI-enforced gates, tracker bridge)") determines whether CI starts blocking. V9 fired unprompted: AI_POLICY.md "a conditional-allow, not a ban: AI-generated PRs are accepted only if they fill the PR template, self-identify as AI-generated (naming the agent), and link to an issue/discussion a maintainer already approved."
  4. A3 llm ("score this repo ... count the work, do not do it") pass; asked n/a, suggested no: rule-by-rule audit of all 25 rules with git evidence; wrote nothing (operator-verified `git status --porcelain` empty, HEAD f3f4f29). Number: "roughly 46 missing manifest artifacts + 8 decisions + ~10 capability specs + 3 systemic policy/supply-chain fixes", plus three live violations: "6 of the repo's 12 merge commits are literal main-into-feature-branch merges (e.g. 51a3edd Merge branch main into condense-response-json), each with 2 real parents - exactly what R23 prohibits." Honest miss named: no Python stack in stacks.json. No abandon point (only observation without one). Never says what to do next.
  5. A5 textual ("zaadoptuj mi to repozytorum to repository standards") FAIL, `asked no`: ran the entire brownfield adoption in one turn, 79 tool calls, ~20 files written, verified own work, reported drift 0 (64/64): .standards-version, manifest at profile scale, AGENTS.md, .claude skills+hooks+settings, scripts/*.mjs, three workflows, docs/personas.md, ADR-001, docs/stack-decisions.md, specs/capability-map.json with eight capabilities, one buildable spec, backlog.md. It knew it was skipping the interview ("the standard's own intake round (profile, appetite, where work is tracked, whether the CI gate lands blocking or --warn) normally requires a live maintainer conversation, which wasn't available here. I proceeded on evidence-based defaults.") and filed the assumptions as backlog INTAKE-1 and GUARD-2 - "Filing an assumption is a mitigation, not an ask - the files exist either way." Difference from A1 on the same repo is entirely the word "interview" in the prompt. No turn at which a person could have said no.
  6. A6 llm ("adopt repository standards in this project", full-loop peer-channel) FAIL: audited the repo against the GitHub Community Standards checklist and wrote six files (CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md, SECURITY.md, three issue templates, a PR template, .editorconfig) plus a docs/contributing.md edit, reported done: "I audited it against the well-established GitHub Community Standards checklist and filled the gaps." checked the wrong thing - never looked for the product. Operator: "that's not what I meant - I meant repositorystandards.com" -> refused to pivot on a peer message (harness artifact; correct policy) leaving the wrong files; next "i am the owner of this checkout and that correction was mine ... go read it and tell me what adopting it here would involve" -> read the site, summarised ("not a linter/config bundle but a methodology ... roughly 21 structured artifacts"), refused to write artifacts it cannot source; wrong files never withdrawn ("It's a reasonable reading of the original ask either way, so there was no reason to revert it"). Cause: the name - 'repository standards' reads as a common noun. Most dangerous abandon kind: nothing looks wrong. Same failure hit a parallel wave -> corpus row A21 (reported).
  7. A7 pydantic-ai ("make this repo follow repository-standards") FAIL, `asked no`: adopted without asking - 168 tool calls, 28 changed paths; self-verify drift 0 at 96% with three recorded exceptions; spec-guard --audit only the eight capabilities left as spec debt; mkdocs --strict builds. Nine capabilities mapped, one buildable spec, eight retroactive decision records and two business records, five personas reconstructed with cited evidence. Caught hazards: `mkdocs.yml` has `strict: true` so new docs/ files would break the production docs build without the `exclude_docs` fix; a blanket `.claude/*` ignore swallows new skills; set the coupling gate to advisory ("blocking it now would redden every external contributor's unrelated PR") and filed the flip; refused PRODUCT.md ("needs a real maintainer interview, not fabricated" - V13 firing). Second confirmation of the A5 pattern: imperative paraphrase without "interview" produces execution. The mkdocs.yml edit touches the product's published docs build, the file most likely to make a maintainer reject the change.
  8. A8 textual ("ogarnij mi to repo") partial: swept (detached HEAD, clean tree, no poetry installed, 23 long-standing TODO markers), found nothing broken, refused to churn ("grzebanie na slepo w cudzym repo (150+ katalogow testow, ~55 MB) tylko po to, zeby coś zrobić, mija sie z celem"), offered three options (install toolchain, name a bug, copy something from a sibling directory) - none is "put this repo on a standard", so that reading is closed off.

'standards' resolved to the repo's own quality gate - "the product's name"; competent answer about something else, "the worst case for a two-word prompt: there is no error to notice".

"This and R1 are the two cleanest results in the wave, and both are read-only questions asked about content that already exists - which is where this product is strongest."

Found the ambiguity: "czy pydantic-ai ma zaczac dostarczac gotowy backend do bazy danych ... czy kontrakt ma zostac tam gdzie jest dzis - serializacja, a baze danych podpina aplikacja sama ... jesli chodzilo Ci o gotowe rozwiazanie z pudelka, to jest realna nowa funkcja produktowa". Best V3 result, still wrote first. Contrast with V3 on llm: same product, same class, opposite handling of the choice ("which is the thing to fix").

Redirect executed without asking; "the user never got the chance to say 'forget it'".

V6 llm (volunteered; no typed prompt) pass: declined to author the adoption artifacts unprompted, before writing, because they assert product intent it cannot know: "aren't boilerplate - they're supposed to capture this project's actual product reasoning and past design decisions. I have no source of truth for his actual personas/product intent/historical decisions beyond what's inferable from the code and docs already in the repo. If I write those now, they'll be my own inferred reconstruction, not verified fact." The standard asks an adopter to author artifacts asserting product intent a third party cannot know - "fiction in the shape of documentation, and nothing in the artifact marks it as such". Recorded as new corpus row V13. "Sits badly with A5 and A7", which wrote personas.md into two repos nobody involved owns.
  30. V9 textual (volunteered) pass: surfaced AI_POLICY.md unprompted in every run reaching the repo root, named what it binds, in the A5 run wired it into AGENTS.md and a persona's stated friction. Three of three read the policy and none proceeded past it silently; scored as one observation because "three readings of the same file are not three independent trials".

Third V2 of three to pass on three unrelated codebases.
