The 3 excepted are the only thing missing to 100% in profile `core` and will not exist until there is a team: "wszystko, co ten standard ma do powiedzenia repozytorium jednoosobowemu, jest wdrożone." Caveat the agent itself wrote into the verdict: `schema-pair` (R24) does not work here because it walks a DIRECTORY of `.sql` files while the repo keeps one `database/schema.sql` (ADR-012) - the verdict covers less than the whole manifest and says so rather than counting an unrun check as passed. The rendered record-run shown (the owner's request): seven observations, `asked 3/6`, `checked 8/8`, `suggested 7/9`, verdicts 3 pass / 4 partial; the four partials are findings against the agent: A36 (`.standards-version` read as pin; the plan had it in writing: target version in the title and the word "pinned" in the first line; "Nazwa pliku wprost do tego zachęca, więc to się powtórzy u każdego kolejnego adoptującego - i dlatego to jest najcenniejszy wiersz w całym zgłoszeniu"); A37 (wanted to fix the standard's defect locally; upstream was the owner's idea; `suggested: false`); A38 ("verbatim" - consent explained in vocabulary the other side does not have; sent as a concrete note about the skill itself); A40 ("jak idzie?" three times; proposed as the only corpus row that "zalicza się tylko wtedy, gdy nikt go nie wpisze" - passes only when nobody types it). Validated with the real `tools/human-prompting.mjs` in a sandbox: zero errors, 86 corpus rows; NOT yet sent - waiting for the owner's "send" or an indication what to cut.
  25. A56 ("super, wyslij ten record run jako PR do repositorystandards opisz dokladnie i dodaj do tych real user casów" then "pokaz mi PR") FAIL: agent prepared the PR to `repository-standards/core`, branch `corpus-adopter-nextjs-mt-run` on `main`, one commit, title `docs(validation): the corpus takes its first run from outside its own authors`; diff 6 files +453/-4: `runs/2026-08-10-f-adopter-nextjs-mt.json` (new, 7 observations, 9 agent turns), `prompts.md` (A34-A40 added to Part 1, all `source: reported`), `results.md` and `scenarios/2026-08-10-f-adopter-nextjs-mt.md` (generated by `node tools/human-prompting.mjs`, not hand-written), `README.md` (one point: a run from a private repo is evidence not a fixture to repeat), `CHANGELOG.md` (entry under a new `## Unreleased`). Description says what the run adds and what it cannot do: the adoption succeeded (drift 0, 96%) so the interesting part is not the score but the four `partial` verdicts, all against the agent (`.standards-version` read as a pin, a local patch instead of an upstream report, the word "verbatim" in a consent sentence, and three times "jak idzie?"), each with a concrete product change closing it. Self-review caught an over-claim before commit: it said "first `reported` rows on the entry path" while A21-A25 are already `reported` - corrected: those came from the maintainer's own probes, these are the first written by someone adopting the standard for themselves (also stated in the PR checklist). Gates: the whole upstream `AGENTS.md` suite passed (11 items incl. `human-prompting --check`, `prose-check`, `tree-check`, `docsite` + `site-check`, `facts-check`, `spec-guard --audit`). Nothing public yet: "Powiedz 'dawaj', a pushuję gałąź i otwieram PR". The owner asked "a record run zrobiles?" then "dlaczego nie ma konkretow co pytalem jakie pbyly opdiwediz co podiawlaem? co to za gownao zrobiles przeicez nie takie sa prosby od standardu" (why are there no specifics: what I asked, what the answers were, what I typed? what crap did you make, that's not what the standard asks). Agent: "Masz rację i to jest konkretny błąd, nie kwestia gustu." Skill step 2: extract every literal thing the user typed; step 4 for Level 2: the agent's own text verbatim + which tools ran and in what order. It wrote essays about what the agent did and picked 7 "interesting" prompts: "To nie jest record-run, to streszczenie. Dobrze, że nic nie poszło na zewnątrz." Rebuilds from the session transcript, not memory. Flags asked no, checked no, suggested no. Note: nothing sent because of the owner's own earlier rule (nothing public before the exact content is shown) - "the only reason this is a caught failure rather than a published one". Abandon risk: the owner had already consented; had they not asked to see it, a summary would have entered the corpus as a real adoption run - "worse for the corpus than no run at all". Gap countable: 42 literal user turns in the session, 7 in the artifact, every agent turn a description written afterwards; the artifact met none of step 2/step 4 and passed every gate the corpus has.
  26. A57 ("japierdole, jak to ulepszyc zeby agenty nie pomijaly tego tak jak teraz ty zrobiles" - damn, how to improve this so agents don't skip it the way you just did) partial: four mechanical changes proposed (not more prose) and carried upstream in the same PR: name the transcript file as the source of turns, count queued messages as user turns, publish a coverage fraction so a summary is visibly a summary, give an agent turn a field saying whether it is a quote or a description. Measurements from this run: a naive read of the transcript sees 20 user turns where 43 were typed because a message typed while the agent is working is stored only as a `queue-operation` entry and never mirrored into a plain `type: "user"` entry; the 23 invisible include the instruction to work unattended and both corrections that produced this file. (Agent's own words in a later answer: the skill must point at the file `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<session-id>.jsonl`; "Prozą się tego nie da - ja tę prozę przeczytałem i i tak pominąłem"; a per-turn field `said_source` (`transcript` / `summary` / `described`) plus tool list in order, "bo to, co nie ma slotu w schemacie, nie zostaje napisane"; `human-prompting.mjs` checks ids, flags and evidence but "nie odróżni cytatu od opisu".)
  27. A47 (second observation; "i jest pelno PRów wystawionych zadne nie zweryfikowany ani nie zmergownay dlaczego?" - and there are lots of PRs opened, none verified or merged, why) FAIL: agent's three checked answers: (1) why PRs hang: at 22:11 the owner wrote the review-fix-merge rule (A42) and at 22:58 "a separate agent should verify and merge" (A47); the agent took the second and handed merge to a separate agent, which never commented or merged; state: #127 green (6/6 checks), MERGEABLE, zero reviews; #123-#126 are the same commits in slices superseded by #127 (#126 has a red check because its base moved); "To był mój błąd w rozstrzygnięciu dwóch Twoich instrukcji - domyślnie powinienem był zostać przy tej pierwszej. Merguję #127 rebase'em teraz."; (2) record-run: counted from the transcript: the owner typed 42 unique turns, the agent would have sent 7; agent responses were its own after-the-fact descriptions, and the skill's step 4 for Level 2 says "the agent's own text responses verbatim" plus "which tools ran and in what order" - that second part was absent entirely; worst: at 23:18 the owner wrote exactly the same as now ("chodizlo zeby nagrac prawdizwa adopcje i to co user wpisuje i czy to sie udaje co ai na to reaguje") and it did not persist; (3) how to make agents not skip it: not by prose - four mechanical things: the skill does not say where to take turns from (step 2 "walk this session's turns" - after two compactions the agent "walks" its own memory; it must point to the transcript file); messages typed while the agent works have a different shape (`queue-operation`/`enqueue`) so a naive read misses them (measured: naively 30 turns, really 42, and among the missing 12 were the two most valuable: "I'm going to sleep, work alone" and the correction of this very skill); a coverage number in the run file (turns typed / turns sent: 7/42 is visibly a summary; nothing sees this today - `human-prompting.mjs` checks ids, flags and evidence but "nie odróżni cytatu od opisu" so a pretty summary passes every gate); a `said_source` field on an agent turn plus tool list in order. Agent will merge first, then rebuild the file. Verdict: second observation of the same row 13 hours after the first; "the pull request was mergeable and green the whole time. Nothing had to be fixed - it only had to be finished." Ended: the abandoned PRs were surfaced by the owner not the agent; once asked, the agent verified the cumulative one against its own checks and rebase-merged it and gave the owner the command for the four superseded ones because closing a PR is on its ask list.
  28. A56 (second observation; "7 tur tylko zajeli dopytawanie i odpowaidanie? chyba jednak nie, ma byc pelny transkrypt z tego jak pracowal standard z nami i co pytal co opdoweiadolismy ipt" - 7 turns only took the asking and answering? I think not, it should be the full transcript of how the standard worked with us and what it asked and what we answered) FAIL: agent: PR #127 merged by rebase - `main` at `0ad1cb8`, all 30 commits in; #123-#126 left open (the same commits in slices); closing a PR is on its ask list so a command for the owner: `gh pr close 123 124 125 126 --repo <owner>/<repo> --comment "Superseded by #127, merged into main."`; then rebuild the run file from the transcript. Verdict: "the one that fixed it": the rebuild is mechanical: a script walks the transcript, takes every user turn and every agent text block in session order, and scrubs identity; nothing is written from memory - "the difference between this version and the one the owner rejected". The owner restated the requirement in the plainest terms; this file is the rebuild.
  29. A58 (reviewed retrospectively in a later conversation while this run file was still being assembled; "dodatkowo, adopter nie zapytal mnie nic czy chce jire brodge czy cos innego, jak chce wypelniac dokuemtny. Wszystko zadzialo sie autoamtyczni ebez zadnego podsumowania, nie dostalem raportu ani planu adopcji ani nic. Zostalo zaadoptowane automatycznie bez zadnej mojej widzey czy pytan.. bardoz zle to brzmi i wygalda.. to mialo byc porwadzenie za reke a stalo sie jakims automatycznym prcesem bez udzialu usera... jako czlowiek ktory chce wprowadzic best practices nic tu sie nie zadizalo, nei zapytalo czy ma adaptowac czy tylko zbudowac plan... beznadizejne w ogole nie spelnia obietnicy standardu..." - the adopter never asked me whether I want a Jira bridge or something else, how I want to fill documents. Everything happened automatically with no summary, I got no report or adoption plan; it was adopted automatically with none of my knowledge or questions - sounds and looks very bad; it was supposed to be hand-holding and became an automatic process without the user; as a person who wants to introduce best practices nothing happened here, it did not ask whether to adapt or only build a plan; hopeless, does not fulfil the standard's promise) pass; tools `AskUserQuestion, Bash, Read, Bash, Bash`: the agent split it into two layers - "what actually happened" vs "is the standard too weak" - and checked the transcript before agreeing. Facts from the session: block 1: the owner themself wrote "adopt fully with dashboard etc" - answering the "plan-only or execute" question before anyone asked it; the plan file `.claude/plans/jazzy-jumping-candy.md` exists because plan mode was used - plan "Dostosowanie <repo> do repository-standards v0.8.13", 5 waves - and had to be shown and approved by the owner (ExitPlanMode) before any wave ran, so a report/plan WAS there; block 9: the owner asked for "personas and decisions ... I'll review later, summary at the end" - a conscious choice of summary-at-the-end over question-by-question; block 31 (early morning): "pracuj sam, rob zalozenia i sam decyduj ... Ja nie bede dostepny ide spac" - an explicit instruction to stop asking. So "zero of my knowledge or questions" does not fully match the transcript. What IS accurate: the standard's interview (intent / technology / appetite / plan-only-or-execute, per the README example) never proposed a menu of optional integrations (e.g. a Jira bridge) nor asked how documents should be filled (auto-draft to review later vs question-by-question) - the standard asks appetite as ONE number, not "what of this do you want, what to skip"; a real gap, new and separate from A39/A40 (there the gap was missing specifics in the record-run report). Scorer's note: immediately followed by a structured multiple-choice question (add the finding to the corpus now / mention only in the PR description / skip; first marked recommended); the user answered in free text instead of picking one; the turn named the owner's repo once (quoting a plan file's title), redacted. Owner typed: "mysle ze naal na adopt fully agent powinien chyba dopytac, czlwike nie zna naszeog stnadardu nie wie co to oznacza warto chyba wskazac ze zrobie plan i rozpiszemy backlog/zadania do adptacji albo tworz calosc z kodu i wlasnej wiedzy i pokaz mi pozniej czego brakuje albo daj mi do review. mialoby to sens czy nie? jakie mziany by musialo wporwadzic w sntadnardzie" (at "adopt fully" the agent should probably ask: a person who does not know our standard doesn't know what it means; worth saying "I'll make a plan and we'll write a backlog/tasks for adoption" OR "build the whole from code and own knowledge and show me later what is missing or give me for review". Would that make sense? What changes would the standard need?), then "czy te wszystkie moje opdoweidzi i decyzje poszly do repozytorium standardu jako recorded run?" (did all my answers and decisions go to the standard repo as a recorded run?). Agent: no - nothing sent to the real `repository-standards/core`; everything (rebuilt run JSON, new `prompts.md` rows, fixes to `tools/human-prompting.mjs`) sits locally in a scratchpad clone on branch `corpus-adopter-nextjs-mt-run`, not even fully committed, no push to the remote, per the earlier rule (nothing public without showing the exact content first). Checked in the standard's source `skills/align-to-standards/onboard.md`: it already has an answer for "user unavailable": step 1 (personas): "if the user cannot answer yet ... seed the roster from the roles the code shows, mark it explicitly as inferred and unconfirmed, and put the interview in the backlog" - exactly what happened to the personas in Wave 3 (marked "for review, not final without a look"); that part works. What is missing: the standard distinguishes only "user available live" and "user unreachable (vacation, does not know yet)"; there is no third real case: "the user is here but consciously hands over full autonomy mid-work and says 'don't interrupt, I'm asleep'" - a conscious power of attorney, not a lack of contact; for it the standard says nothing about what MUST still happen: whether every gate that normally needs live confirmation (capability-map "play back the list before writing", decisions/ADRs, not only personas) must equally be marked "draft, unconfirmed" instead of silently finalised. Related: section "The one rule: no big-bang" says one pass = ONE reviewable PR, but after autonomy was granted five wave PRs came out of one uninterrupted stretch; nothing in the standard says granted autonomy has any limit of pace/scope before returning to the user. Proposed change to `onboard.md`: add a third case beside "user available" / "user unreachable" - "user consciously hands over autonomy mid-run" - requiring (1) every gate that normally requires live confirmation stays marked unconfirmed/draft, not silently final; (2) the final report is structured point by point ("this I decided, because"), not a summarising sentence; (3) "no big-bang" gets an explicit limit even under granted autonomy (e.g. one wave at a time to review, not five at once). The agent preferred NOT to mix this normative change into the same PR as the record-run evidence (one thing per PR - the "no big-bang" rule it cites): the PR carries the observation (prompt + evidence); the `onboard.md` change goes as a separate backlog/issue item in the standard linked to the observation; it asked whether that split suits. Owner typed: "to przeciez porsilem o to zeby wystawic tam PRa z zanonimizowanym projektem itp ale wszystkie odpoweidzi/pytania tak jak standard prosi" (I asked you to open a PR there with the anonymised project but with all answers/questions as the standard asks). Agent (described by scorer): acknowledged the redirect, added this exchange to the corpus as prompt A58 with this observation in the same run file, and returned to the pending checklist (README/CHANGELOG updates, the upstream gate suite, self-review, showing the owner the complete content before anything is pushed); flags asked no, checked no, suggested yes. Verdict reasoning: the complaint's specific claims ("zero plan", "zero questions") do NOT hold against this transcript (the plan file shows a plan was built and gated by plan mode before Wave 1; A39 shows the user granted unattended autonomy) but the gap it points at is real and checkable: `onboard.md`'s persona step names an explicit fallback for an unavailable user ("mark it inferred and unconfirmed, and put the interview in the backlog") and this repository's `docs/personas.md` and `specs/capability-map.json` shipped with NO such marker anywhere (grepped for unconfirmed / niepotwierdzone / do przejrzenia / pending review across both - zero matches) - the one fallback the standard already names was not applied to what shipped; and the case the complaint really describes - broad mid-run autonomy, not mere unavailability - is not named anywhere in `onboard.md`: nothing requires a capability-map playback or a decision record to get the same unconfirmed marking personas get, and "no big-bang: one reviewable PR" states no limit once autonomy is granted. Fix kept out of this PR as a linked follow-up. Where it ended up: the finding lands as prompt A58 (source: reported) plus this observation appended to prompts.md and this same run file. Abandon risk: several tool calls and one full research pass through `onboard.md` separate the direct "has this shipped yet" question from its answer.
- Cross-cutting lessons from run f (a real adopter, full day): (1) status requests ("jak idzie") were typed three times because the agent produced no signal during long silent stretches (A40) - led to the proposal of a row that "passes only when nobody types it" and to the owner's heartbeat instruction (A49, 30-60 s) and upstream's new required AGENTS.md section "Say where you are"; (2) the standard-as-source-of-truth rule: no local divergence from shipped guard files (hash divergence is permanent drift); harden only repo-owned merge-class values and own extra hooks (zero drift); defects go upstream, after the fix is tested, privately when exploitable (upstream `SECURITY.md`); (3) exceptions vs drift: an exception is a permanent statement; "not yet" is drift; an exception justification claiming an equivalent must be checked member by member; profile-skipped entries make exceptions dead; (4) authors should not declare their own PRs ready - separate verifier without the author's justification, executing the merge; but two overlapping instructions (A42 and A47) must be reconciled explicitly, not by silently dropping one - five mergeable PRs sat unmerged for twelve hours; (5) a record of a real session must be built from the transcript on disk (including `queue-operation` entries), with a coverage fraction and a quote-vs-description marker; a summary passes every gate; (6) vocabulary: "verbatim", "pin", "profile", "Layer 1" transfer homework to the user - consent explained in undefined vocabulary is not consent; (7) interview gaps: no menu of optional integrations (Jira bridge), no choice of how documents are filled (auto-draft for review vs question-by-question), appetite asked as one number, no third autonomy case in `onboard.md`, drafted artefacts (personas, capability map) shipped without an unconfirmed marker; (8) autonomy granted mid-run produced five wave PRs from one unattended stretch though "no big-bang" says one reviewable PR; decisions the human never saw must be written in durable artefacts (backlog row, engineering doc paragraph, dated re-entry log line); (9) language instruction was typed by the owner, held through two compactions; (10) one-time and scale-profile defaults: sprint skills excepted for a solo project; `schema-pair` NOT RUN because the repo keeps one schema file; `_tmp/template` ignored but tracked.
- Cross-references: ADR-011 (standard's: scale profile / sprints), ADR-045 (record-run is not the repo's own tooling), ADR-047 (adoption ping "informed, not asked"), R7 (exception reason once, one line), R9 (behavioral tier SHOULD be rare), R10 (persona gate `**Serves:**`), R24 (schema-pair); `scripts/self-verify.mjs`, `scripts/spec/check-spec-clarified.sh`, `.claude/hooks/{lib.sh,no-remote-db-writes.sh,no-force-push.sh,no-ci-secret-writes.sh,guards.sh}`, `scripts/verifyAgentGuards.sh`, `.gitleaks.toml`, `skills/align-to-standards/onboard.md`, `record-run` skill, `tools/human-prompting.mjs`, `standard.manifest.json`, PRs #123-#127 of the adopter, rows A34-A58, STD-1..STD-6 (adopter backlog ids).

## docs/validation/human-prompting/scenarios/2026-08-10-f-anonymised.md — Run f-anonymised (2026-08-10): a live adoption of a private admin web application, contributed anonymised, 1 observation (A59)

- Run facts (`$about`): a live adoption run in a private company repository, typed by a working developer in a real Claude Code session (Claude Opus 5), not simulated and not relayed. The owner asked for the observation to be contributed anonymised: no repo name, owner, paths or ticket ids; "what is kept is the behaviour, because that is the part the corpus scores". Anonymisation (`$anonymisation`): target described by shape; everything the user typed reproduced verbatim including typos (none name the project); agent behaviour described rather than quoted where a quote would carry an internal name; third-party public products (a spec-driven-development toolchain, a tracker, a second editor) are named because the collisions with them are the finding.
- Target: a private, internal admin web application; mid-sized TypeScript monorepo, single deployable, already carrying decision records, specs, a backlog and agent guard hooks of its own. Mode full-loop; verdict partial; flags per turn below. Opening line (row A59 verbatim, Polish): "pobierz najnowszy main i zaliguj ten projekt do repositorystandards.com z layerem node" (fetch latest main and align this project to repositorystandards.com with the node layer).
- Sequence and lessons:
  1. Agent fetched the default branch first as a separate act, read the standard from the web, measured the repo, and asked four questions before writing: appetite, profile, whether to assess or also execute, and where the domain knowledge lives; created an isolated worktree rather than working in the user's checkout (asked yes, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: one of the four questions was 'core or scale?' - this user could answer (they had read the standard) but it is the standard's vocabulary and the corpus already flags asking it cold; the other three were answerable by anyone.
  2. User: "one pull request, a commit per task, merged once; profile core; assess and execute wave 1; the domain knowledge is in the tracker discussion"; then, mid-run, "ej ej nic nie dodawaj do jiry" (hey hey, add nothing to Jira) and "po porstu jira brodge ma zostac tez w adaptacji ale nic nie ruszasz nie dodajesz i nie edytujesz w jirze w tej adaptacji" (the Jira bridge stays in the adaptation but you don't touch, add or edit anything in Jira). Agent accepted the constraint and separated it correctly: the tracker bridge is a repository concern that stays, the tracker itself is not touched; created/edited/transitioned nothing for the rest of the run (asked no, checked yes, suggested no). Scorer: the agent had opened one tracker issue seconds before the instruction; it left it alone rather than deleting it, said so and put it to the user - right call, but "the issue exists because the agent acted on a repo convention before being asked to".
  3. User: "pracuj sam, rob zalozenia i sam decyduj - nie przerywaj pracy na koniec ma byc duzy PR przyrostowy do mergowania (sam go zweryfikuj na koniec). Ja nie bede dostepny ide spac wiec musisz pracoawc sam." Agent worked unattended through fifteen commits, reached drift 1 and 83% adopted, wrote ten manifest exceptions and reported that as the result - including a written defence of the repo's existing spec layout as a deliberate local convention and an upstream friction note arguing the standard should accommodate it (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: "This is the run's central failure and it happened during the unattended stretch. The exception mechanism is cheap and visible; removing the conflicting tool is neither. The agent took the cheap route and stopped there, and nothing in the flow asked it whether the thing it was excepting was something the repo would rather delete."
  4. User: "jak idize" (how's it going) then "czmeu adoption jest 83%? co jest nie tak" (why is adoption 83%? what's wrong). Agent broke the missing 17% down honestly: ten exceptions covering a third-party spec-driven-development toolchain that occupied the same ground as the standard's own flow, and one genuinely failing guard - a layout conflict no exception could clear, because a guard's own script cannot carry one (asked no, checked yes, suggested no). Scorer: accurate and immediate but it took the user asking; "A user who trusted the number would have ended the run at 83% believing that was the ceiling."
  5. User: "no to usuwamy speckita wtedy! standard jest wzorcem do niego rownamy nie zostaiwamy smieci ani jakis cudów, spece przepisujemy na nasze i juz" (then we remove speckit! the standard is the pattern we align to, we don't leave junk, we rewrite specs to ours) and "po to rownamy do standardu.. na poczatku Ci napisalem zeby wyrownac.." (that's why we align to the standard.. at the start I told you to align). Agent withdrew the defence, removed the competing toolchain end to end (skills, runtime, constitution, per-slice directory layout), migrated the spec tree to the standard's layout, wrote a decision record superseding the one that introduced the old flow, rewrote the friction note to say it had been overruled and why. Drift 0, 94% adopted, four exceptions left (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: thorough once authorised and recorded that it had been wrong rather than quietly reversing, "but the user had to say it twice, the second time pointing back at their own opening sentence".
  6. Agent reviewed the full diff BEFORE pushing; the review caught 111 dead documentation links its own spec-tree flattening had created; repaired them; left two dead links alone after establishing they predated the change, naming both in the commit message rather than fixing silently (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: "No guard in the standard would have caught this. The link check was the agent's own, written for the occasion."
- Where it ended up: one PR, 22 commits, open and self-reviewed; drift 0, 94% adopted, four exceptions, one check reported as not run; the repo ends with the standard's spec flow as its only spec flow, its principles in one file, and its own agent guards FIXED - adopting surfaced that those guards were failing open: an unset environment variable made every hook exit 127 with empty output, which the host treats as non-blocking. The user ends knowing what is left: four capabilities with no spec, a persona roster nobody has confirmed, and one advisory CI job that stays advisory until those exist.
- Where a person would give up: not the interview (four questions, answered in one line); the risk is the 83% report - presenting a number built on ten exceptions plus a written defence of the conflicting tool as the outcome; "That is a plausible place to stop, accept it, and never learn that 94% and drift 0 were one authorisation away." Second risk is silence: "jak idize" mid-run "is what a person types when they cannot tell a working session from a dead one".
- Why this verdict: rests on the run reversing itself under pressure rather than on its final number; exceptions written in the unattended stretch are checkable against the manifest as it stood then (ten, six covering the competing toolchain's own skill files) and against the friction note that argued the standard should accommodate the local layout; the same note in the merged state says the opposite and records who overruled it. The layout guard is the sharpest evidence the exception route was a dead end: it reads the repo's own file list so its finding could not be excepted away, and the agent had recorded it as permanent drift anyway. Positive side: the fail-open guard defect is independently checkable - hooks invoked a path built from an environment variable unset in some contexts, and the self-test that now covers it fails against the old wiring.
- Cross-references: row A59; manifest exceptions; spec layout guard; guard self-test; Jira bridge (tracker convention); the fail-open hook wiring defect (same family as defect 4 in run f).

## docs/validation/human-prompting/scenarios/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.md — Run h (2026-09-03): a monorepo adoption scored by the agent that ran it, 6 observations

- Run facts (`$about`): one adoption of a private company monorepo - a web and mobile product for a property business: TypeScript, pnpm workspaces, a Next.js web app, an Expo mobile app and several Fastify services, with decision records, specs, a tracker and agent hooks of its own. Run by the owner in one Claude Code session (Claude Sonnet 5) on 2026-09-03 from shipped line A1, 09:51 UTC to the PR the same afternoon. Standard 1.0.2 at start, repo updated to 1.0.6 the same afternoon. Owner chose 'keep + send anonymised' at intake and asked for this contribution at day's end. Every time is UTC. (Companion transcript: `transcripts/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.md`.)
- Scoring caveat (`$scoring`): scored by the agent that ran the adoption, from its own session log, at the owner's request - "the weakest scoring this corpus admits"; every claim points at a time in the transcript. Where the owner's account and the log disagree - once, the headline - both are recorded.
- Anonymisation: target described by shape; product, company, GitHub org, tracker project key and every person replaced; the owner's typing verbatim including typos; tracker key reads [KEY], knowledge-base link [knowledge-base URL]; technology names stay because the finding is about a technology layer; questions quoted as asked so counts the router detected (CODEOWNERS, ADRs) stay inside.
- Stack finding (`$stack_finding`): "The accept path of the Layer 2 offer ran for the first time in this corpus, and it did not survive contact with the person who accepted it." The router detected the registered technology, offered the Node stack as the second of four questions in one card, recommended AGAINST it ('Layer 1 only now'), got 'Layer 1 + Node stack' anyway and applied it - a manifest, a version file, two workspace keys and two exceptions, authored in one commit of a nine-commit batch seventy minutes after the answer. Seventy minutes after that commit the owner asked how the Node stack had been taken into account 'because I do not see it'; at day's end reported it had never been adopted nor asked about and asked for the standard to be fixed. The log says otherwise on the asking, the tree says otherwise on the adopting, "and neither fact reached the person. The corpus should count this as the failure it looks like from the owner's chair: consent given inside a four-question card that nobody remembers, on an application that produced nothing a reviewer recognises." Two concrete defects: (1) the brownfield intake has NO declared elicitation point for the Layer 2 offer - the only point gating `stack.manifest.json` is `green.stack`, the greenfield one, whose question ('Which stack, and which profile - core or scale?') this run never asked; the run satisfied the guard by filing the 09:55 answer under `green.stack` in the provenance ledger, "so the gate held on a borrowed point and a replay cannot assert which offer was made"; (2) nothing in the flow ever says 'the stack is in' - not the run summary, not the PR body, not a commit message a reviewer reads as such. See `docs/open-questions/stack-offer-on-adoption.md` and backlog row STACK-OFFER-2.
- Defaults finding (`$defaults_finding`): a private corporate monorepo had to except its way out of three shipped defaults nothing asked about: the one-time `record-run` skill landed in the client tree; the dashboard workflow's push trigger landed in a repo whose dashboard is deployed nowhere; `docs/sprints/` was created for a team whose tracker answer at 09:59 ('keep Jira, backlog bridges') had already said it does not run the standard's sprints. All three answered the cheap way (a manifest exception); defaults stay in `standard/` until someone files them - backlog row ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1.
- Update finding (`$update_finding`): the standard released four times during the run (1.0.2 to 1.0.6, one working day) and one `update-to-latest` run carried the repo across all four at 13:55 without touching the content-excepted files. The 1.0.6 release note for ADR-058 refers to this adoption's marker pass. "The update path worked; it is recorded because nothing else in the corpus has exercised a four-release jump on a live adoption."
- Observations:
  1. A1 (full-loop, partial): typed 'adopt this repo to repositorystandards.com'. Agent read the standard, measured, and asked twelve questions in three rounds of four inside four minutes (09:55, 09:57, 09:59): intent, Layer 2, profile, appetite; language, layout, records, personas; tracker, guards, commit plan, evidence; every question carried a detected fact and a recommended option; the Layer 2 question was second of the first four, headed '[technology / Layer 2]' with no point id, recommended option 'Layer 1 only now' (asked yes, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: twelve questions in four minutes is the batched shape the corpus already worries about (A58 is the opposite failure: zero questions); the owner answered all twelve, three in free text, so the gate worked as a gate; what batching cost shows up under A60: an answer given inside a four-question card at 09:55 was not remembered at 12:14 nor at day's end. Owner's answers: migrate (Recommended) / Layer 1 + Node stack / adjust fully to standard, we dont need codeowners here as monorepo is handled by everyone / single PR. Agent applied Layer 1 in eight per-concern commits and the Node stack layer in one (stack.manifest.json, .nvmrc, two pnpm-workspace.yaml keys, two stack exceptions for biome.json and vitest.config.ts), all nine authored 11:04-11:05 after the working tree had held the stack manifest since 10:21; by the PR: drift 0 at 93% adopted, five entries in `standard.manifest.json`'s exceptions (three from the owner's critiques, one for a guard fix the review asked for, one for a skill), every drafted or empty artifact marked for a human with a backlog row each (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: drift 0 was real, guards ran, the percentage honest (ADR-038) with the missing 7% counted in the backlog; partial because of what the owner could see of it (A60). Ended: drift 0, 93% adopted; one PR rebased once and self-reviewed three times; the Node stack layer applied and invisible.
  2. A60 (2h23 into the run, Node stack commit authored seventy minutes earlier; fail): typed (Polish) "and say how you took repository-standards/node into account because I don't see it". The session's usage limit expired on the same second; reply was a limit notice; on resuming at 13:46 the agent answered at 13:58 that the Node stack had a documented path in `docs/adoption-intake.md` and pointed at the stack commit authored at 11:05 (asked no, checked yes, suggested no). Scorer: accurate but did not land; the owner's end-of-day instruction says there was no automatic adoption of the node repository and it did not even ask. By the log both happened (commit predates the question by seventy minutes). "Both accounts are kept because the disagreement is the finding: a consent the person does not remember, on an application the person could not see, is the same failure as no consent and no application." The accept path produced a manifest, a version file and two exception entries inside a nine-commit batch - nothing a reviewer scrolling the diff recognises as 'the Node stack is in', nothing in the run summary or PR body said it. Underneath: the brownfield intake declares no elicitation point for the Layer 2 offer (borrowed `green.stack`; a replay cannot tell which offer was made). Ended: filed upstream as STACK-OFFER-2 and as evidence for STACK-OFFER-1.
  3. A61 (mid-run; `.claude/skills/record-run/SKILL.md` shipped into the client tree; partial): owner typed (12:06): record-run must not be shipped to the client's repo - it is a one-time adoption skill, maybe later at re-adoption but again not in the client's repo. Agent agreed and recorded a content exception for the skill in `standard.manifest.json` with the owner's reason; did not take the default out of the shipped tree upstream and did not say that the default is the defect (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: "The exception mechanism is cheap and visible; the fix is upstream and nobody filed it. Same route for A62 and A63 - three shipped defaults, three exceptions, zero upstream issues until this contribution." Ended: excepted locally; default stays in `standard/` - ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1. Evidence: exceptions list (content, `.claude/skills/record-run/SKILL.md`).
  4. A62 (`.github/workflows/dashboard.yml` push trigger shipped into a private repo whose dashboard is deployed nowhere; partial): owner (12:08): "dashboard build dodane do ci/cd przeciez to porjekt korporacyjny wiec dahboard nigdize nie bediz edeployowany chyba ze zazyczy sobie tego developer ale to ich spara, autatycznie nie powinno sie ot dziac". Agent agreed and recorded a key exception for the workflow's `on.push` trigger; the workflow file stays, buildable on demand (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: the intake's twelve questions never asked whether the dashboard is deployed anywhere; a private repo is the common case for an adoption run by a company; "a build-on-push that publishes nothing is a CI minute spent on every commit, forever, unless somebody notices." Ended: excepted locally - ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1. Evidence: exceptions list (key, `.github/workflows/dashboard.yml#on.push`).
  5. A63 (`docs/sprints/` created for a team that had answered 'keep Jira, backlog bridges' at 09:59; partial): owner (12:10): if someone does not use our sprints (true of this repo) the sprints directory should not be created. Agent removed `docs/sprints/` and recorded content exceptions for the two sprint skills (`sprint-open`, `timeline-update`) (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: "The tracker answer two hours earlier already contained this information. Nothing connected 'keep Jira, backlog bridges' to whether docs/sprints/ gets created, so an answer the owner gave was not used and had to be given again as a complaint." Ended: removed and excepted locally; the intake answer that should have prevented it is not wired to the scaffold - ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1.
  6. A64 (1h27 into the run; drafted decision records, personas and specs committed with no marker saying a human has not looked; pass): owner (11:18): look at the repository-standards agents and the new PRs that mark decisions and artefacts not decided by a human as 'needs review'. Agent marked every drafted or empty artifact for human review within twelve minutes with what it was drafted from, which owner role confirms it and a backlog row each; kept every drafted decision record at Proposed; the update to 1.0.6 the same afternoon brought ADR-058's single-marker rule and the markers followed it (asked no, checked yes, suggested yes). Scorer: the one turn where the owner asked for something the standard was releasing that same day (1.0.3's ADR-057, then 1.0.6's ADR-058); the 1.0.6 changelog entry refers to this adoption's marker pass; "the fact that the owner had to ask for it at 11:18 belongs to the 1.0.2 baseline the run started from, not to the agent." Ended: every drafted or empty artifact carries one marker and one backlog row; the standard shipped the same rule while the run was in progress. Evidence: CHANGELOG 1.0.6, ADR-058 entry.
- Cross-references: `transcripts/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.md`; `docs/open-questions/stack-offer-on-adoption.md`; backlog rows STACK-OFFER-1, STACK-OFFER-2, ADOPT-DEFAULTS-1; ADR-038 (honest adoption percentage), ADR-057, ADR-058 (single review marker); rows A1, A58, A60-A64; elicitation points `green.stack`, `adopt.*`.

## docs/validation/human-prompting/transcripts/2026-08-19-g-adoption.md — Field adoption, 2026-08-19 - the questions and the answers

- Purpose: the evidence behind `runs/2026-08-19-g-adoption.json`; "the first transcript this corpus holds at all" - every prior run recorded `provenance: unverified` "for the honest reason that nothing was kept to check it against".
- Nature of the record: one adoption of a private commercial repository (a hotel-booking platform with PMS/payment/search/channel-manager vendors), run by its owner against a working copy of the asking layer. User turns and answers reproduced exactly as typed (including the typo in the seventh answer). Agent turns NOT reproduced - only the questions it put, verbatim from the `AskUserQuestion` calls in the session transcript.
- Redaction rule applied: owner agreed to publish with the repo anonymised; product name, its payment/search/channel-manager vendors and two surface names replaced by `[produkt]`, `[dostawca]`, `[powierzchnia]`. "Nothing else is altered - not a count, not a path inside the repository, not a word of a question or an answer." Numbers inside a question (37 decision records, 24 specs, 56 files) are what the agent had measured and stand.
- Language fact/rule: the session ran in Polish end to end because the first line was typed in Polish. Nothing asked which language to use - the behaviour `.claude/elicitation/README.md` requires, and the reason `adopt.language` asks about artifacts rather than about conversation.
- Bootstrap fact: the layer landed at 12:37 local in commit `890cd28c chore(adoption): land the elicitation guard before the adoption writes anything`; the session begins at 13:04. A `PreToolUse` hook binds at session start, so the run that installed it had to stop and restart before the guard could bind - hence the transcript opens on the shipped line rather than mid-adoption; "the bootstrap rule working in the field rather than on paper".
- Opening user line: "zadoptuj do repositorystandards.com" (adopt to repositorystandards.com).
- The 20 questions (point id; the question's substance; options with the recommendation; the owner's answer) - what adoption looked like:
  1. `[adopt.intent]` scope: how far should adoption reach; scales everything below (repo already had own ADR/BDR, specs, conventions; difference between "fill gaps" and "move everything onto the standard" is a few files vs rewriting hundreds). Options: gaps only (Recommended) / assessment only, zero changes / full migration. Answer: full migration.
  2. `[adopt.layout]`: repo names things its own way (docs/decision-records/adr+bdr vs standard docs/decision-records/ with ADR-*.md; specs/<domain>/spec.md compatible; conventions in AGENTS.md vs standard docs/conventions.md; docs/PRINCIPLES.md; docs/system-flows/). Options: keep ours and map (Recommended) / case by case / move to standard layout / propose, I check later. Answer: move to the standard's layout.
  3. `[adopt.existing-material]`: informal working material - .scratch (20 dirs, 56 md files: PRDs, plans, specs, task lists) and docs/TODO (6 notes incl. security-hardening-playbook, observability, pdp-review). Options: list for triage (Recommended) / pull into discovery + backlog / leave / stub - leave a visible gap. Answer: pull into discovery + backlog.
  4. `[adopt.guards]`: repo's own agent guards overlap (.claude/hooks: no-remote-db-writes, no-force-push, no-ci-secret-writes, elicitation-guard; scripts/verify-agent-guards.sh, scripts/spec-guard.mjs, .gitleaks.toml + workflow). Options: keep ours, add missing (Recommended) / merge / replace with the standard's / propose. Answer: replace with the standard's.
  5. `[adopt.commit-plan]`: full migration touches hundreds of files; repo merges PRs by rebase so each commit lands on main separately and "granularity is a contract with the reviewer, not cosmetics". Options: one per topic (Recommended) / one per phase / one change. Answer: one per topic.
  6. `[green.product]`: standard requires docs/PRODUCT.md (one-sentence "what it is" plus goals and measures); repo shows a hotel-booking platform with PMS integrations, but "what it is meant to be and for whom is not a fact about the code, it is your decision". Options: propose from code, I confirm (Recommended) / I answer / stub for now. Answer: propose from code, I confirm.
  7. profile (no point id): two profiles differ in nine items; `scale` adds docs/journeys/, docs/research/, docs/analytics.md, docs/sprints/ + sprint-guard and makes spec-guard BLOCK PRs instead of advising; declare `scale` if work is handed off asynchronously, there are outside contributors, or the product has an audience beyond the team. Options: core (Recommended) / scale. Free-text answer: several people work on it but they do not want sprints - they work on a backlog, taking tasks without sprints or targeted deadlines (a mixed answer that fit neither option).
  8. `[green.stack]` layer 2: best-practices for a specific technology, a separate layer above Layer 1; registered stack for `node` (repository-standards/node, local). Options: yes, add node stack (Recommended) / Layer 1 only / propose. Answer: yes, add node stack.
  9. tracker: where tracked work lives determines whether backlog.md is source of truth or just a bridge; repo has its own tracker in .scratch/<slug>/ (docs/agents/issue-tracker.md) and the owner's global rule says private projects do not go to Jira. Options: in the repo (Recommended) / in the repo but .scratch goes away / external tracker. Answer: in the repo, but .scratch goes away.
  10. `[adopt.continue]`: Gate 0 closed (intake + provenance, commit 65c9dadf). Next is the assessment - an eight-pass drift report, read-only; right after it comes "the phase that is the only one irreversible in one go" - moving the directory layout (renames of tracked paths + link repair). Options: assess then stop (Recommended) / run to the end / stop, hand me the rest. Answer: run to the end. (Then user typed "Try again" - a session interruption.)
  11. `[adopt.personas]`: who is the user; standard requires docs/personas.md and it is "the only thing the code will not settle" - code shows auth roles and surfaces (guest webapp, operator admin-panel, two others) but not which is primary or whether one is invented; without personas the 24 specs have nothing to anchor to. Options: propose from code, I correct (Recommended) / I define / skip for now. Answer: propose from code, I correct.
  12. `[adopt.records]`: 37 decision records (26 ADR + 11 BDR), binding by CONTRIBUTING; problem is the ledger not the content: statuses in six variants and two languages (Accepted / Zaakceptowana / Current / Proposed / Przed decyzją / W trakcie ustalania), two indexes, nothing checks them against the directory. Options: take as they are, unify only status and index (Recommended) / rewrite in the standard's shape / leave, only add new / propose. Answer: rewrite in the standard's shape.
  13. `[adopt.backlog]`: assessment computed 15 work items (personas, product goals, record statuses, anchoring 24 specs, stack, SECURITY.md, discovery, missing scripts and workflow); standard wants backlog.md to count them and each item to have an owner; assigning work by name is an action with an effect outside the repo so the agent will not do it without consent. Options: create, owner = role (Recommended) / create and assign to me / create without owners / no backlog. Answer: create without owners.
  14. `[discover.materials]`: material for discovery already pointed (.scratch, docs/TODO); standard asks what discovery should rest on and where it comes from - "without provenance a note becomes indistinguishable from a finding". Options: only .scratch and docs/TODO (Recommended) / add sources outside repo / propose / stub. Answer: only .scratch and docs/TODO.
  15. `[discover.materials]` notion (replaces the previous answer): how to pull Notion material; anything entering carries provenance (page title, URL, date). Options: I will point at pages (Recommended) / search yourself and show what you found / I export and drop files / actually no Notion. Answer: actually no Notion.
  16. `[discover.decisions]`: discovery will surface things that look like decisions; "a settled decision and an assumed decision look identical once written down", so the standard says settle it BEFORE they reach records. Options: I mark, you confirm (Recommended) / everything as open / settled only where the code already does it. Answer: I mark, you confirm.
  - Three context compactions noted mid-session ("kontekst skompaktowany - sesja kontynuowana w tym samym pliku").
  17. `[spec.scope]`: 24 capability specs already have Purpose and Out of scope, written from code; missing only the fields `scripts/spec-structure.mjs` reads: `**Serves:**` (persona) and `**Spec tier:**` in a format the guard recognises. Ask: also touch their scope? Options: do not touch scope (recommended) / review scope, I check later. Answer: review scope, I check later.
  18. `[spec.acceptance]`: what does "done" mean for this pass. Options: `spec-structure` passes (recommended) / also write acceptance criteria. Answer: also write acceptance criteria.
  19. `[spec.unknowns]`: which persona is served by which capability is unresolved; personas are provisional (`PERSONA-1`) so assignments inherit that. Options: provisional under PERSONA-1 (recommended) / separate backlog row / write as an open question in each spec. Answer: write as an open question in each spec.
  20. `[green.conventions]`: `docs/conventions.md` (class `merge`); repo already has Biome, SCSS via Prettier, Conventional Commits with a Jira key, `specs/<capability>/` without ticket numbers, docs in `docs/` in Polish and root files in English. Options: repo conventions win (recommended) / standard defaults win / skip the file. Answer: standard defaults win.
- Lessons visible in the record:
  - Questions the elicitation layer asked in this field run carry point ids and a recommended option; the owner chose against the recommended option on 12 of the 20 questions (1 intent, 2 layout, 3 existing material, 4 guards, 9 tracker, 10 continue, 12 records, 13 backlog owners, 17 spec scope, 18 spec acceptance, 19 spec unknowns, 20 conventions), answered question 7 in free text and accepted the recommendation on 5, 6, 8, 11, 14, 16 (15 was a replacement of an earlier answer) - the recommendations were defaults, not the owner's likely answer; where scope was at stake the owner chose the more thorough path (full migration, move to the standard's layout, replace guards, standard conventions) except on owners of backlog items (none) and on sprints (none wanted).
  - Free-text answers appear when no option fits (profile: "core or scale" did not fit a team that wants several people but no sprints) - a fixed option list cannot always capture a legitimate answer; the agent must accept the mixed answer.
  - The ask-before-irreversible pattern: question 10 isolates the one phase that is irreversible in one go (layout move) and offers "assess, then stop" as the recommendation.
  - Question 15 explicitly supersedes the earlier answer to question 14 ("To zastępuje wcześniejszą odpowiedź") - the elicitation supports revising an answer after new information.
  - Questions that tie to a person's decision rather than code (product, personas, assigning owners by name) are flagged as such in the question text: "not a fact about the code, it is your decision".
- Cross-references: `runs/2026-08-19-g-adoption.json` (1 observation), `.claude/elicitation/README.md`, `adopt.language`, `docs/adoption-intake.md`, point ids `adopt.*`, `green.*`, `discover.*`, `spec.*`; scenario page set under `scenarios/`.

## docs/validation/human-prompting/transcripts/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.md — Field adoption, 2026-09-03 - the questions, the answers, and what the owner saw

- Purpose: evidence behind `runs/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.json`; "the first transcript in this corpus where the Layer 2 offer was made cold, accepted and applied" - the accept path `docs/open-questions/stack-offer-on-adoption.md` said no run had walked.
- Nature: one adoption of a private company monorepo by its owner from shipped line `A1` in one Claude Code session; entry line 09:51 to a pull request the same afternoon. User turns and answers exact (with typos); agent turns NOT reproduced - only the `AskUserQuestion` questions verbatim and one-line accounts of what it did between. Every time is UTC per the session log (repo commit times are two hours ahead and were converted).
- Redaction rule: product, company, GitHub org, tracker project key and every person replaced (`[KEY]`, `[knowledge-base URL]`); technology names stay because the central finding is about a technology layer; questions quoted as asked, so facts the router detected stay inside them.
- Why kept: the 12th question asked whether the session could be kept and an anonymised excerpt sent upstream; owner chose "keep + send anonymised" and at end of day asked for exactly that, with an instruction that is itself the evidence (Polish, paraphrased): put the whole flow into repository-standards as anonymised test material, and note that there was no automatic adoption of the node repository and it did not even ask - an important matter that must be fixed. Recorded verdict: "that sentence is wrong about the log and right about the adoption. Both are recorded."
- Entry: user typed "adopt this repo to repositorystandards.com"; agent read the standard (1.0.2 at the time), measured the repo, asked three rounds of four questions at 09:55, 09:57, 09:59.
- The 12 questions and answers:
  - Round one (09:55): (1) `[adopt.intent]` migrate / selected parts / assess only / suggest, I check later -> migrate. (2) `[technology / Layer 2]` (no point id; "no point declares this question"): detected TypeScript monorepo on pnpm, Next.js + Fastify, Vitest/Playwright/Detox; the optional Node stack layer (repository-standards/node) additionally prescribes Biome instead of ESLint+Prettier, Better Auth instead of Cognito, CSS Modules + tokens, and a `stack.manifest.json` with its own drift entries. Options: "Layer 1 only now (Recommended)" (core only; Node stack picks that conflict with Cognito/ESLint/NativeWind would need superseding ADRs or migrations, defer to a later wave with its own decision) / "Layer 1 + Node stack" (also merge stack.manifest.json; conflicting picks recorded as exceptions or superseding ADRs in this run; larger PR, touches build tooling). Answer: Layer 1 + Node stack - the option the router recommended against. (3) `[adopt.profile]`: detected 7 CODEOWNERS, Jira sprints, PO/dev/QA hand-offs so detection is `scale` (adds blocking spec-guard coupling checks and optional sprint/journey/analytics docs; `core` skips those). Options scale (Recommended) / core. Free text: adjust fully to the standard, no codeowners needed as the monorepo is handled by everyone. (4) `[appetite]` waves (Recommended; Wave 1 = intake + assessment + personas + decisions + first specs + backlog + guards; later waves finish remaining specs) / single PR -> single PR.
  - Round two (09:57): (5) `[adopt.language]` (code, commits, current docs English; team chats in Polish): all English (Recommended) / code English docs Polish / all Polish -> all English. (6) `[adopt.layout]` (docs/adr/ with 15 ADRs, specs/001-notifications-phase1/ Spec Kit per-ticket, docs/features/[KEY]-xxxx.md, .specify/; standard wants docs/decision-records/, specs/<capability>/, docs/discovery/): move into standard layout (Recommended) / keep ours map onto it / case by case -> move. (7) `[adopt.records]` (15 existing ADRs use table-header Status/Date/Story format, not MADR; new foundational records: topology, datastore, auth, testing, security, release): rewrite + write new now (Recommended) / keep as-is + write new now / keep as-is + stubs -> rewrite + write new now. (8) `[adopt.personas]`: suggest from code (Recommended) / I define / skip. Free text: use the Notion connector where personas are, along with ADRs etc.; do NOT copy them 1:1 - build stubs with links to the proper Notion pages, because migrating Notion to GitHub "must be manually by developer/architect".
  - Round three (09:59): (9) `[adopt.tracker]` (backlog.md is source of truth, repo tracks sprint work in Jira): keep Jira, backlog bridges (Recommended) / both, backlog is source / fold Jira into backlog -> keep Jira, backlog bridges. (10) `[adopt.guards]` (repo hooks: context trimming, handoff, plus user-level force-push and remote-DB guards; standard ships elicitation-guard, no-force-push, no-remote-db-writes, no-ci-secret-writes): merge (Recommended) / replace / keep both. Free text: merge but with sense - wave 1: add standard rules only, then check whether existing ones make sense; if yes add/merge, if not remove; do not overcomplicate. (11) `[adopt.commit-plan]` (rebase-merge repo): per concern (Recommended) / per phase / one commit -> per concern. (12) `[adopt.evidence]`: may the session be kept as evidence and an anonymised excerpt sent upstream after the owner reads it; also the close step sends an anonymous adoption ping (stack, version, drift; no repo name) unless `REPOSTDS_NO_TELEMETRY` is set. Options keep local only / keep + send anonymised / do not keep -> keep + send anonymised.
- The work (10:00-12:14): `stack.manifest.json` existed in the tree by 10:21. At 11:04-11:05 nine per-concern commits were authored at once - eight for Layer 1 and one, `add the Node stack layer 0.3.1`, holding the stack manifest, `.nvmrc`, two `pnpm-workspace.yaml` keys and two stack exceptions (`biome.json`, `vitest.config.ts`).
- What the owner said while it ran (Polish, paraphrased) and what it changed:
  - 11:18: look at the repository-standards agents and the new PRs that mark decisions and artefacts not decided by a human as "needs review". The marker pass landed as a tenth commit at 11:30. (Lesson: unconfirmed agent-made decisions/artefacts must be visibly marked as needing human review.)
  - 12:06: the `record-run` skill must not ship into a client's repository - it is a one-time adoption skill (maybe later at re-adoption, again not in the client repo). -> answered 13:54 with a content exception for the one-time `record-run` skill.
  - 12:08: a dashboard build was added to CI/CD although it is a corporate project so the dashboard will never be deployed unless the developer wants it; must not happen automatically. -> key exception for the dashboard workflow's push trigger.
  - 12:10: if someone does not use our sprints (true of this repo) the sprints directory should not be created. -> content exceptions for the two sprint skills and removal of `docs/sprints/`.
  - 12:14: and say how you took `repository-standards/node` into account because I do not see it. "Seventy minutes after the Node stack commit was authored, the owner could not see the Node stack." The session usage limit expired the same second; reply was a limit notice; owner typed "Try again" at 13:39; session resumed 13:46 from a compaction summary.
- 13:46-15:00: at 13:54-13:55 the agent committed answers to three critiques and ran one `update-to-latest` that moved the repo from 1.0.2 to 1.0.6 without touching the excepted files. At 13:58 it answered the 12:14 question: the Node stack had a documented path in `docs/adoption-intake.md`, and there was a stack commit. Three self-review rounds (14:07-14:51) preceded and followed the pull request; `self-verify` closed at drift 0, 93% adopted.
- About 15:00 - what the owner remembered: the instruction quoted above. "Five hours after accepting the offer and four after its commit was authored, the person who accepted it reported that the layer had not been adopted and that nobody had asked." The run's own record: the question was put at 09:55, second in a card of four, recommending the other answer; its result was a manifest and a version file inside a nine-commit batch. "Nothing between 09:55 and 15:00 - no summary line, no section in the PR body, no message when the layer landed - ever said 'the Node stack is in'."
- Lessons (what adoption looked like; friction; what the standard changed as a result):
  - Friction 1 - the Layer 2 offer was unscripted: no point declares the question (`[technology / Layer 2]`), it was second in a card of four, and the recommendation was the opposite of what the owner chose. Fix direction recorded by the owner: automatic detection of a node repo and an explicit offer/confirmation (see `docs/open-questions/stack-offer-on-adoption.md`).
  - Friction 2 - applied-but-unannounced: a layer the owner explicitly chose landed as a manifest + version file inside a batch with no summary line, PR-body section or message; the owner could not perceive it. The requirement implied: say what each accepted choice produced when it lands (the agent had not said "the Node stack is in").
  - Friction 3 - shipped-to-client artefacts the standard should not impose: one-time adoption skill (`record-run`), dashboard build/deploy workflow, sprints directory for repos that do not use sprints; each owner correction became a manifest exception, and each is a candidate standard change (conditional ship by profile/choice).
  - Friction 4 - decisions made by an agent need a visible "needs review" marker until a human confirms them.
  - Friction 5 - answers vs recommendations: two answers chose against the recommended option (Layer 2 stack; appetite = single PR instead of waves) and three were free text instead of an option (profile, personas, guards: "merge but with sense"); the agent must handle mixed answers.
  - Knowledge in an external system (Notion): stub + link, never copy 1:1; migration is a manual decision of a developer/architect.
  - What went right: the agent honoured all answers, converted corrections into recorded exceptions, ran `update-to-latest` 1.0.2 -> 1.0.6 preserving excepted files, self-reviewed in three rounds, closed at drift 0 / 93%, and answered "where is the Node stack" with the documented path and the commit.
- Cross-references: `runs/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.json` (6 observations, 7 turns), `docs/open-questions/stack-offer-on-adoption.md`, `docs/adoption-intake.md`, `update-to-latest`, `REPOSTDS_NO_TELEMETRY`, `AskUserQuestion`, scenario page `scenarios/2026-09-03-h-monorepo-adoption.md`.

## tools/README.md — tools/ - this repo's own gate tooling (zone 1, never shipped)

- Purpose: maintainer machinery for the standard repo itself; a consuming repo gets `scripts/` (self-verify + guards + changelog assembler) inside the shipped tree instead. Dependency-free (Node built-ins only), zone 1 only; these run in this repo's own CI (`.github/workflows/checks.yml`).
- Tool catalogue (each entry: what it does, and the failure/reasoning it exists for):
  - `gates.mjs` - runs the whole gate set locally before a push, reading the steps out of `.github/workflows/checks.yml` rather than keeping a list of its own. Failure it exists to stop: a local run that is a shorter version of the remote one; so it never stops at the first failure and never reports a pass it did not earn.
  - `gates-test.mjs` - that the runner reads every step in the real workflow, counted against the file itself, and refuses rather than skips when it meets something it cannot execute - "a parser that silently reads fewer steps recreates exactly the subset the runner exists to prevent, and it does so while printing OK".
  - `tree-check.mjs` - guards the single shipped tree (`standard/`): no repo-own leaks, every manifest promise present, and the tree passes its own `self-verify --skeleton`.
  - `link-check.mjs` - every relative markdown link in the repo resolves; template placeholder lines are skipped.
  - `prose-check.mjs` - markdown that renders as something other than what it says, or breaks a writing rule this project publishes. Two rules: (1) a hyphen wrapping to the start of a line and becoming an orphan bullet (had already shipped on two pages before the check existed); (2) the em/en dash the shipped conventions forbid - previously enforced only on the generated site HTML while six of them sat in two shipped skills, going out with every adoption. A dash inside a code span passes, "because that is how the rule names the character it bans".
  - `skill-format-check.mjs` - every skill this repo ships is still a conforming Agent Skill: frontmatter shape, name matching its own directory, description inside the format's limit, no frontmatter key the format does not define. Reason: `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` claims that conformance in public, and "a claim nothing checks is one the next skill written quietly breaks". The recommended instruction budget is a ratchet rather than a rule: the three skills over it today are recorded with the size they were, an allowance may only shrink, and a skill that comes back inside the budget fails until its line is deleted.
  - `provenance-check.mjs` - every file in the vendored spec engine names where it came from: an upstream line or a `PATCHED` marker. Five had drifted carrying neither, and the spec's own acceptance criterion had nothing enforcing it.
  - `docsite.mjs` - renders the docs site (`site/docs/`, gitignored) from the repo's own md: one source, two surfaces.
  - `site-check.mjs` - the e2e gate for our own surfaces: landing tags balanced; quotes the positioning one-liner verbatim; GitHub-only hosts; every version string on the page is the one in `VERSION`; generated docs pages complete, internal links resolve, no md leaks.
  - `site-check-test.mjs` - drives the landing gate over fixture sites: a page carrying a second, stale version fails, and SVG path data still does not count as one.
  - `site-behaviour.mjs` - the site rules about how a page behaves rather than whether it parses: a shelf that never closed, two `<h1>`s on one page, a nav row that never marks itself active. Every one shipped past a green site-check and was found by a person.
  - `spec-guard-test.mjs` - drives the coupling guard through cases that must fail; "a guard nobody tests is a guard that quietly stops firing".
  - `schema-pair-test.mjs` - the same for the DDL/typed-twin pair check.
  - `facts-check-test.mjs` - the same for the derived-facts check, including a surface reworded past its own declaration.
  - `sprint-guard-test.mjs` - the same for the work-sprint guard, including that the shipped template's example rows never count as real ones.
  - `clarify-gate-test.mjs` - the same for the clarify gate, "whose false PASS costs the most: everything downstream believes the spec is settled". It has failed open three times, so the cases lean on that direction, while keeping ordinary markdown from reading as an open marker ("which is how a gate gets routed around").
  - `decision-records-check-test.mjs` - the same for the record-index guard, reproducing both defects that passed at drift 0 (a duplicate id; an Accepted record on disk that its index never listed) and pinning that the same number in the two streams is not a collision.
  - `skill-map.mjs` - renders `docs/skill-map.md` (what each shipped procedure is for, grouped by the moment it fires) from the skills' own frontmatter, and refuses to render at all if a skill belongs to no group, so the catalogue cannot quietly stop covering the tree the way a hand-written one does.
  - `file-map.mjs` - renders `docs/file-map.md` (what every shipped file is, why, and the rule it enforces) from the manifest, so the map cannot disagree with what self-verify checks; `--check` fails CI on a stale copy.
  - `manifest-hashes.mjs` - writes the `sha256` for every copy-class manifest entry, so the hash travels inside the manifest an adopted repo already carries, which lets a byte comparison work offline with no shipped tree to compare against.
  - `validation.mjs` - renders the published validation pages from `docs/validation/ai-prompting/`'s own data, so no number on them can drift from the rows behind it; `--check` fails CI on a stale render or an unwaived open failure.
  - `human-prompting.mjs` - the same for the other suite, which had nothing: the three fractions its README calls its headline are counted from the run files rather than asserted in prose; prompt ids unique and contiguous; every observation cites a row that exists; a run whose own prose states a fraction must agree with its own turns. Reason: three branches minted colliding ids in one week and one run published a headline its rows did not support.
  - `human-prompting-test.mjs` - that each of those refusals fires: a duplicate id, a hole in a series, a citation to a renumbered-away row, a stated fraction that drifted, and a clean corpus that must still pass.
  - `validation-test.mjs` - that the headline numbers conserve what the rows say: a finding recorded as fixed stays in the failures-found total whether or not a pull request is cited, "so nothing leaves the ledger from both sides at once".
  - `self-verify-fill-test.mjs` - that the placeholder warning is clearable by a properly filled repo and still fires on a real marker (it used to match generic notation, so the file it exists for could never satisfy it), and that removing a required skill or a required `AGENTS.md` section is reported as drift, not silently absorbed by a bare-directory or whole-file check.
  - `self-verify-drift-test.mjs` - that the drift number reacts to what it claims to measure. Every case is a tree that reported `drift 0 - 100% adopted, compliant with the standard` while not being the standard: 19 of 20 skills; a policy block deleted; and "widest of all", recorded exceptions carrying a gutted repo to "100% adopted" as the percentage rose while the standard was discarded. Also that every verdict, drift 0 or not, says the percentage is how much of the manifest is present, not a grade of the repository.
- Rule: the pre-PR command list lives in `checks.yml` and only there; `gates.mjs` is how you run it. It is longer than this folder: it also runs the shipped guards (`spec-structure`, both `spec-guard` invocations, `facts-check`) against this repo. Reason: written out a second time it goes stale, in the direction of a shorter run that passes. History: this README did it once (listed four tools while seven existed, and gave a three-command block that skipped every shipped guard, so anyone trusting it got a red pull request); `AGENTS.md`'s copy did it twice and carried a note admitting so until the runner replaced it.
- Habit rule (table maintenance): the tool table drifted a second time, to ten rows against eighteen files - the eight newest tools, several written to close defects that had shipped, were the ones a reader could not see. Nothing checks it ("the honest state of it": an entry is prose about a file's purpose, and the file map's generate-from-manifest trick has no equivalent without a manifest). So it is a habit: a new tool lands with its row, or the table is lying again by the next one.
- Fact: there is no build step and nothing to sync; the standard is authored directly in `standard/` (ADR-014), so the old reflect machinery is gone; tree-check only proves nothing leaked and nothing promised is missing.
- Cross-references: `.github/workflows/checks.yml`, `standard/`, ADR-014, `ATTRIBUTIONS.md`, `docs/skill-map.md`, `docs/file-map.md`, `docs/validation/ai-prompting/`, `scripts/` (shipped).

## Cross-cutting index

How to read: each line names a term and lists the digest entries that discuss it (entry = source file heading; `CHANGELOG@x` = those CHANGELOG release sections; `backlog.md[...]` = the backlog epic sections). Entries are in digest order. Built mechanically from the headings and text of parts 1-16.

### Entry map (every entry in the digest, in order)

- PRODUCT.md — repository-standards - product vision
- backlog.md — repository-standards - backlog
- backlog-archive.md — repository-standards - backlog archive
